Decision records (EPM-DEC-001)

Every decision behind the ontology and its build: context, decision, alternatives, the decision owner's recorded words, evidence, status and date.

Built from README.md and the 109 records EPM-DEC-001-*.md in this folder.

This page is generated from the Markdown beside it, which is the master copy. A change goes into the Markdown first; then python3 scripts/build_docs_html.py rebuilds this page.

  • 0109 Databricks and the ontology: KPI and OPM identifiers, metric views by fact grain, Genie agents under a supervisor, the agents read epm_triples, lineage in its own places, a technical graph in Neo4j, and three years of demo data

One file per decision: EPM-DEC-001-NNNN-<slug>.md, with context, decision, alternatives, the decision owner's recorded words, evidence, status and date. A record says Decided only when the decision owner has recorded the decision. A PR merge alone does not decide it. A decision recorded as "delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028" counts as the decision owner's recorded decision, within that record's scope. See docs/agents/domain.md for the format.

Records 0001 to 0024 come from the grilling session on the handover plan (2026-10-05). Later records come from follow-up decisions in Claude Code sessions.

EPM-DEC-001-0001: PPC is a planned consumer, not an active one#

From EPM-DEC-001-0001-ppc-consumer-status.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided
Date2026-10-05
QuestionsQ1
SourceGrilling session on the handover plan

Context#

PR #198 (2026-10-02) registered PPC as a downstream proving-ground consumer (D-18, Candidate) and drafted EPM-ARCH-REL-001, an EPM consumer release contract (Draft). The handover plan still said the ontology has no consumer. The core 1.0.0 release gate (playbook evidence rule 10) needs a fresh no-consumer attestation.

Decision#

Treat PPC as planned but not yet active. Build the ontology as if there is no consumer. Use EPM-ARCH-REL-001 as the target shape for the Step 10 release package, and avoid choices in core 1.0.0 that would block it (for example, unversioned or unstable IRIs). The no-consumer attestation stays valid while D-18 and REL-001 are Candidate or Draft.

Alternatives considered#

(a) PPC is a real consumer now, and core 1.0.0 must meet REL-001's manifest and checksum shape. (c) Ignore PPC until D-18 is decided.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Round 1: "Agree with the rest."

Session: Grilling session on the handover plan, 2026-10-05 (Claude Code with the decision owner). The decision owner answered numbered questions Q1 to Q34 in five rounds and confirmed the full decision list with: "Confirmed, go ahead and open the two PRs." They also stated: "If i dont comment on a question, it means I am aligned." A question they did not comment on is therefore recorded as agreed with the recommendation.

Evidence#

EPM-DEC-001-0005: What the ontology holds: concepts and public reference facts, no records of what happened#

From EPM-DEC-001-0005-ontology-data-scope.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided
Date2026-10-05
QuestionsQ6, Q11, Q19, Q26
SourceGrilling session on the handover plan

Context#

The anchor-company research produced many figures (capacities, margins, counts). The build needed a rule for which of them, if any, belong in the ontology.

Decision#

The ontology holds concepts, definitions, relationships, and publicly available reference facts about downstream companies and assets (for example MPC, its refineries, MPLX), each with a cited source. Stable numeric attributes of an entity, such as refinery capacity, an ownership share or a terminal count, are reference facts and carry an as-of date and a filing citation. The ontology holds no records of events or transactions ("Customer A bought 5 barrels") and no observed values of a measure over time ("the crack spread is $5"). Measure definitions are in scope; their values are not.

Alternatives considered#

No figures at all (the first reading of Q6 and Q11); public figures of every kind, including measure values.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Q11: "We will not have the actual data. The ontology is only for concept structuring, definition, and relationship to enable semantic knowledge management system. We have Pricing domain KPI category or type such as Crack Spread. I will share them later." Q19: "I do want this kind of data. The data that I dont want is to say something like Crack Spread is $5 or something like Cusomer A bout 5 barrels of oil. I do want data that are oublickly available about the downstream oil and gas. So having the MPC, refrienery is fine where appropriate. [...] Excatly record of what happen is what we dont want in the ontology." Q26: Not commented on; agreed per the decision owner's rule "If i dont comment on a question, it means I am aligned."

Session: Grilling session on the handover plan, 2026-10-05 (Claude Code with the decision owner). The decision owner answered numbered questions Q1 to Q34 in five rounds and confirmed the full decision list with: "Confirmed, go ahead and open the two PRs." They also stated: "If i dont comment on a question, it means I am aligned." A question they did not comment on is therefore recorded as agreed with the recommendation.

Evidence#

All 44 competency questions (business_architecture/ontology/competency-questions.md) ask about concepts or structure; none needs an observed value.

EPM-DEC-001-0015: Step 5: classes plus public reference instances in a separate module; MPLX#

From EPM-DEC-001-0015-step5-scope-reference-instances.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided
Date2026-10-05

SourceGrilling session on the handover plan

Context#

Step 5 builds ORG and RACI. The question was whether MPC itself (segments, refineries, MPLX) appears as instances.

Decision#

Step 5 models the classes and relations (role, organization, site, the RACI assignment pattern) and adds MPC reference instances where appropriate, under EPM-DEC-001-0005. Reference instances live in a separate module (ref-mpc); the core modules stay company-neutral. MPLX is an Organization. MPC controls and consolidates MPLX through a control relation separate from org:subOrganizationOf. The ownership share, as-of date and source sit on a qualified-relation node (EPM-DEC-001-0020). The Midstream segment is a separate concept that MPLX's operations roll up into. Customer roles wait (EPM-DEC-001-0004). 2026-10-21: the Customer work resumed (EPM-DEC-001-0067).

Alternatives considered#

Classes only, with MPC as evidence and not as instances (the Round 3 recommendation, which the decision owner changed); MPC instances inside the core modules.

EPM-DEC-001-0017: Step 7: overlay order and KPI module seed#

From EPM-DEC-001-0017-step7-overlay-order-kpi-seed.md

Date2026-10-05

SourceGrilling session on the handover plan

Context#

Each definition cites the MPC or peer filing that uses the measure; the filing is evidence for the definition, not a source of values.

Alternatives considered#

EPM-DEC-001-0020: Adopt the ontology-to-LPG guideline: Step 12, design Rules 1, 2, 3 and 5, no reifiers#

From EPM-DEC-001-0020-lpg-projection-guideline.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided
Date2026-10-05
QuestionsQ24, Q29, Q30, Q33
SourceGrilling session on the handover plan

Context#

The decision owner asked to project the finished ontology to a Neo4j labelled property graph using their guideline (Ontology_to_LPG_Conversion_Playbook v2, enterprise-people-graph commit cd63e12). An external check on 2026-10-05 found: RDF 1.2 is a W3C Candidate Recommendation Snapshot (7 April 2026), not a Recommendation; rdflib has no RDF 1.2 support (issue #3524, all stages open), and pySHACL depends on rdflib; RDF4J documents support; Jena's tracking issue #2805 is open; n10s is maintained (commit 29 May 2026, Neo4j 2025.06.2) and does not read RDF 1.2.

Decision#

(1) Add Step 12, LPG projection, after Step 11. It follows the guideline's pipeline (check, reason, load, verify) once the ontology is built. (2) Apply the guideline's design Rules 1, 2, 3 and 5 from core 1.0.0 on: one kind and a concrete range per property; SHACL cardinality on every property; stable IRIs, no blank nodes, fixed prefixes; explicit LPG names where the local name is not good enough. (3) Do not adopt Rule 4 (RDF 1.2 reifiers). When a relationship needs its own properties, model it as a qualified-relation node with its own IRI, the pattern of org:Membership and PROV-O qualified relations. This needs no RDF 1.2 tooling and removes the guideline's flatten step. (4) Record the playbook policy: LPG projection follows the guideline with this deviation, and Turtle stays the master copy.

Alternatives considered#

Apply the guideline only at projection time; adopt all five rules including RDF 1.2 reifiers; adopt Rule 4 at Step 5.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Q24: "can we adopt the guideline to project ontology to label property graph at [link removed under EPM-DEC-001-0049: the guideline_to_map_ontology_to_LPG folder of the enterprise-people-graph repository] Can we update our playbook with this." Q29: "I am aligned . But can you check this ontology to LPG projection guideline by doing a quick extrrnal search to make sure the guideline hold up and that it does not make the ontology unnecessarily complex." Q33: "We still have a way of representing this behavious without using reifers." Q30: Not commented on; agreed per the decision owner's rule "If i dont comment on a question, it means I am aligned."

Session: Grilling session on the handover plan, 2026-10-05 (Claude Code with the decision owner). The decision owner answered numbered questions Q1 to Q34 in five rounds and confirmed the full decision list with: "Confirmed, go ahead and open the two PRs." They also stated: "If i dont comment on a question, it means I am aligned." A question they did not comment on is therefore recorded as agreed with the recommendation.

Correction (2026-10-06)#

The context above says Jena's RDF 1.2 support was unconfirmed because its tracking issue (#2805) is open. That was wrong. Apache Jena has full RDF 1.2 syntax input and output, and SPARQL 1.2, from 6.1.0 (May 2026); 5.4.0 to 6.0.x was an experimental preview, and 6.2.0 is current (https://github.com/apache/jena/blob/main/CHANGES.txt). Eclipse RDF4J has RDF 1.2 from 6.0.0 (July 2026). The reviewer of enterprise-people-graph PR #1 found the error. The decision stands: this build's Python toolchain (rdflib, pySHACL) has no RDF 1.2 support, which is why this build does not adopt Rule 4 (RDF 1.2 reifiers) and uses qualified-relation nodes instead.

Note (2026-10-06): guideline v2.1#

Guideline v2.1 (enterprise-people-graph PR #1, commit e7b09e8) made qualified-relation nodes its Rule 4 option 4a, the default, and kept RDF 1.2 reifiers as option 4b. In v2.1's terms, this decision applies Rules 1, 2, 3 and 5 and Rule 4 option 4a. The decision itself is unchanged.

Evidence#

https://www.w3.org/TR/rdf12-concepts/ ; https://github.com/RDFLib/rdflib/issues/3524 ; https://rdf4j.org/documentation/programming/rdf12/ ; https://github.com/apache/jena/issues/2805 ; https://github.com/neo4j-labs/neosemantics ; business_architecture/ontology/lpg-projection/.

EPM-DEC-001-0023: Public ontologies: map to them, do not import them#

From EPM-DEC-001-0023-external-ontologies-map-not-import.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided
Date2026-10-05
QuestionsQ34
SourceGrilling session on the handover plan

Context#

An external search on 2026-10-05 found no public business-architecture or process ontology for downstream oil and gas. Public oil and gas ontologies cover upstream (OSDU, O3PO) or plant equipment (ISO 15926, IDO ISO/FDIS 23726-3). APQC's Downstream Petroleum PCF 7.2.2 is a framework and is already the Step 0 reference. Five sources each cover a piece of the scope: GLEIF Level 2 (accounting consolidation), PIDX (downstream product and terminal codes), the Open Energy Ontology (fuels; CC0 or MIT, BFO-based), IOF Supply Chain (MIT, BFO-based) and FIBO (ownership and control; MIT, large).

Decision#

Keep EPM's own terms and link them to these sources with SKOS mapping properties (skos:exactMatch, skos:closeMatch) at the step where each fits: GLEIF at Step 5 (MPC and MPLX), PIDX and OEO at Step 7 and the Customer product-line facet, IOF Supply Chain at Step 7 if P01 brings in fuels logistics. Do not import them. Check PIDX's access terms before the Customer work. 2026-10-19: checked; the standards are royalty free under PIDX's end user licence, for use without modification (EPM-DEC-001-0064). 2026-10-20: P01 brought fuels logistics in, and IOF Supply Chain is parked until a concept layer for logistics assets or movements, or a consumer that needs IOF terms (EPM-DEC-001-0064). 2026-10-23: for PIDX, which publishes no IRIs, party 1.1.0 records family letters as plain identifiers instead of a SKOS mapping; OEO is mapped with SKOS (EPM-DEC-001-0092).

Alternatives considered#

Import one or more of them; ignore them.

The decision owner's recorded words#

"can you do a external search to see if there are publickly available business architecture/process ontology for Downstream oil and gas that we can adapt in our ontology here." Q34: Not commented on; agreed per the decision owner's rule "If i dont comment on a question, it means I am aligned." Later: "make sure to update the playbook with how we use this publickly aavailable ontology."

EPM-DEC-001-0026: A scoped sequence relation in core, before 1.0.0#

From EPM-DEC-001-0026-scoped-sequence-relation.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided
Date2026-10-12
QuestionsQ1 to Q18 of the #226 design round
SourceDesign round in a Claude Code session, 2026-10-12; approval on #226

Context#

In #205, the decision owner answered 20 held precedes/follows rows as source author. For 17 of them their answers describe a sequence that holds per trade, delivery, claim, forecast version or other unit, sometimes only under a condition, and never as completion of a whole process. core:precedes stays locked as a process-level finish-to-start ("The source completes before the target begins"), so it cannot carry them. On PR #225 the decision owner decided: "Define a scoped sequence relation for these answers before the core 1.0.0 release, tracked in its own issue under #207." Issue #226 tracks that work. The three other rows (REL-01227, REL-00184, REL-00149) have no sequence.

Decision#

The 18 decisions, as posted on #226 (summary, #226 comment 6123931847, saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/226-design-summary.md):

Shape

  1. A scoped sequence is a qualified-relation node with its own IRI (EPM-DEC-001-0020), pointing at the earlier and the later process and holding the scope, any condition and the source sentence. core:precedes is unchanged.
  2. The endpoints are the existing process nodes. The narrower part of each process is described in text; no new sub-step identities are created.
  3. The scope comes from a controlled SKOS list of scope units, each with a definition.
  4. Every node runs from the earlier process to the later process, whatever the direction of its source row, and cites its row ID.
  5. A scoped sequence and core:precedes are fully separate; neither implies the other.
  6. A scoped sequence may link a process to one of its own ancestors only when a source-author answer says so explicitly (REL-00112). SHACL warns on any other ancestor link, and only the approved node may declare one.
  7. P-Plan p:isPrecededBy is not mapped now; Step 6 looks at it again (EPM-DEC-001-0016).

Definition and properties

  1. Term: Scoped sequence: "A scoped sequence states that, for each instance of a named scope unit, a stated part of an earlier process happens before a stated part of a later process. Neither process needs to complete." Parts: core:ScopedSequence, with core:earlierProcess, core:laterProcess and core:sequenceScope, one value each.
  2. Ten scope units: transaction, physical movement, delivery period, posting, demurrage claim, forecast version, replenishment requirement, plan-selection decision, quote version, scheduling horizon. A new value is added only when no existing value fits.
  3. A condition is optional text, at most one per node (core:sequenceCondition).
  4. The node's skos:definition is the approved scope-note sentence, word for word, and the node cites its row ID and the answer comment.
  5. REL-00008 and REL-01223 describe one forecast handoff at two levels. Each gets a node; the child's node points at the parent's through core:refinesSequence, and handoff counts leave out a refining node.
  6. Each node carries core:evidenceBasis: source-confirmed or reference-model assumption. SHACL requires it.

Verdicts, counting and release

  1. All 17 rows get a fresh APPROVE, emitted as scoped sequences, not as core:precedes. REL-01227, REL-00184 and REL-00149 stay not emitted with their recorded reasons.
  2. The ledger gets its own scoped-sequence category (617 emitting / 669 held / 12 deferred / 1 external governance / 2 structured flow / 17 scoped sequence = 1318), and epm_facts.py counts reports 17 nodes and 16 handoffs.
  3. The class, its properties and both code lists go in the core module. core 1.0.0 releases only when the relation and its SHACL shapes pass. This amends EPM-DEC-001-0013 and EPM-DEC-001-0025.
  4. In later batches (#209, #210 and after), an answer that gives a sequence only per a scope unit goes straight to a scoped-sequence verdict under these rules. A new design round is needed only for a new scope unit or an answer that does not fit the definition.
  5. The decision owner's approval is saved under step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/ and quoted below.

Alternatives considered#

  • A plain link between the two processes with the scope only in scope-note text (Q1): no place for a scope or condition that a query or SHACL can check.
  • New sub-step identities such as "Initial trade capture" (Q2): The decision owner's batch 2 answer says "Do not create a new target identity by inference", and it would reopen the finished Step 3 taxonomy.
  • Free-text scope (Q3) and a controlled list of conditions (Q10).
  • The name "handoff" (Q8): not every row hands something over.
  • Mapping to P-Plan p:isPrecededBy now (Q7): it orders steps inside one plan, with no scope or condition.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner accepted the recommendations in three rounds of the session; those answers are not saved, so this record does not quote them. They then approved the posted summary on #226 (#226 comment 6123946413, saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/226-design.md): "Approved: Q1–Q18 as recommended in the comment above"

Amendments to earlier records#

  • EPM-DEC-001-0013: core 1.0.0 also requires the scoped sequence relation and its SHACL shapes to pass.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0025: the 17 scoped rows of #205 get their fresh verdict as scoped sequences. They count as Phase 1 rows with a fresh verdict, not as held rows.

Evidence#

business_architecture/ontology/step4/scoped-sequence/ (scoped-sequences.csv, vocabulary.ttl, shapes.ttl, scoped-sequences.ttl, build.py); review-evidence/precedes-follows-batch1-review.csv and -batch2-review.csv; review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/205-batch1.md, 205-batch2.md, 225-option1.md, 226-design.md; evidence-gate.py section 2k.

EPM-DEC-001-0027: Two mirror raw verbs, provides-input-to and governs#

From EPM-DEC-001-0027-mirror-raw-verbs.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided
Date2026-10-12
QuestionsOne question, after the Phase 1 register approval
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-12; recorded on #207 comment 6133567292

Context#

The decision owner approved the Phase 1 answer register on 2026-10-12 (#207 comment 6133390674). In 101 of its rows the approved fact runs opposite to the workbook row's direction. 90 are stored as T core:dependsOnOutputOf S: a raw "S enables T" that means T consumes an output of S. 11 are stored as T core:governedBy S. Q3 of that approval stores each fact in the direction shown.

Corrections belong in the source workbook, not the promotion script (rule 11; source-workbook-backlog.md). The direction table had no raw verb that states either relationship from S's side. Step 4 numbers REL rows by segment position in the workbook, so moving a segment to T's row would renumber every later REL ID and break every list keyed by one. The direction table says later changes need a decision-log entry.

Decision#

Two raw verbs join the canonical direction table. Each is the mirror of an existing verb and uses the stored form of that verb:

Raw verb (S, T)Stored asMirror of
provides-input-toT core:dependsOnOutputOf Suses-input
governsT core:governedBy Sgoverned-by

A correction rewrites the row's segment in place with the new verb, so its REL ID stays the same. The raw verb stays in the fact's provenance, as for the other inverse verbs (rule 5). The rules of the stored predicate apply unchanged: the dependsOnOutputOf and governedBy no-two-cycle gates, and the governed-by emission guard, read with T as the governed process and S as the owner.

Alternatives considered#

  • Move each segment to T's row as uses-input or governed-by. Rejected: it renumbers REL IDs after every moved segment.
  • Reinterpret the verb in the promotion script. Rejected: rule 11 forbids changing source meaning at emission.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner answered the question in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-12 (#207 comment 6133567292, saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/mirror-verbs.md): "yes, add both verbs and record the decision."

Consequences#

  • mapping_v2.py stored_fact and evidence-gate.py _stored map both verbs.
  • Structural nearness never counts toward promoting governs, as for governed-by (context_pass.py _real).
  • Enables grouping treats "S provides-input-to T" like "T uses-input S" when it looks for an existing output dependency (mapping_v2.py enables_group).
  • The context pass's two-way test is unchanged: the mirror verbs add no new inverse pair there.
  • The direction table in verb-predicate-mapping-v2.md lists both.
  • core:providesInputTo and core:governs remain query-only inverse predicates; no fact is stored with them.

EPM-DEC-001-0028: Claude decides the open questions of emitting the ontology on the decision owner's behalf, from evidence#

From EPM-DEC-001-0028-delegated-decisions.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided

QuestionsOne instruction, 2026-10-12; amended 2026-10-13 to cover Steps 5 to 12, 2026-10-18 on the reviewers, 2026-10-20 on merging past the approval agent and on the PR verdict while Cursor is out of usage, 2026-10-22 on the review rounds and the PR reviewer, and 2026-10-25 on the order of independent reviewers, later that day on reviewers inside Rex, and then on the work queue after the ontology closed, except major releases, Market Data accountability and Q17 (Claude approves the queue's other minor and patch releases; an item H measure needs an MPC or peer definition)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-12; recorded on #207 comment 6137222677

Context#

Until now every open question went to the decision owner, and nothing was marked Decided without their recorded words (AGENTS.md; this folder's README). The ontology still has Phase 1 batches, the release gate and Steps 5 to 12 ahead, each with questions. The decision owner asked Claude to keep going until the ontology is finished and to decide for them.

Decision#

Claude decides, on the decision owner's behalf, the open questions of emitting the ontology and updating its playbook, and, since the 2026-10-13 amendment, the open questions of Steps 5 to 12 of the build, and, since the amendment of 2026-10-25 (evening), those of the work queue after the ontology closed, except the points that amendment keeps for the decision owner: major releases, Market Data accountability, and ownership and stewardship (Q17). Under it Claude approves the queue's minor and patch releases beyond the ones the decision owner approved, and admits an item H measure only with an MPC or peer definition. Each delegated decision:

  • rests on the evidence order in AGENTS.md: Marathon Petroleum (MPC) filings and public materials first, then U.S. refining peers' SEC filings, then downstream-specific industry sources. Generic frameworks are support only. The repository's own process authority and the decision owner's material rank above all of them (tier 1, EPM-DEC-001-0059);
  • uses light external search only to resolve an ambiguity the repository's evidence leaves open, and cites what it finds;
  • is recorded where the question lives (a decision record, a review CSV row, or a backlog entry), with its evidence and the words "delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028";
  • counts as Decided, because this record is the decision owner's recorded decision to delegate. The decision owner can reverse any delegated decision; the reversal is recorded the same way.

Unchanged: every change still goes through a pull request, the checks in AGENTS.md and an independent review.

Scope and review: the work runs until the ontology is emitted and the ontology playbook is updated, and then, under the 2026-10-13 amendment, through Steps 5 to 12, with a log of what was done. 2026-10-25 (evening): and then through the work queue after the ontology closed, with the exceptions that amendment names. Each PR gets at least one independent review before it opens and at most two: a full review of the branch, then, if it found something, a focused review of the fix. A fix after the second round gets scripts/check.sh and CI, and the PR body says it had no third review. 2026-10-22: replaced: a fix after the second round also gets a Pi quick check, and the PR body says which rounds and quick checks ran; see the amendment of 2026-10-22 below. 2026-10-25: the independent reviewer is now the first available in an order; see the amendment of 2026-10-25. After the PR opens, one PR reviewer reviews it the way the Cursor review bot did: one agent, no subagents, one MERGE or HOLD comment, and a new review after each push until merge. The decision owner named the reviewers in the same session: the Cursor agent on Grok 4.6 for the independent review, and Pi on glm-5.3-flash:cloud for the PR review (Kimi K3 first, until its usage ran out). 2026-10-13: changed by the decision owner; see the amendment of 2026-10-18 below.

Merging: The decision owner asked "how do i remove the merge bock. I am going to bed and wont be around to merge". Claude answered with the steps to add the Claude Code permission rule Bash(gh pr merge:*); the session then shows that rule added, and the decision owner replied "done". Claude merges a PR with a merge commit only when all of these hold for its current head: scripts/check.sh passes, every independent-review finding is fixed or answered, CI is green, and the PR reviewer's latest comment is MERGE. Claude records the merge on the PR. A PR that changes approval policy or routing, as this one does, still waits for the decision owner (APPROVAL_POLICY.md). 2026-10-20, see the amendment below on merging when the approval agent asks for a human: the decision owner need not be the one who merges such a pull request once the PR reviewer's MERGE is on its head and checks passed; Step 3c definition batches still wait for them.

Amendment, 2026-10-25 (evening): the work queue after the ontology closed#

The ontology closed on 2026-10-23 (EPM-DEC-001-0096), so the original scope and the 2026-10-13 extension no longer cover new work. On 2026-10-25 the decision owner gave Claude a queue: the qSKOS dispositions for the released modules, item G (the systems' definitions from public sources), item F's releases (EPM-DEC-001-0097), item E (the playbooks, and moving the ontology playbook into the ontology skill) and item H (the synthetic-data demo in Databricks Free Edition). While Claude worked on it, the decision owner gave the permission quoted below. Claude then asked whether it also covers approving releases, and the decision owner chose "I approve each release (Recommended)". They approved item G's three releases and item F's release plan the same evening, then asked Claude to take the rest of the night's approvals so that nothing waits for them, and chose Claude's proposal: "Yes, delegate as described (Recommended)".

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The delegation covers the open questions of that queue. Each decision uses the decision owner's order: this repository's sources first, then MPC's public information, then U.S. refining peers, then generally available sources. This is the source tiers' order (EPM-DEC-001-0059): the repository's own sources, the process authority and the decision owner's material, are tier 1; MPC, peers and industry sources follow, in AGENTS.md's order; generic frameworks stay support only. Each is recorded as "delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028", as the Decision section says.
  2. Releases. The decision owner approved item G's releases (system 1.1.0, party 1.2.0, integration 2.1.0) and item F's release plan (data-product 1.2.0, market-data 1.1.0, kpi 1.2.0, system 1.2.0, integration 2.2.0). Claude approves any other minor or patch release the queue needs, under this delegation, and records it as delegated. Claude approves no major release: one that would need it is deferred and listed in the night's report. For the queue this replaces the module policy's rule that a minor or patch release is the module owner's (step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md, "Who approves a release").
  3. Item H's candidate measures enter kpi only where MPC's public information or a U.S. refining peer's filing defines the measure, cited; any other stays a candidate in the night's report. The internal systems' descriptions (item G's question 10) are approved by Claude under this delegation, each drawn from the repository's own evidence.
  4. Two points stay with the decision owner. Market Data accountability: the decision owner said "we wont make that decision now" (answers-2026-10-25-item-f.md; business-closeout.md, point 2). Ownership and stewardship (Q17) stay parked by the decision owner's decision (EPM-DEC-001-0086).
  5. Claude leaves an HTML report of what was done, each decision made for the decision owner, and what is still open.

Amendment, 2026-10-25 (later): reviewers inside Rex#

On 2026-10-24 local time, two reviews ran through Rex by hand, and Claude recommended adding Rex as a second host for the review scripts, chosen automatically. The decision owner answered "And I like your recommendations." personal-agent-skills #54 added it (merge commit bb9a399, 2026-10-25): inside a Rex pane and outside Herdr, herdr-review.sh hands off to rex-review.sh, which starts the same reviewers with the same flags and prompt (skills/engineering/way-of-working/scripts/herdr-review.sh, the --host choice; test_rex_review.py, test_both_hosts_launch_and_prompt_alike). Claude then asked, as its numbered question 1, whether, inside Rex and once that host is merged, Kimi and the Cursor agent CLI count as available for this repository's reviews. The decision owner answered question 1 with "i agree". The decision owner also set the pane layout ("for Rex I want the first review pane to be a horizonta split pane in the current window") and allowed answering a reviewer's folder-trust question ("it is okay to always trust the folder and continue").

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Inside Rex, Kimi and the Cursor agent CLI are available as the way-of-working skill's "Who reviews" section defines it, through herdr-review.sh, which hands off to rex-review.sh there. The order of the amendment of 2026-10-25 is unchanged: Kimi, then Pi, then Codex. The Cursor agent CLI's stand-in verdict (amendment of 2026-10-22) runs the same way.
  2. Outside both Herdr and Rex, Kimi and the Cursor agent CLI stay not available, and Codex runs through the Codex plugin, as before.

Amendment, 2026-10-25: the order of independent reviewers#

On 2026-10-24 and 25, Pi's Ollama weekly usage limit stopped it from reviewing personal-agent-skills #50 to #52, and Codex stood in. The decision owner then set an order for the independent review: "can we use kimi K3.1 Pro at medium effort for independent review, if it is not avaliable we use Pi as before and if Pi is also not available we use codex 6.0 luna and if that is not available we use cursor agent as the fall back." Kimi Code 2.3.1 offers low, high and max effort for K3.1 Pro, with high as the default (support_efforts and default_effort for kimi-code/k3.1-pro in its model catalog, ~/.kimi-code/config.toml, read 2026-10-24), and the decision owner chose "set it at high". The way-of-working skill took the order in personal-agent-skills #52. The decision owner then asked for this repository to follow it: "also amend EPM's AGENTS.md to use the same order."

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The independent review is the first available of three reviewers, in this order: Kimi on K3.1 Pro at high effort (herdr-review.sh --kind kimi, model kimi-code/k3.1-pro); then Pi (pi-review.sh --role independent); then Codex on gpt-6-luna (herdr-review.sh, or outside Herdr the Codex plugin with --model gpt-6-luna). A reviewer is not available as the way-of-working skill's "Who reviews" section defines it. This replaces Pi as the only independent reviewer under the amendment of 2026-10-22. 2026-10-25 (later): inside Rex, herdr-review.sh runs these reviewers too, and the Codex plugin is for outside both Herdr and Rex; see the amendment of 2026-10-25 (later).
  2. The Cursor agent CLI is not in this repository's order. Here the PR verdict always comes from Cursor: the Cursor bot, or the Cursor agent CLI on Auto as its stand-in (amendment of 2026-10-22). The merged commit's two reviews must come from different tools, so the decision owner's last fallback cannot apply here. When Kimi, Pi and Codex are all not available, nothing is pushed, and Claude tells the decision owner. This paragraph is Claude's reading of the rule, not the decision owner's words.
  3. Rounds stay as the amendment of 2026-10-22 sets them. Each round, and each quick check of a later fix, uses the first available reviewer in the order.

Amendment, 2026-10-22: review rounds, and the PR reviewer back to Cursor#

In session 7 (2026-10-21) Claude ran more than two independent (Pi) rounds on several pull requests (for example #337, by its PR body). It had read the decision owner's "keep it uncapped, do the full sweep first" (answers-2026-10-21-review-uncapped.md) as covering the independent review; that answer covered PR verdict rounds only, as Claude recorded on #337. The session 7 morning summary asked whether to keep the two-round limit. Claude proposed at most two full Pi rounds per pull request, then a Pi quick check of each later fix commit, with PR verdict rounds uncapped. The decision owner answered "I agree".

During the same session the Cursor bot was out of usage, and Codex (through herdr-review.sh) gave the PR verdict. When the bot came back on 2026-10-21, the decision owner kept Codex for the rest of that goal: "for the rest of this work. Let us continue to use CODEX for this goal. for consistentcy. We can siwtch back to cursor cli next goal". At the start of the next goal, on 2026-10-22, Claude asked whether Pi gives the independent review and the Cursor bot the PR verdict, with the Cursor agent CLI on Auto as the bot's stand-in. The decision owner chose "Yes, go (Recommended)".

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Independent review rounds. Round 1 is a full Pi review of the branch. Round 2, only if round 1 found something, is a full Pi review of the fix. Each fix commit after round 2 gets a Pi quick check of that commit only, plus scripts/check.sh and CI. The PR body says which rounds and quick checks ran. 2026-10-25: the independent reviewer is now the first available in an order; see the amendment of 2026-10-25.
  2. PR verdict rounds are uncapped. The PR reviewer reviews every push until it posts MERGE on the head.
  3. The PR reviewer is the Cursor bot again, from 2026-10-22. If the bot's check has not started 8 minutes after a push, or it has posted no verdict 9 minutes after the push, the Cursor agent CLI on Auto gives the verdict (herdr-review.sh --kind cursor --model auto), posted word for word as the amendment of 2026-10-20 (later) says, and stays the PR reviewer for the rest of that pull request, as the way-of-working skill's switch rule says. Pi gives the independent review in both cases, so the merged commit's two reviews come from different tools. 2026-10-25: the independent reviewer is now the first available in an order; see the amendment of 2026-10-25. This replaces the fallback of the amendment of 2026-10-18 (the Cursor agent CLI as independent reviewer and Pi as PR reviewer).

Amendment, 2026-10-20 (later): the PR verdict while Cursor is out of usage#

The decision owner said the Cursor review still matters: "But cursor still ask to merge. correct? that is important that cursor finds no issue". Later that day Cursor's on-demand usage ran out: the review bot, the approval agent and Bugbot failed to start on PR #313's new head, and the Cursor agent CLI on Grok 4.7 refused for the same reason. The decision owner first chose "Use cursor agent cli with GROK 4.7 as the reviwer." and then, when Grok was out too, "Cursor CLI on Auto". Claude Code's safety check refused a merge on a verdict posted from the decision owner's account; they chose "Allow the merge script (Recommended)" and "Allow me to post reviews (Recommended)" and added the permission rules Bash(~/.claude/skills/way-of-working/scripts/merge-on-verdict.sh:*) and Bash(gh pr review:*).

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. While the Cursor bot is out of usage, the Cursor agent CLI on Auto gives the PR verdict (herdr-review.sh --kind cursor --model auto). Its report starts with MERGE or HOLD and the commit it reviewed. This replaces Pi as the PR reviewer named by the amendment of 2026-10-18 for as long as the bot is out. Pi gives the independent review, as when the bot runs, so the merged commit's two reviews still come from different tools. 2026-10-25: the independent reviewer is now the first available in an order; see the amendment of 2026-10-25.
  2. Claude posts the report on the pull request word for word, as a review comment with a line naming the reviewer, and merges on its MERGE plus passing checks with merge-on-verdict.sh --bot <the decision owner's account> --admin-if-only-review. A HOLD is fixed and the new head reviewed again.
  3. When the bot runs again, it gives the PR verdict as before.

Amendment, 2026-10-20: merging when the approval agent asks for a human#

PR #308 had the Cursor review bot's MERGE on its head and passing CI, but the Cursor approval agent withheld its approving review as "medium risk", and ruleset 25440735 requires one approving review, so the PR waited until the decision owner merged it. The decision owner then wrote: "You should not be waiting for me to merge. What made you wait for me this time?" Asked whether Claude should merge such pull requests itself with an admin merge, they chose "Yes, admin-merge on MERGE + CI (Recommended)".

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. When Claude admin-merges. Only when the PR reviewer's verdict on the current head is MERGE, the checks status has passed on that head, and the only unmet requirement is the approving review. Claude merges with gh pr merge --admin --match-head-commit <head>, through way-of-working's merge-on-verdict.sh --admin-if-only-review (personal-agent-skills #41), which first checks that every check on the head passed, that the head is not behind main where the rules require an up-to-date branch, and that no current review thread is unresolved. This ruleset does not require an up-to-date branch, so a branch behind main merges without admin privileges too.
  2. Never past a HOLD or a failed check. An admin merge is not a way round the PR reviewer, CI or the independent review; those rules are unchanged.
  3. Each one is visible. Claude says on the PR, and in the session log, that it merged with admin privileges and why.
  4. APPROVAL_POLICY.md still tells the approval agent when to ask for a human; the decision owner's answer is that, for those pull requests, they do not need to be the one who merges.
  5. Step 3c definition batches are the exception. Their flow says the decision owner merges them (step3c-review-handoff.md, "Never merge — the decision owner merges."); this amendment does not change that.

Amendment, 2026-10-18: who gives which review#

On 2026-10-13, during the Steps 5 to 12 night run, the decision owner changed the reviewers named above: "the night run now default to Cursor bot for PR review and only use PI when cursor is not available." From then on the way-of-working skill's "Who reviews" table applied: while the Cursor bot is available, Pi gives the independent review and the bot gives the PR verdict; when it is not, the Cursor agent CLI gives the independent review and Pi the verdict. 2026-10-20: while Cursor is out of usage, see the later amendment of that date above. 2026-10-22: replaced; when the bot is not available, the Cursor agent CLI on Auto gives the verdict and Pi still gives the independent review (amendment of 2026-10-22 above). 2026-10-25: the independent reviewer is now the first available in an order; see the amendment of 2026-10-25. This record and AGENTS.md still named the Cursor agent for the independent review, and on 2026-10-18 Claude followed that text. The decision owner asked "why are you using indepdent cursor review. are you not supposed to use PI?". AGENTS.md now points to the table. The decision owner allowed Claude to merge that routing change itself: "Yes, merge it (Recommended)". The review rounds and merge conditions above are unchanged.

Amendment, 2026-10-13: Steps 5 to 12#

After core 1.0.0 was released (#249) and the playbook updated (#250), the original scope was complete. Claude listed the remaining steps: 5 (organizations and roles), 6 (interfaces and provenance), 7 (integration), 9 (full SHACL), 10 (publication), 11 (regression tests) and 12 (property-graph projection). Step 8 was done on 2026-09-17. Claude said the delegation covered none of them and asked whether to extend it. The decision owner extended it to Steps 5 to 12.

The amendment changes only the scope. Everything else in this record applies unchanged to Steps 5 to 12: the evidence order, the recording rule, the one or two review rounds per PR (2026-10-22: plus quick checks of later fixes; see the amendment of 2026-10-22), the reviewers (changed 2026-10-13; see the amendment of 2026-10-18) and the merge conditions. A major release still needs the decision owner ("Major (including any deprecation): The decision owner. No exceptions", step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md, "Who approves a release"). APPROVAL_POLICY.md still routes some PRs to a human.

Not part of the decision: in the message the decision owner answered, Claude proposed bringing three kinds of business facts to them in batches for approval, the way the Phase 1 register worked: the RACI assignments (Step 5), the KPI and Market Data and Commercial Risk definitions (Step 7), and the hosting and license choices (Step 10). The decision owner's words extend the scope only; they neither adopt nor reject that proposal. Those questions are delegated like any other in scope, and Claude may still choose to put them to the decision owner.

Alternatives considered#

  • Leave ready PRs for the decision owner to merge. Rejected: The decision owner asked how to remove the block because they would be away, then replied "done".
  • Keep asking the decision owner each question. Rejected by the decision owner's instruction.
  • Decide without recording. Rejected: a later reader could not tell a delegated decision from an unreviewed assumption.

EPM-DEC-001-0029: How core 1.0.0 is built: intake: targets, provenance, IRIs and the release package#

From EPM-DEC-001-0029-core-1-0-0-release-design.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner approved, 2026-10-13)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsThe open implementation questions of the core 1.0.0 release
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's delegation of 2026-10-12 (#207 comment 6137222677); EPM-DEC-001-0028 merged in #242; the decision owner approved the design on 2026-10-13

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0013 releases core 1.0.0 after Phase 1: the canonical facts at that time, zero intake: triples, and a fresh attestation. Step 4 decision Q2 retires all 5,571 intake: triples in one release (step4/step4-decisions.md:52-62). The approved decisions fix most targets (Q5 flows, Q6 horizon facets, Q7 responsible domain, Q8 concept kind, Q9 status dimensions, Q10 terminology notes). They leave implementation questions open, and the conservation ledger (step4/ledger-v2.md, still a draft) disagrees with the approved decisions in three places. The decision owner delegated these questions: "Make decisions for me using available evidence to make the best decision and light external searcch to resolve ambiguity."

Decision#

Precedence. Where the draft ledger and an approved decision disagree, the approved decision wins: core:taxonomyLevel is kept as derived metadata (step4-decisions.md:35-37), not retired (ledger, master conservation table, level row); Q8 and Q9 are approved (step4-decisions.md:151, :219), not pending (ledger, master conservation table, conceptTypeCheck and status rows); cross-functional domains are normalized with the detail in a note (Q7, step4-decisions.md:118-128), not kept verbatim (ledger, responsibleDomain section).

Targets for each intake: predicate (counts from the census, ledger, master conservation table):

intake: predicateTarget in core 1.0.0Basis
levelcore:taxonomyLevel, xsd:integer 0–6, derived from the L-levelstep4-decisions.md:35-37
apqcDecisioncore:apqcMappingDecision → code list core:ApqcMappingDecision (review-link, no-source, no-candidate, rejected, adopted)ledger, apqcDecision section; one kind and range per property (EPM-DEC-001-0020)
terminologyNotesthe ledger's deterministic rule: migration events, and dual-aspect notes, → dcterms:provenance; authoring history and decision-record pointers → skos:editorialNote, pointer text keptQ10 (step4-decisions.md:236-257); ledger, terminologyNotes section
former namesthe approved label report (label-report/label-dispositions-v1.2.csv): AddAsAltLabel and RetainAsAltLabel → skos:altLabel; MigrationOnly and RetireAsAmbiguous, and scoped aliases, → core:priorPreferredLabel (annotation, not searchable)Q10; label report v1.1 approved (the 90 rows it carries); the 14 rows v1.2 adds have their own recorded verdicts of 2026-09-24 and 2026-09-25
referenceSourcesone dcterms:source link per cited source ID to src:<id in lower case>, a dcterms:BibliographicResource carrying the registry's title, publisher and permitted use; the 8 annotation suffixes (the census's 9 counted one annotation twice; ledger, referenceSources section) → skos:editorialNote on the concept, prefixed with the source IDledger, referenceSources section
primaryPurposecore:primaryPurpose, rdf:langStringledger, master conservation table
conceptTypeCheckcore:conceptKind → code list core:ConceptKind (process, capability, structural-anchor); "not-process" marks the two L0 roots, which Q8 names as structural anchorsQ8 (step4-decisions.md:151-161)
relatedConceptsremoved. The canonical facts (step4/canonical-facts.csv) and the scoped sequences replace it. Held, deferred, external-governance and structured-flow mentions emit nothing and stay recorded in the Step 4 evidencemapping v2 (approved 2026-09-29); EPM-DEC-001-0025, -0026
responsibleDomaincore:responsibleDomain, rdf:langString; the 12 cross-functional rows (6 distinct values) → "Cross-functional", the original text in skos:noteQ7
processHorizoncore:operatingMode, core:cadence, core:planningLevel, each to its own code list; daily, weekly and monthly also set operating mode periodic; "periodic" alone sets no cadenceQ6; ledger, processHorizon section
primaryOutput, keyInputscore:primaryOutputFlow and core:keyInputFlow, rdf:langString, one triple per flow name (inputs split on |), whitespace normalized; no nodesQ5 (step4-decisions.md:93-103)
parkedChildrencore:plannedChildNote, annotation, rdf:langStringledger, master conservation table
intake:status"retired" (CM-1-1-4-6) → core:lifecycleStatus retired, with its existing owl:deprecated true; "blocked" (CM-1-1-4-6-1, parked by PTC-001 until its destination exists) → core:holdStatus implementation-hold, with its note as core:holdReason. All three Q9 code lists are defined in fullQ9 revision (step4-decisions.md:219-234)

Facts carry no reifiers. Each canonical fact is one direct triple, proc:S core:P proc:O. Its provenance (row IDs, raw verbs, notes) ships beside the ontology as canonical-facts.csv, which satisfies "the raw verb is retained in migration/provenance evidence on every emitted triple" (verb-predicate-mapping-v2.md, direction table) without reification, which EPM-DEC-001-0020 rules out.

No blank nodes. The 500 APQC dcterms:references nodes become src:apqc-pcf-7.2.2/element/<id> resources with the same identifier, title and dcterms:isPartOf. The 503 workbook dcterms:source nodes, which all carry the same title, become one resource, src:step3c-definition-workbook. (Playbook: "No blank nodes in released data"; EPM-DEC-001-0020 rule 3.)

The release package. business_architecture/ontology/release/build_core.py builds business_architecture/ontology/releases/core/1.0.0/ from the pinned Step 4 baseline: core.ttl (the ontology header, the core vocabulary, the code lists, the taxonomy with its migrated fields, the canonical facts and the scoped sequences), canonical-facts.csv (provenance), conservation.csv (the after half of the ledger: every census triple's destination), counts.json (the build's measured figures), label-dispositions.csv (the label report the former names follow), manifest.json (files and SHA-256 checksums, the source commit) and CHANGELOG.md; core.jsonld, shapes.ttl and the DCAT record catalog.ttl ship beside them. The header follows the playbook's worked example (ontology-playbook.md, "Ontology IRI and version IRI"). Until the decision owner approves the release, the package is built as release candidate 1.0.0-rc.1, with no dcterms:issued; at the cut, build_core.py sets the release date as dcterms:issued and drops the candidate label. The SHACL release slice (EPM-DEC-001-0014) runs on core.ttl before the package is written.

Approvals. The decision owner approved this design, the conservation ledger and the release on 2026-10-13, and gave the fresh no-consumer attestation (see the decision owner's recorded words). A major release needs their approval ("Major (including any deprecation): The decision owner. No exceptions", step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md, "Who approves a release").
