EPM-DEC-001-0030: The organization module's vocabulary: RACI assignments, control between legal entities, RACI levels#

From EPM-DEC-001-0030-organization-module-vocabulary.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 5, slice 1: the classes and properties the organization module needs before roles and assignments are emitted
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028 amended in #252, merged)

Context#

Step 5 models organizations, roles and responsibilities (ontology-playbook.md, Step 5). Earlier decisions already fix much of it:

  • The module policy (step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md, "Module policy — decided 2026-09-18") makes organization the module for roles and RACI assignments, depending on core only.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0015 puts MPC reference instances in a separate ref-mpc module, and models MPC's control of MPLX as a relation separate from org:subOrganizationOf, with share, as-of date and source on a qualified-relation node (EPM-DEC-001-0020).
  • EPM-DEC-001-0023 maps to public ontologies and does not import them.

The process authority, business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json, carries a raci object (responsible, accountable, consulted and informed, each a list of role names) on 153 processes.

Decision#

The organization module's namespace is https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/modules/organization/ (the …/modules/{module} pattern of the URI policy). Its vocabulary, in business_architecture/ontology/organization/vocabulary.ttl:

  • orgm:ResponsibilityAssignment. A node binding one process (orgm:forProcess, a concept of the core scheme), one role (orgm:assignedRole, an org:Role) and one RACI level (orgm:raciLevel). It also keeps the role as the source wrote it (orgm:sourceRoleText) and cites its source (dcterms:source). One node per process, role and level, because a single property cannot carry the three-way binding (playbook Step 5).
  • orgm:RaciLevel. A code list of four members (responsible, accountable, consulted, informed), the levels the process map uses.
  • orgm:ControlRelationship. A node with the controlling and controlled legal entities (org:FormalOrganization), the ownership share (0 to 1), the as-of date and the source.
  • W3C ORG terms by IRI. org:Role, org:FormalOrganization and org:OrganizationalUnit are used by IRI. ORG is not imported (EPM-DEC-001-0023).

shapes.ttl states a cardinality on every property (EPM-DEC-001-0020, rule 2):

  • Assignment: exactly one process, role, level and source text, and at least one source.
  • Role: exactly one English name. A definition is optional until the role register supplies definitions.
  • Control relationship: exactly one of each entity, at most one share, exactly one as-of date, and at least one source.

Left to later slices: the roles themselves and their normalization from the source's 91 role strings; the assignments; the ref-mpc instances; the links from core:responsibleDomain to org units; and the relation for who owns a meaning (competency question 17). (Superseded 2026-10-13 for the core:responsibleDomain links: The decision owner decided the values are business domains, not org units, EPM-DEC-001-0038.)

Alternatives considered#

  • A property per RACI level from process to role (responsibleRole, accountableRole, …). Rejected: the playbook requires the n-ary node, and a node can carry its source text and citation.
  • Import W3C ORG. Rejected by EPM-DEC-001-0023: map, don't import.
  • Make a role definition required now. Rejected for this slice: the source gives role names only. Definitions come with the role register.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner extended the delegation in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-13 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md; also added in #252, merged): "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." The decision owner did not make the choices above themselves. Claude made them under that delegation, and the decision owner can reverse any of them.

Evidence#

ontology-playbook.md, Step 5; EPM-DEC-001-0015, 0020, 0023; the module and URI policies in step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md; downstream_process_map.json (raci on 153 processes).

Consequences#

  • Competency questions 3 to 5 become answerable once the roles and assignments are emitted (later slices).
  • Every assignment traces to its source entry through orgm:sourceRoleText and dcterms:source.

EPM-DEC-001-0031: The role register: how the process map's RACI role strings become roles#

From EPM-DEC-001-0031-role-register.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028); its questions answered by the decision owner, EPM-DEC-001-0045 (2026-10-18)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 5, slice 2: which of the RACI role strings in the process map name the same role, and how a team, a qualifier or a compound string is handled
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028, amended in #252)

Context#

The process map, business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json, holds a raci object on 153 processes. Its lists hold 913 entries, written as 91 distinct strings. Many strings are spellings of one role: "Refinery Scheduler", "Refinery scheduler", "Refinery Schedulers" and "Refinery schedulers". Some name a team ("Risk Group"), some add a qualifier ("Distribution planner (optional)"), and 11 name two or three roles at once ("Supply Coordinators/Traders").

EPM-DEC-001-0030 makes each RACI entry an orgm:ResponsibilityAssignment that names one org:Role. The roles must exist before the assignments do (slice 3).

The repository does not expand the acronyms in the role strings (CCI, CP, CPVC, E&P, GFVC, PSM, RCVO, RVCO, BP&A, RFC, RO). No MPC or peer filing in business_architecture/reference/ names these roles.

Decision#

business_architecture/ontology/organization/role-register.csv maps each of the 91 strings to one or more of the 60 roles in roles.csv. build_roles.py checks the register against the process map and writes roles.ttl. The rules:

  1. Merge only on the surface of the text. Two strings name the same role only when they differ in case, plural, punctuation, or "&" against "and". Nothing is merged on a guess at what an acronym or a title means.
  2. A qualifier stays with the entry, not the role. "Distribution planner (optional)" maps to Distribution Planner. The qualifier is kept in the register, and slice 3 keeps the full source string on each assignment (orgm:sourceRoleText).
  3. A compound string is split into its roles. "Supply Coordinators/Traders" maps to Supply Coordinator and Trader. Slice 3 then emits one assignment per role.
  4. A team named in a RACI list becomes a role, with a scope note. Six strings name a team: BP&A, Operations, Regional Planning Team, Risk Group, Site Tactical Team and Tech Services. Each becomes an org:Role whose scope note says it stands for the team acting as one RACI party. The org units themselves come in slice 5.
  5. Each role gets one English name (skos:prefLabel). A different spelling of the whole role, with no qualifier, is kept as skos:altLabel.
  6. Role IRIs are https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/modules/organization/role/{slug}, the …/modules/{module} pattern of the URI policy.

The result: 60 roles, 54 positions and 6 teams. Splitting the 11 compound strings turns the 913 entries into 940 role references. build_roles.py prints the figures.

Questions for the decision owner#

2026-10-18: answered by the decision owner; organization 2.0.0 carries the answers (EPM-DEC-001-0045). Forecaster and PSM stay open. 2026-10-28: PSM is expanded as Process Safety Management in organization 2.2.0; Forecaster stays open (EPM-DEC-001-0045, amendment of 2026-10-28).

These do not block the build. Each would merge two roles, or define one, if the decision owner confirms it. roles.csv held each one in its question column; since 2.0.0 that column holds only the questions still open (Forecaster, PSM and Crude RFC's expansion; 2026-10-28: since organization 2.2.0, only Forecaster, EPM-DEC-001-0045, amendment of 2026-10-28), and the answers and settled rationales sit in its note column.

  • RCVO Advisor (written once) and RVCO Advisor (written 32 times): one role misspelt?
  • Forecaster and Refinery Technical Service Representative appear only inside two compound strings. Are they the Local Refinery Forecaster and the Refinery Technical Representative?
  • The two VP strings differ only in RVCO. Are they one VP?
  • Crude RFC: is it the crude Refinery Feedstock Coordinator?
  • The acronyms (CCI, CP, CPVC, E&P, GFVC, PSM, RVCO, BP&A, RO): what does each stand for? The answers become role definitions.

Alternatives considered#

  • Merge by meaning now (RCVO into RVCO, Forecaster into Local Refinery Forecaster). Rejected: the evidence is the spelling alone, and a wrong merge would assign one role's responsibilities to another. Splitting later costs more than merging later.
  • Model teams as org:OrganizationalUnit, assigned directly. Rejected for this slice: EPM-DEC-001-0030 gives an assignment exactly one org:Role, and changing that shape for six strings would widen every query that asks who is accountable. Slice 5 links the units.
  • Put the whole register to the decision owner before building on it. Rejected: under rule 1 the register applies only mechanical rules, which the delegation covers. The judgement calls are listed above, for the decision owner to answer when convenient.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner extended the delegation in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-13 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md): "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." The decision owner did not make the choices above themselves. Claude made them under that delegation, and the decision owner can reverse any of them.

Evidence#

downstream_process_map.json (raci on 153 processes); EPM-DEC-001-0030; ontology-playbook.md, Step 5; the URI policy in step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md. A search of the repository for each acronym found no expansion.

Consequences#

  • scripts/check.sh runs build_roles.py --verify and test_roles.py. A new role string in the process map fails the check until the register maps it.
  • Slice 3 emits the assignments from this register.
  • An answer to a question above changes roles.csv and the register in a later PR, and records the answer here.

EPM-DEC-001-0032: The RACI assignments: one per source entry and role, nothing inferred#

From EPM-DEC-001-0032-raci-assignments.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028; RACI scope by the decision owner, 2026-10-13)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 5, slice 3: how the process map's RACI entries become orgm:ResponsibilityAssignment nodes, and what the ontology says about processes with no RACI
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028, amended in #252)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0030 defines the assignment node: exactly one process, role, RACI level and source text, and at least one source. EPM-DEC-001-0031 maps each RACI role string in the process map to one or more roles. The playbook's Step 5 gate reads: every in-scope process has RACI, and none is inferred from the hierarchy.

The process map holds RACI on 153 processes. Two cases need a rule:

  • Compound strings. One entry can name two or three roles.
  • One role under two strings. On "Gather Manual Data Inputs" (CM 1.1.2.2.3), the consulted list holds three strings naming Trader and Supply Coordinator, each with a different qualifier (for Petrochemicals, Blendstocks or Feedstocks), plus "Supply Coordinator - Asphalt".

Decision#

  1. One assignment per RACI entry and per role the entry names. A compound string gives one assignment per role. Two entries that name the same role on the same process and level stay two assignments, because each keeps its own source text and qualifier. A query that asks who holds a level uses DISTINCT over process, level and role.
  2. Nothing is inferred. A process without a raci object in the source gets no assignment, even when its parent or child has one.
  3. The IRI is …/organization/assignment/{process slug}--{level}--{role slug}--{hash}, where the hash is the first eight hex digits of the SHA-256 of the source string. Reordering a source list does not change any IRI.
  4. A ledger reconciles the counts. raci-conservation.csv records the RACI entries in and the assignments out, each in total and by level, and the extra roles from compound strings in total. The build fails if entries plus extra roles differ from assignments.
  5. Competency questions 3 to 5 become answerable. The regression runner loads the organization module with the core release. Two queries must return nothing: an assignment that points outside the core scheme, at a structural anchor or at an unnamed role (Q3), and a process that has RACI but no accountable role (Q4). Structural anchors carry no RACI (the conceptKind decision of 2026-09-22 in the worklog), so Q4 leaves them out.

build_assignments.py prints the figures.

RACI scope: decided by the decision owner, 2026-10-13#

Q4 lists the live concepts of the core scheme with no accountable role, other than structural anchors. They include concepts of kind process, concepts of kind capability, and concepts with no kind. Today that means every live, non-anchor concept without RACI in the source. The Step 5 gate asks for RACI on every in-scope process; until the decision owner's answer below, no recorded decision said which processes were in scope for RACI.

Claude recommended counting the processes that have RACI in the source as the RACI scope for this release, and treating the rest as the source author's backlog, not as a gap for the ontology to fill. The decision owner chose that option (recorded below). So the 153 processes with RACI in the source are this release's RACI scope. Every one has RACI and an accountable role (Q4's must-be-none query), so the Step 5 gate's RACI condition holds. Q4 keeps listing the other live concepts, as the source author's backlog.

Alternatives considered#

  • One assignment per process, level and role. Rejected: it drops the qualifier of all but one entry, and orgm:sourceRoleText allows exactly one value.
  • Inherit RACI from a parent process. Rejected by the playbook's Step 5 gate.
  • IRIs from the entry's position in its list. Rejected: a source edit that reorders a list would remint IRIs.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner extended the delegation in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-13 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md): "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." The decision owner did not make the design choices above themselves. Claude made them under that delegation, and the decision owner can reverse any of them.

The decision owner decided the RACI scope themselves in the same session (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/raci-scope.md). Asked which processes need RACI for this release, they chose: "The 153 with source RACI (Recommended)".

Evidence#

downstream_process_map.json (raci on 153 processes); EPM-DEC-001-0030, 0031; ontology-playbook.md, Step 5; competency-questions.md, questions 3 to 5.

Consequences#

  • scripts/check.sh runs build_assignments.py --verify, test_assignments.py and the regression with the organization module loaded.
  • A change to the process map's RACI fails the check until the assignments are rebuilt.

EPM-DEC-001-0033: The ref-mpc module: MPC, MPLX, the control relationship, segments and refineries#

From EPM-DEC-001-0033-ref-mpc-module.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 5, slice 4: which MPC reference instances the ref-mpc module holds, and how each fact carries its source and as-of date
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028, amended in #252)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0015 puts MPC reference instances in a separate ref-mpc module, makes MPLX an Organization that MPC controls through a relation separate from org:subOrganizationOf, and keeps the Midstream segment as a separate concept. EPM-DEC-001-0030 defines orgm:ControlRelationship. The playbook's Step 5 asks for a source and an as-of date on each fact.

MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K (MPC-S01, accession 0001510295-26-000009, filed 2026-02-26) states:

  • "As of December 31, 2025, we owned the general partner of MPLX and approximately 64 percent of the outstanding MPLX common units."
  • "Our operations consist of three reportable operating segments: Refining & Marketing, Midstream and Renewable Diesel."
  • MPC owns and operates refineries in the Gulf Coast, Mid-Continent and West Coast regions, with 2,986 mbpcd of crude oil refining capacity. Item 2 names 13 refineries (business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-fy2025-10k-fact-sheet.md, section 3).

Decision#

business_architecture/ontology/ref-mpc/ref-mpc.ttl holds:

  1. MPC and MPLX as org:FormalOrganization.
  2. MPC's control of MPLX as one orgm:ControlRelationship: share 0.64, as of 2025-12-31, source MPC-S01. A note quotes the filing's "approximately", because the share is rounded.
  3. The three reportable segments as org:OrganizationalUnit, each org:unitOf MPC. Midstream's scope note says MPLX's operations roll up into it.
  4. The 13 refineries as org:Site, each org:siteOf MPC, with its location and region in a note.
  5. Source and date on every instance. Each instance cites src:mpc-s01 (dcterms:source) and states 2025-12-31, the date its facts held. The organizations, segments and refineries state it with dcterms:date. The control relationship states it with orgm:asOf, the property EPM-DEC-001-0030 requires on a control relationship. The source node carries its title, accession number, filing date and URL. It gives the URL as a dcterms:bibliographicCitation, as core's source nodes do, so core's source shape (every dcterms:source target is a titled resource) holds when the modules are validated together.
  6. GLEIF by identifier (EPM-DEC-001-0023). MPC's LEI is 3BNYRYQHD39K4LCKQF12 and MPLX's is 5493000CZJ19CK4P3G36, each as org:identifier with an rdfs:seeAlso link to its GLEIF API record. GLEIF lists MPLX's registration as lapsed, and records no accounting-consolidation parent for it (reporting exception NO_KNOWN_PERSON). So the control relationship has no GLEIF Level 2 relationship to map to. MPLX's note says so.
  7. No terms of its own. The module uses the organization module and W3C ORG by IRI.

Left out for now:

  • Refinery capacity. It is a dated quantity with a unit, which waits for the measure pattern of Step 7.
  • Renewable diesel facilities, terminals and joint ventures. Nothing in Steps 5 to 7 asks for them yet.

Alternatives considered#

  • MPLX as org:subOrganizationOf MPC. Rejected by EPM-DEC-001-0015.
  • Only the Midstream segment, the one EPM-DEC-001-0015 names. Rejected: the FY2025 10-K names three, and Refining & Marketing holds the refineries.
  • An as-of date on the source only. Rejected: the playbook asks for one on each fact, and a later filing may restate some facts but not others.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner extended the delegation in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-13 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md): "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." The decision owner did not make the choices above themselves. Claude made them under that delegation, and the decision owner can reverse any of them.

Evidence#

MPC-S01, the FY2025 Form 10-K (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1510295/000151029526000009/mpc-20251231.htm), checked for the MPLX and segment sentences on 2026-10-13; the GLEIF API (https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records), queried on 2026-10-13 for both legal names and for MPLX's parent relationships; business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-fy2025-10k-fact-sheet.md for the refinery list; EPM-DEC-001-0015, 0020, 0030.

Consequences#

  • scripts/check.sh runs ref-mpc/test_ref_mpc.py.
  • A later filing adds or restates facts with its own source and date. Earlier facts stay as they were.

EPM-DEC-001-0034: Step 6: a run links to the process definition it followed through prov:hadPlan#

From EPM-DEC-001-0034-definition-to-run-pattern.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 6: how a consumer's record of a process run links to the process definition, and what the ontology checks
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028, amended in #252)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0016 adopts P-Plan as the bridge from definitions to runs. The ontology defines the pattern and holds no occurrence records, and consumers such as PPC hold the runs. The playbook's Step 6 is done when no definition is typed as an occurrence, the definition-to-run pattern is defined, and every occurrence a consumer records can link to its definition.

The core 1.0.0 process concepts are skos:Concepts, and none is a prov:Activity. P-Plan (release 17 September 2013, revision 1.1, http://purl.org/net/p-plan#) states that the association of a whole plan with its execution "can be modeled with PROV (with the prov:hadPlan property)". PROV-O is a W3C Recommendation of 30 April 2013.

Decision#

  1. A run is a prov:Activity held by the consumer. It has exactly one prov:qualifiedAssociation, whose prov:hadPlan names exactly one core process concept: the definition the run followed. It states prov:startedAtTime, and may state prov:endedAtTime and prov:agent.
  2. The definition must be a live process. It is in the core scheme, not retired, not a structural anchor, and not a capability. The worklog's conceptKind decision of 2026-09-22 calls structural anchors not work anyone performs, and defines a capability as "an ability the organization has, realized by processes". The capability exclusion is Claude's conclusion from that definition, added on 2026-10-13 after #258 merged; see the amendment below.
  3. No new terms. The pattern uses PROV-O as published. P-Plan's steps and variables are not used now. The process taxonomy is a naming hierarchy, not a plan's step structure (playbook: SKOS first), and the 2026-09-22 flow decision keeps inputs and outputs as governed values, not nodes.
  4. The ontology publishes the shapes. business_architecture/ontology/provenance/run-shapes.ttl holds the shapes a consumer's run must meet, plus one that fails any core concept typed as a prov:Activity. example-run.ttl illustrates a run; it is not data.

Step 6's first procedure, planned inputs and outputs on the definitions, is not done here. The only source for them is the process map's sioc field, which holds suppliers, inputs, outputs and customers on 153 processes. The playbook says to confirm that field's business meaning before naming a term after it. That question went to the decision owner, who answered it on 2026-10-13 (below); competency question 8 waits for the core release that carries the answer.

The sioc field: answered by the decision owner, 2026-10-13#

The decision owner confirmed that sioc is SIPOC. EPM-DEC-001-0038 records their words and carries the inputs and outputs into core 2.0.0 as planned inputs and outputs.

Amendment, 2026-10-13#

#258 merged with a shape that let a run follow a concept of kind capability. core 1.0.0 holds 23 of them. The worklog defines a capability as "an ability the organization has, realized by processes". Claude concluded that a run follows one of the processes that realize a capability, not the capability itself. The live-process shape now rejects a capability, decided under the same delegation; the worklog itself does not say this. Concepts with no kind stay allowed: the shape rejects only the two kinds it names.

Alternatives considered#

  • Type each process concept as a p:Plan and its children as p:Steps. Rejected: it changes the released core 1.0.0, and it reads the naming hierarchy as an execution structure.
  • A direct property from the run to the definition (for example core:runOf). Rejected: PROV-O's prov:hadPlan already says it, and consumers that speak PROV-O read it without a mapping.
  • Mint planned input and output nodes from sioc now. Rejected: the field's meaning was unconfirmed when this was decided (the decision owner confirmed it on 2026-10-13), and the 2026-09-22 decision defers flow nodes.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner extended the delegation in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-13 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md): "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." The decision owner did not make the choices above themselves. Claude made them under that delegation, and the decision owner can reverse any of them.

Evidence#

ontology-playbook.md, Step 6 and the PROV-O and P-Plan entry; EPM-DEC-001-0005, 0016; the worklog's flow-modeling and conceptKind decisions of 2026-09-22; the P-Plan specification (https://www.opmw.org/model/p-plan/, release 17 September 2013, revision 1.1); PROV-O (https://www.w3.org/TR/2013/REC-prov-o-20130430/); downstream_process_map.json (sioc on 153 processes).

Consequences#

  • scripts/check.sh runs provenance/test_run_shapes.py. A core release that types a definition as an activity fails it.
  • A consumer validates its runs against run-shapes.ttl together with the core release.

EPM-DEC-001-0035: Step 9: code-list values come from their own list; evidence links wait for the next core release#

From EPM-DEC-001-0035-step9-controlled-values.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028); items 2 and 4 carried out in core 2.1.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0044, 2026-10-18)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 9: how the controlled-value shapes are added after the core 1.0.0 release, and what the evidence-link shape needs
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028, amended in #252)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0014 orders the Step 9 shapes after the release slice: every relationship fact links to its source evidence; controlled values come from their allowed lists; no definition is typed as an occurrence. The third is in EPM-DEC-001-0034 (Step 6).

The core 1.0.0 release shapes check sh:in on core:conceptKind only. Seven other code-list properties are checked only with sh:class skos:Concept. core:evidenceBasis and core:sequenceScope have no release shape; their build-time shapes in step4/scoped-sequence/ check them. Either way, a value from the wrong list passes release validation: for example, a cadence used as an operating mode.

The 640 canonical facts in core 1.0.0 are direct triples. (The 17 scoped-sequence nodes do carry their source rows.) Their evidence (the REL row IDs) is in the release's canonical-facts.csv, not in the graph.

Decision#

  1. A controlled-value shape for every code-list property. business_architecture/ontology/validation/controlled-values.ttl requires each value of the ten coded properties of core (APQC mapping decision, operating mode, cadence, planning level, lifecycle status, governance status, hold status, concept kind, evidence basis, sequence scope) to be in its own code list. It applies the same rule to the organization module's RACI level. core 1.0.0 and the organization module's RACI assignments (EPM-DEC-001-0032) conform.
  2. The shapes run against the released core without changing it. scripts/check.sh runs them. They join the release shapes at the next core release. 2026-10-18: they did, in core 2.1.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0044).
  3. A test keeps the list complete. It fails when a core property takes values from a core code list and no shape covers it. That test found evidenceBasis and sequenceScope while this slice was built.
  4. The evidence-link shape waits for the next core release. EPM-DEC-001-0014 orders it first; this slice takes the second item first because today every canonical fact would fail it: no canonical fact carries its evidence in the graph. Adding that is a change to the released module. 2026-10-18: done in core 2.1.0, one evidence node per fact (EPM-DEC-001-0044).

For the decision owner: evidence on the facts#

EPM-DEC-001-0014 says I walk you through each shape and its violation report. Two things to approve:

  • The controlled-value shapes. core 1.0.0 has no violations.
  • How the next core release carries evidence. Recommendation: one qualified-relation node per canonical fact (EPM-DEC-001-0020, rule 4 as decided), naming the subject, the predicate, the object and the REL rows from canonical-facts.csv. That adds 640 nodes. The evidence-link shape then requires one such node per fact.

Alternatives considered#

  • Add the shapes to core 1.0.0. Rejected: the release is fixed; its shapes change only in a new release.
  • Check the evidence links against canonical-facts.csv in a script. Rejected as the Step 9 shape: the rule is that the graph itself links each fact to its evidence. The release's build already checks the CSV.

EPM-DEC-001-0036: Step 12: the property-graph projection pipeline#

From EPM-DEC-001-0036-lpg-projection-pipeline.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028); 2026-10-19: the decision owner accepted #262 as merged, after the fact (EPM-DEC-001-0050)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 12: how the ontology is projected into Neo4j under the guideline in lpg-projection/ (v2.1, Rule 4a), and what the pipeline proves
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028, amended in #252)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0020 adds Step 12: follow the guideline's pipeline (check, reason, load, verify), apply its Rules 1, 2, 3 and 5 from core 1.0.0 on, and use Rule 4a, qualified-relation nodes. EPM-DEC-001-0021 pins the guideline in lpg-projection/. The guideline's done-when: SHACL clean in the graph, an empty round-trip diff, and equal determinism hashes.

Decision#

business_architecture/ontology/projection/pipeline.py runs the guideline's five steps over core 1.0.0, the organization module and ref-mpc, with every shape file the build has:

  1. Check. The five rules over the ontology, then pySHACL over the data with all the shapes, with SHACL-AF on. Any problem stops the build.
  2. Reason. ELK through ROBOT 1.9.10, once. The OWL API writes declarations into the reasoner's output (owl:NamedIndividual on 2,086 instances, plus owl:Class, owl:AnnotationProperty, owl:Thing, rdfs:Datatype). They are not inferences about the domain, so inferred.nt drops any triple whose object is an OWL or RDFS built-in. Today that leaves no inferred triples: the ontology is SKOS-first and has almost no class axioms.
  3. Serialise. Sorted, de-duplicated N-Triples and an input fingerprint (SHA-256 of loadable.nt and inferred.nt), recorded in projection-manifest.json.
  4. Load. Neo4j 2025.06.2 with n10s 2025.06.1 and APOC, n10s.rdf.import of both files, with generated configuration and the fixed prefixes.
  5. Verify. n10s SHACL in the graph, a round-trip diff, and two loads (sorted, and shuffled) whose hashes must match. The result is projection-report.json, which records the input fingerprint and a fingerprint of every shape file, so a change to either makes the committed report stale.

Choices where the guideline leaves room or does not fit the tools:

  • SHORTEN, not the guideline's MAP example. In MAP mode, n10s 2025.06.1 would not import the shapes here: n10s.validation.shacl.import.fetch failed with "The graph is not namespace aware". The same mode is behind neosemantics issue #237 (open since 2021-11-09: SHACL not validating with handleVocabUris set to MAP); its fix, PR #361, is still open. In SHORTEN mode each name is the fixed prefix plus the local name (skos__prefLabel), so no two terms share a name (Rule 5) without a separate names file.
  • A subset of the shapes runs in the graph. The in-graph subset leaves out sh:datatype rdf:langString, which n10s rejects, and sh:not, sh:languageIn, object and subject targets and sh:node, which it does not support. Step 1 checks all of them with pySHACL, SHACL-AF included (SPARQL targets such as core's ProcessConcept, and SPARQL constraints such as the scoped-sequence ancestor check), on every triple that is loaded: the asserted data and inferred.nt. Shapes with a SPARQL target do not run in the graph at all. A zero round-trip diff proves the graph holds those triples, so the full shapes hold in the graph. To show the in-graph check can fail, Step 5 removes one role's name and requires n10s to report it.
  • The round trip compares values, not spellings. A plain literal and an xsd:string literal are the same (RDF 1.1). Integers load as INTEGER and return as xsd:long, and decimals load as FLOAT and return as xsd:double (guideline section 8). n10s keeps a language tag inside the stored string, and the export returns it untagged, so the comparison reads the tag back. The build refuses a source string that ends like a tag, and a decimal whose value, printed as a double, is not its own lexical form (0.64 prints back as 0.64; a decimal such as 0.1000000000000000055 would not).
  • Add-on C (exact decimals) is not adopted. The one decimal property is the two-place ownership share, and no query computes with it. Adopt Add-on C if a decimal needs exact arithmetic.
  • Release gaps are listed, not hidden. core 1.0.0 gives core:earlierProcess and core:laterProcess no rdfs:range, which breaks Rule 1. Only a new core release can fix that. The pipeline names both gaps with their fix and fails on any other gap, and on a listed gap that no longer occurs.
  • Steps 2, 4 and 5 run locally. Step 2 needs Java, and Steps 4 and 5 need a Neo4j container. scripts/check.sh runs Steps 1 and 3 and checks that the committed report matches the current inputs and passed.

The first run found a defect in ref-mpc, fixed in #261 (merged as d5eb311): its source nodes linked their URLs through dcterms:source, which core's source shape forbids.

For the decision owner#

2026-10-22: the 16 release gaps listed in the amendment of 2026-10-22 close at the next release of value-stream, kpi, party and integration. Each fix adds a range or a cardinality shape and changes no released IRI, so each fits a patch or minor release, which the module policy gives to the module owner (worklog, "Who approves a release"; EPM-DEC-001-0037). 2026-10-23: except integration's con:reliesOn range, which narrows its published definition; the decision owner chose a major, integration 2.0.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0094). Claude asks the decision owner before building any of them, as for every release issued on 2026-10-22 (EPM-DEC-001-0074, and 0076 to 0080). 2026-10-23: the decision owner approved the releases (EPM-DEC-001-0088).

The next core release should add rdfs:range skos:Concept to core:earlierProcess and core:laterProcess. The pipeline then fails until the two listed gaps are removed.

Note 2026-10-14: guideline v2.2 (enterprise-people-graph PR #3, 8ce9ada) adopts the choices above (SHORTEN, the in-graph subset, the declaration filter, the round-trip value rules, the CI report check), so they are no longer deviations from the guideline. lpg-projection/ is re-pinned to it (EPM-DEC-001-0021).

Amendment 2026-10-13: done. core 2.0.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0038) adds both ranges. The pipeline now projects 2.0.0, and its list of release gaps is empty.

Amendment 2026-10-22 (Phase E, delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028): the pipeline projects every released package, each at its newest version: core 2.2.0, organization 2.0.0, ref-mpc 1.2.0, kpi 1.1.0, party 1.0.0, data-product, value-stream, system, lane and market-data 1.0.0, and integration 1.0.0. It reads the packages under releases/, not the source files, and runs each package's own shapes.ttl with the scoped-sequence and run shapes. kpi 1.1.0 is loaded, though value-stream, market-data and integration were released against 1.0.1, because the projection takes each module's newest release; their own release checks still test the pinned versions. Under the delegation:

  1. The rules cover every module's namespace. Rules 1 and 2 apply to the predicates of the 13 module namespaces, and each has a fixed prefix (Rule 3), the prefix its package uses where it has one.
  2. Rule 4a knows five more qualified-relation classes: value-stream's measure link (EPM-DEC-001-0054), market-data's leg (EPM-DEC-001-0057, 0066), and party's role, data flow and customer sourcing (EPM-DEC-001-0068, 0069). The measure link, the leg and the role are reached from one end by an inverse link (vs:measureLink, mkt:leg, party:plays), which the guideline allows; the rule checks that exactly one such link points to each node.
  3. 16 release gaps are listed, each with its fix. Eight own properties declare no single concrete range (Rule 1), and eight have no shape stating their cardinality (Rule 2), in value-stream 1.0.0, kpi 1.1.0, party 1.0.0 and integration 1.0.0. Only a new release of each module can close them, so the pipeline names each with its fix, as it named core 1.0.0's two gaps.
  4. Two more constraints stay out of the graph. n10s matches sh:pattern against the whole string, where SHACL searches it. Its SHACLValidator (in the n10s 2025.06.1 jar) tests each value with Cypher's =~, and the Cypher Manual's examples of =~ ("String operators", https://neo4j.com/docs/cypher-manual/current/expressions/predicates/string-operators/) need a leading or trailing .* to match part of a string ('.*@company.com'). In the run, party's "\S" failed on 101 values longer than one character, while the anchored ^[1-7](, [1-7])*$ passed. n10s also did not match a loaded true against market-data's sh:in (true), which failed on 2 series. So sh:pattern, and an sh:in list that holds a literal, join the subset left to Step 1's pySHACL check. An sh:in list of IRIs still runs in the graph.

The run: 33,742 triples, no inferences, 3,925 nodes and 19,692 relationships, no SHACL violation in the graph over 588 in-graph constraints (the mutant still caught), an empty round-trip diff, and equal hashes for the sorted and shuffled loads. 2026-10-23: core 2.3.0 replaced 2.2.0 among the packages (EPM-DEC-001-0089); the rerun gave 33,751 triples, 3,927 nodes and 19,695 relationships, with every check passing as before. 2026-10-23, later: value-stream 1.1.0 closed its two gaps, leaving 14 (EPM-DEC-001-0090); party 1.1.0 closed its eight, leaving 6, and the rerun gave 34,054 triples, 3,971 nodes and 19,840 relationships, every check passing (EPM-DEC-001-0092); kpi 1.1.1 closed its one, leaving 5, all in integration (EPM-DEC-001-0093); integration 2.0.0 closed them, so the list is empty, and the rerun gave 34,058 triples, 3,971 nodes and 19,843 relationships, every check passing (EPM-DEC-001-0094).

Alternatives considered#

  • MAP mode with explicit names. Rejected: the SHACL import failed in that mode (above).
  • Keep the OWL API declarations in inferred.nt. Rejected: every instance would gain an owl__NamedIndividual label and nothing about the domain.
  • Skip the in-graph SHACL. Rejected: the guideline requires it; the subset plus the round trip keeps the guarantee.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner extended the delegation in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-13 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md): "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." The decision owner did not make the choices above themselves. Claude made them under that delegation, and the decision owner can reverse any of them.

Evidence#

EPM-DEC-001-0020, 0021, 0033; lpg-projection/Ontology_to_LPG_Conversion_Playbook.md (v2.1), sections 3, 5, 7 and 8; ROBOT v1.9.10 (https://github.com/ontodev/robot/releases/tag/v1.9.10, 2026-02-18); n10s 2025.06.1 (https://github.com/neo4j-labs/neosemantics/releases/tag/2025.06.1, 2026-06-16); Neo4j 2025.06.2 (tag 2025.06.2, "Release 2025.06.2", 2025-07-11, https://github.com/neo4j/neo4j/releases/tag/2025.06.2); projection-report.json.

Consequences#

  • scripts/check.sh runs pipeline.py build --verify and test_pipeline.py. A change to any input or shape file fails it until the pipeline is rerun and the manifest and report are committed.
  • The graph can be rebuilt from the Turtle files at any time; Turtle stays the master copy.

EPM-DEC-001-0037: Step 10: release candidates for the organization and ref-mpc modules#

From EPM-DEC-001-0037-module-release-candidates.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028); the decision owner approved the 1.0.0 releases, 2026-10-13
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsStep 10: how the modules after core are packaged for publication, and what waits for the decision owner
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13, under the decision owner's extension of the delegation to Steps 5 to 12 (EPM-DEC-001-0028, amended in #252)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0018 decides Step 10: w3id permanent IRIs, a DCAT catalog of each release and its files, and a release package shaped to EPM-ARCH-REL-001's manifest. core 1.0.0 shipped that way (EPM-DEC-001-0029): its package holds the module's Turtle and JSON-LD, its shapes, a DCAT record, a changelog and a manifest with each file's SHA-256. The module policy (2026-09-18) gives minor and patch releases to the module owner and majors to the decision owner. A module's first release, 1.0.0, is a major.

When this was decided, the organization and ref-mpc modules existed on main with no release.

Decision#

  1. One builder for the other modules. business_architecture/ontology/release/build_module.py builds a module's package in the shape of the core 1.0.0 package: <module>.ttl, <module>.jsonld, shapes.ttl, catalog.ttl, CHANGELOG.md and manifest.json. It validates the module with its shapes, with core 1.0.0 loaded for the concepts the module refers to.
  2. Candidates first, then releases on the decision owner's approval. Both modules were first built as 1.0.0-rc.1, with no issued date, as core was before the decision owner approved it. The decision owner approved both on 2026-10-13, so both are now 1.0.0, issued 2026-10-13 (below). EPM-ARCH-REL-001 asks a release to record an immutable commit; the merge commit's tags record it, as core-v1.0.0 did.
  3. Dependencies name versions that exist. Organization 1.0.0 requires core 1.0.0. ref-mpc 1.0.0 requires organization 1.0.0. While both were candidates, the ref-mpc candidate required the organization candidate, 1.0.0-rc.1, because organization 1.0.0 did not exist yet. Each requirement is a dcterms:requires link on the ontology header and an entry in the manifest; no module imports another.
  4. The packages stay current. scripts/check.sh runs the builder with --verify for both modules, and test_build_module.py checks the packages.

The decision owner's approval, 2026-10-13#

The decision owner approved both modules as 1.0.0 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-13-session2.md): "aprove now". Organization 1.0.0 and ref-mpc 1.0.0 are the candidates with the issued and modified dates set to 2026-10-13 and the stage removed; ref-mpc now requires organization 1.0.0. The packages are in releases/organization/1.0.0/ and releases/ref-mpc/1.0.0/, and each manifest names where their approval is recorded. When this merges, the merge commit gets the tags organization-v1.0.0 and ref-mpc-v1.0.0.

For the decision owner#

Step 10 is done when the catalog resolves and the release can be retrieved. Three things are theirs:

  1. Approve organization 1.0.0 and ref-mpc 1.0.0 (each a major). Done: approved on 2026-10-13 (above).
  2. Register w3id.org/lsc. The IRIs resolve only after a redirect is registered with w3id, a public, outside action. 2026-10-14: The decision owner decided not to host the namespace and to keep it as a placeholder; under the delegation, it is not registered either (EPM-DEC-001-0039).
  3. Choose where the files are hosted and under which license. 2026-10-14: not hosted (the decision owner, EPM-DEC-001-0039); the licence is still open. The ontology README says the folder is proprietary, all rights reserved; a public catalog needs a decision on what is published. 2026-10-18: proprietary (the decision owner, EPM-DEC-001-0041). Item 3 is closed.

Alternatives considered#

  • Issue 1.0.0 now under the delegation. Rejected: the module policy reserves majors for the decision owner.
  • Extend build_core.py to the other modules. Rejected: its steps (intake migration, canonical facts, conservation ledger) are specific to core.
  • Let the ref-mpc candidate require organization 1.0.0. Rejected while organization 1.0.0 did not exist; the released ref-mpc 1.0.0 requires it.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner extended the delegation in the Claude Code session on 2026-10-13 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md): "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." The decision owner did not make the choices above themselves. Claude made them under that delegation, and the decision owner can reverse any of them.

EPM-DEC-001-0038: core 2.0.0: business domains as a code list, SIPOC planned inputs and outputs#

From EPM-DEC-001-0038-core-2-business-domains-and-sipoc.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-13: SIPOC, the domain split, the 2.0.0 release and the SIPOC backlog; the build choices delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028); 2026-10-19: the decision owner accepted #266 and #269 as merged, after the fact (EPM-DEC-001-0050)
Date2026-10-13
QuestionsWhat the process map's sioc field means; whether the core:responsibleDomain values are org units or business domains, and how "Commercial & Marketing" splits; how those answers and the two missing ranges reach core
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-13

Context#

  • sioc. The process map holds a sioc field on 153 processes with four lists: suppliers, inputs, outputs and customers. The playbook says to confirm an ambiguous field's meaning before naming terms after it (EPM-DEC-001-0034 put the question to the decision owner).
  • responsibleDomain. core 1.0.0 carries core:responsibleDomain as an interim literal with five values: Commercial & Marketing (456), Finance-enabled Supply & Trading (13), Cross-functional (12), Finance (2) and Refining (2). Step 4's Q7 left it for Step 5 to map "where the operating model evidences the relationship (some labels are business domains, not org units)".
  • Ranges. core 1.0.0 gives core:earlierProcess and core:laterProcess no rdfs:range (EPM-DEC-001-0036 lists them as release gaps).

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions:

  1. sioc is SIPOC: suppliers, inputs, process, outputs and customers. The process is the node itself.
  2. The domain values are business domains, not org units. "Commercial & Marketing" is two domains. CM 1.3 is Marketing; CM 1.1 and CM 1.2 are Commercial; CM 1.4 (IT Services) is its own domain.

How they reach core (Claude, under the delegation):

  1. A business domain code list. core:BusinessDomain holds seven concepts: Commercial, Marketing, IT Services, Finance-enabled Supply & Trading, Finance, Refining and Cross-functional. A new object property, core:businessDomain, links each concept that carries a 1.0.0 core:responsibleDomain value to one of them: 485 concepts, of which 463 are of kind process and 22 of kind capability. Today that is 205 Commercial, 251 Marketing, 13 Finance-enabled Supply & Trading, 12 Cross-functional, 2 Finance and 2 Refining; no CM 1.4 process has a domain label yet. A Cross-functional process keeps the note that names its domains.
  2. core:responsibleDomain is deprecated, not removed. It is marked owl:deprecated true, and its values stay, so consumers that read it keep working while they move to core:businessDomain.
  3. Planned inputs and outputs. core:plannedInput and core:plannedOutput carry the SIPOC inputs and outputs as governed literals, one per item (the 2026-09-22 flow decision: no flow nodes). That is 362 inputs and 219 outputs on 153 processes. Suppliers and customers stay parked: they name roles and systems, which need their own mapping.
  4. The two ranges. core:earlierProcess and core:laterProcess get rdfs:range skos:Concept.
  5. A major release, built as a candidate. A deprecation makes this 2.0.0 (module policy: majors are the decision owner's). release/build_core_next.py builds it from the released 1.0.0 graph, so 1.0.0 stays as built from its pinned evidence. It wrote releases/core/2.0.0-rc.1/ with no issued date until the decision owner approved the release.
  6. The release. The decision owner approved core 2.0.0 on 2026-10-13. The builder now writes releases/core/2.0.0/: the candidate's content with dcterms:issued and dcterms:modified set to 2026-10-13 and the stage removed. The candidate package is removed, as 1.0.0-rc.1 was at the 1.0.0 cut. The manifest and the tests name the sentence that approved it. The merge commit gets the tag core-v2.0.0.

The decision owner's decision on the rest (2026-10-13):

  1. The live processes with no SIPOC in the process map (504 on core 2.0.0, listed by the Q8 query regression/queries/q08a-no-planned-flows.rq) are the source author's backlog. The ontology does not draft inputs and outputs for them. When the process map gains a SIPOC field, the next core build carries it.

Left out: the evidence nodes on canonical facts (EPM-DEC-001-0035), which wait for the decision owner's walkthrough.

For the decision owner#

  1. Approve core 2.0.0. Approved 2026-10-13 (item 8). The projection (EPM-DEC-001-0036) and the regression then moved to 2.0.0 in their own change. That closed the two listed release gaps, and question 8 is answerable from the planned inputs and outputs.
  2. Consider moving CM 1.4 IT Services under the enabling functions (L1-process-excellence-it). That changes the process map, so it is a proposal for the source author.

Alternatives considered#

  • Change the core:responsibleDomain values in place. Rejected: consumers filtering on "Commercial & Marketing" would break silently, and a literal is not a governed list.
  • Mint org units for the domains. Rejected: The decision owner decided they are business domains.
  • Mint nodes for inputs and outputs. Rejected by the 2026-09-22 flow decision until a use case needs one flow traced.

EPM-DEC-001-0039: the w3id namespace is a placeholder; nothing is registered or hosted#

FieldValue

EPM-DEC-001-0042: most planner measures are operational metrics; a KPI is a designation with a target and an owner#

From EPM-DEC-001-0042-kpi-measure-classification.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028; the decision owner's instructions recorded in EPM-DEC-001-0040); 2026-10-19: the decision owner approved the eight classes (EPM-DEC-001-0050); the rule and each row's class stay delegated
Date2026-10-18
QuestionsHow to classify the measures in the decision owner's planner list (EPM-DEC-001-0040, the decision owner's decisions 6 and 7); what Crude Value to Breakeven is; whether the Mid-Term Planner's turnaround work is optimization or tracking
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-18; research in reference/mpc/mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md and reference/downstream-peers/kpi-classification-evidence.md

Context#

The decision owner said most of their list are operational metrics, not KPIs, and asked for each to be classified from industry practice and MPC's public information, with KPI candidates marked (EPM-DEC-001-0040). Under the delegation, no target or owner is invented (EPM-DEC-001-0040, What follows). The classification is in business_architecture/ontology/kpi/measure-classification.csv: one row per distinct measure.

Decision#

  1. A KPI is a designation, not a kind of measure. A measure is a KPI only when the business names it as one of the few measures of a stated objective, with a target and an accountable owner. The same ratio can be a KPI for a region and an operational metric for a unit. Basis: the KPI Institute (KI-03), SCOR "Only the top-level Metrics are referred to as strategic metrics or key performance indicators" (KI-23), Parmenter's team and critical-success-factor tests (KI-01, KI-02), ISO 22400-1's note that KPIs are chosen by criteria (KI-06).
  2. The rule. Apply the tests in order and stop at the first that assigns a class:
    1. No formula, unit or time grain: tracker or report (or not a measure, for a capability request).
    2. Emitted only when a condition is met: alert.
    3. Value set by an external market or benchmark the region cannot influence: market indicator. A value computed from market prices and the planning model to start or rank a decision, with no performance target: decision-support indicator.
    4. Actual results, or a plan, compared with a plan, forecast, prior plan version or history, for one period: plan-variance diagnostic.
    5. Designated as above, with a target and owner: KPI. Otherwise: operational metric. Where the evidence makes a measure a strong KPI candidate, the row says kpi_candidate = yes.
  3. Result. 41 measures: 32 from the decision owner's list, 8 that MPC publishes and 1 from industry practice. By class: 15 operational metrics (Swing Stream Disposition counted here on the assumption that its TBD calculation is a volume per period), 7 market indicators, 6 plan-variance diagnostics, 6 trackers or reports, 2 alerts, 2 not measures (future capabilities, parked), 2 KPIs and 1 decision-support indicator. Six operational metrics are KPI candidates: Crude Utilization, R&M Margin per Barrel, Margin Capture, Refining Operating Costs per Barrel, Planned Turnaround Costs per Barrel and Lost Margin from Turnarounds.
  4. No row of the decision owner's list is a KPI on present evidence. None has a recorded target or owner. The two KPIs are MPC's own: Relative Refining Margin per Barrel by Region and Relative Adjusted EBITDA per Barrel of Total Throughput, which MPC's annual cash bonus sets with targets (MPC-S16, MPC-S18).
  5. Crude Value to Breakeven is a decision-support indicator. It is a crude-selection screen: each crude's breakeven value from the LP model against its delivered price, flagging incremental barrels for a full evaluation. Its after-the-fact counterpart is margin capture (VT-01, VT-02, VT-04, VT-09, VT-12; MPC-S07). No public source uses the name "V2Be", so the reading matches the list's description to standard practice.
  6. The Mid-Term Planner's turnaround work is optimization. At M+4 to M+36 the planner chooses turnaround windows and their sequence, and plans supply and inventory around them; schedule adherence is the turnaround team's tracking (VT-16, VT-17, VT-18; MPC-S01). The TAR Activities Tracker stays a tracker for the Prompt Planner. Planned Turnaround Costs per Barrel (MPC) and Lost Margin from Turnarounds are the turnaround measures the mid-term view affects.
  7. Comparison is part of a variance measure, and only a view of other measures. "Demand vs. Forecast" is defined by its comparison. "Pipeline Utilization" against the distribution plan, or against five years of history, is the same measure asked against different references, not a second measure (competency question 30). The compared_against column records the references each persona uses.
  8. Utilization and per-barrel measures carry their basis. MPC's crude utilization divides by average calendar-day capacity; the planner list divides by constrained capacity; peers use stream-day or regional bases (mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md, §2). A definition names its denominator, and R&M margin per barrel is versioned because MPC added derivative impacts in 2026 (MPC-S17).

Alternatives considered#

  • Call every row on the list a KPI. Not chosen: The decision owner said most are operational metrics, and no row has a target or owner.
  • Parmenter's narrow rule (at most 10 KPIs, none financial, none monthly). Not chosen as the rule: it would exclude margin capture and all of MPC's bonus metrics. It is cited as support.
  • ISO 22400's wide use of "KPI" for shop-floor ratios. Not chosen: it would make every utilization ratio a KPI, which does not separate the few from the many.
  • Treat Crude Value to Breakeven as a market indicator. Not chosen: it depends on each refinery's LP model, not on the market alone.

The decision owner's recorded words#

The decision owner's instructions are recorded in EPM-DEC-001-0040 and saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-18.md: "Some of these are not actually KPI, majority are operational metrics. Please lassify those as such." and "7. Mark candidate based on industry best practices and publickly available information". On the two open measures they wrote "8 and 9.. can you research this". The rule, the classes and every row's verdict are Claude's, made under EPM-DEC-001-0028 (saved in delegation-steps-5-12.md: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12."), and the decision owner can reverse them.

Evidence#

business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md (MPC first); business_architecture/reference/downstream-peers/kpi-classification-evidence.md (industry practice, support only for the generic frameworks; Part B for Crude Value to Breakeven and turnarounds); business_architecture/ontology/kpi/measure-classification.csv.

Consequences#

  • The kpi module defines these 41 measures (definitions only, EPM-DEC-001-0005), with the class, the candidate flag and the personas from the table.
  • A measure moves to KPI only by a recorded business decision naming its target and owner.

EPM-DEC-001-0043: the kpi module's vocabulary: measure definitions, measure kinds, planning personas and process links#

From EPM-DEC-001-0043-kpi-module-vocabulary.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-18
QuestionsHow the kpi module represents the measures classified in EPM-DEC-001-0042
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-18 (night run)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0040 seeds the kpi module from the decision owner's planner measure list, and EPM-DEC-001-0042 classifies its 41 measures. The module now needs a vocabulary that answers the Named KPI competency questions (13, 14, 27 to 36) without holding a value (EPM-DEC-001-0005).

Decision#

  1. A measure is a kpi:MeasureDefinition, also a skos:Concept in the scheme kpi:Measures, with one name, one definition and a notation equal to its row ID. It carries no value.
  2. Its kind comes from the code list kpi:MeasureKind: the eight kinds of EPM-DEC-001-0042, exactly one per measure. A SHACL shape lets only a measure definition carry a kind, so Customer or a process cannot be classified as a KPI (competency question 13's "done when"). Another shape lets only an operational metric be a KPI candidate, and a third gives a tracker, report or non-measure no grain. Q13's locked "done when" ("Customer" cannot be classified as a KPI, and "3-2-1 Crack Spread" can) is read as a capability: a measure definition such as the crack spread can carry any kind, including kpi, and Customer can carry none. EPM-DEC-001-0042 gives the crack spread the kind market indicator; that is a classification, not a limit of the model.
  3. Comparison is an attribute, not a new measure. kpi:comparedAgainst lists the plans, forecasts, plan versions or history a measure is asked against (competency question 30). kpi:grain and kpi:basis state the grain and, in words, the numerator, denominator and unit. The basis is a description; the ontology computes nothing (competency question 34's "without putting the formula in the ontology").
  4. "What it is not" is a link. kpi:notToBeConfusedWith names a nearby measure that is often mistaken for this one, and the measure's skos:scopeNote says how they differ. The 3-2-1 crack spread links to R&M margin per barrel, margin capture and the R&M Margin Indicator (competency questions 32 and 36). Where the nearby thing is not a measure in the module (distillate sales, MPLX pipeline throughput, a contract tariff), the scope note says what the measure is not without a link; six measures carry a note of that kind. Aliases such as "the crack" are skos:altLabel.
  5. Personas are org:Role instances in the kpi module, each with a plan type and a plan horizon. They stay out of the organization module, whose role register reconciles every role with the process map's RACI strings (EPM-DEC-001-0031); these four come from the decision owner's list instead.
  6. kpi:informsProcess links a measure to the core processes it informs, as context, not certification (competency question 38). Each link was chosen by matching the measure's use to a process name in core 2.0.0 (the same in 2.1.0, which the release requires), for example demand against forecast to CM 1.1.1.1.5 Monitor Demand Forecasting Performance and Reporting, and margin capture to CM 1.1.5 Regional Backcasting and CM 1.3.5.1.4 Analyze Gaps Between Forecast & Actual Margins. Four measures have no link: the two capabilities parked as not measures, and MPC's two bonus KPIs, which no process in the map covers.
  7. The module is built and released as kpi 1.0.0. build_measures.py builds measures.ttl from three tables (measure-classification.csv, measure-details.csv, personas.csv) and --verify fails unless the file equals a fresh build. Its first release is 1.0.0, a major, which needs the decision owner ("Major (including any deprecation): The decision owner. No exceptions"). The decision owner approved that cut ("Approve kpi 1.0.0 release too", saved in answers-2026-10-19-kpi.md); 2026-10-19: 1.0.0 is cut (releases/kpi/1.0.0/), requiring core 2.1.0. 2026-10-19: 1.0.1, a patch, gives the planner list's source its name, VCO DSV (EPM-DEC-001-0040).

Alternatives considered#

  • A qualified node per persona and measure, carrying each persona's horizon and comparison. Not chosen: the competency questions ask what a measure is and what it is compared against, not who compares it at which horizon. The persona's horizon sits on the persona.
  • Put the personas in the organization module. Not chosen: see decision 5.
  • Record the formula. Not chosen: competency question 34 asks for the concepts a measure quantifies "without putting the formula in the ontology".

The decision owner's recorded words#

On the release, saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-19-kpi.md: "Approve kpi 1.0.0 release too".

EPM-DEC-001-0044: Step 9: one evidence node per canonical fact, the controlled-value shapes in the release, and core 2.1.0#

From EPM-DEC-001-0044-step9-evidence-nodes-core-2-1-0.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-18: the shapes, the evidence pattern and cutting 2.1.0; the build choices delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-18
QuestionsStep 9 walkthrough (decision-packages/2026-10-14/step9-walkthrough.md), questions 1 and 2; whether Claude may cut the minor release
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-18

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0035 left two things for the decision owner: approving the eleven controlled-value shapes, and choosing how canonical facts carry their evidence in the next core release. The walkthrough set out both. core 2.0.0 holds 640 canonical facts on nine predicates, supported by 812 workbook rows, which sat only in releases/core/1.0.0/canonical-facts.csv.

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions:

  1. Approve the eleven controlled-value shapes as written and include them in the next core release, keeping the existing release constraints.
  2. Add one evidence node per canonical fact and keep the direct triple. Each node names exactly one subject, predicate and object and carries every distinct supporting REL ID, in a new evidence property, not core:sourceRow. Triples and nodes come from one mapping, with the checks their answer lists. The evidence mapping travels with the release.
  3. Cut the minor release, core 2.1.0.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Names. core:CanonicalFact, with core:factSubject, core:factPredicate, core:factObject and core:evidenceRow. Fact IRIs are https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/fact/<subject>--<predicate>--<object>, so a fact's IRI follows its triple.
  2. One build. release/build_core_2_1.py reads the 1.0.0 canonical-fact mapping, stops unless its 640 rows are exactly the 640 direct triples of 2.0.0, and then writes one node per row. The mapping ships in the package as canonical-facts.csv.
  3. The decision owner's seven checks. Fact structure, predicate scope, evidence presence and both directions between triple and node are release shapes (shp:CanonicalFact, shp:CanonicalTriple). Evidence correctness is a build check (evidence_problems): a node's rows must equal the mapping's rows for that exact fact, so a well-formed REL ID on the wrong fact fails. Reproducibility is --verify in scripts/check.sh. release/test_build_core_2_1.py breaks each check once and shows it fails.
  4. The controlled-value shapes are appended to the release shapes verbatim. They check scheme membership; no sh:maxCount 1 is put on skos:inScheme. validation/test_controlled_values.py now reads core 2.1.0 and counts core:businessDomain as covered by the release's own shape from 2.0.0.
  5. Rights. The header's dangling dcterms:license gives way to dcterms:rights: all rights reserved, with APQC's attribution (EPM-DEC-001-0041). This removes a triple, but only one that named no licence text, so the release stays minor.
  6. Consumers. The projection (Rule 4a now names core:CanonicalFact) and the regression read 2.1.0. The projection was rerun end to end on Neo4j 2025.06.2 with n10s 2025.06.1: 2,811 nodes and 14,862 relationships, no SHACL violation in the graph, an empty round-trip diff, equal hashes for two load orders. 2026-10-19: with organization 2.0.0 the run loads 2,811 nodes and 14,865 relationships (EPM-DEC-001-0045). The regression is unchanged: 9 answerable, 5 partial, 30 waiting.

Alternatives considered#

  • 2b, the node alone. Not chosen by the decision owner; it would remove triples queries read (a major).
  • Reuse core:sourceRow. Not chosen: its domain is the scoped sequence, so a reasoner would type every fact node as one.
  • Check evidence correctness in SHACL. Not chosen: the shapes cannot know which row supports which fact without loading the mapping into the graph; the build check reads it directly.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-18.md: "1a, 2a." and "Add one evidence node per canonical fact and keep the direct triple." Their approval of the cut is saved in answers-2026-10-18-night.md: "Yes, cut 2.1.0 (Recommended)". They also gave standing approval for the night's releases, saved in answers-2026-10-19-releases.md: "You have my approval to keep going and release things as needed. while I am out". This release needed no other module release. Items 1 to 6 under "What follows" are Claude's, made under EPM-DEC-001-0028 (saved in delegation-steps-5-12.md: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12."), and the decision owner can reverse them.

Evidence#

Consequences#

  • EPM-DEC-001-0035's evidence-link item is done. Step 9's three shape sets (EPM-DEC-001-0014) are all in a release.

EPM-DEC-001-0045: The decision owner's role answers, carried by organization 2.0.0#

FieldValue

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0031 merged role strings on spelling only and left seven questions for the decision owner. Organization 1.0.0 was released with those roles on 2026-10-13. A merge cannot delete a released role: the playbook deprecates it and points it at its successor, in a major release only (ontology-playbook.md, Appendix B, "Deprecation (never delete)").

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions:

  1. RCVO Advisor and RVCO Advisor are one role; "RCVO" is a misspelling.
  2. Forecaster stays apart from Local Refinery Forecaster until the refinery Economics and Planning process owner confirms they are the same.
  3. Refinery Technical Service Representative is the Refinery Technical Representative.
  4. Of the two long VP strings, the one with "RVCO" survives.
  5. Crude RFC stays its own role. RFC is read as Refinery Feedstock Coordinator, as a contextual reading pending confirmation. 2026-10-28: the decision owner confirmed the reading (amendment of 2026-10-28 below).
  6. The acronym expansions with evidence are adopted: CP (Clean Products), CPVC (Clean Products Value Chain), GFVC (Global Feedstocks Value Chain), RVCO (Refinery Value Chain Optimization), CCI (Continual Commercial Improvement), E&P (Economics and Planning), BP&A (Business Planning and Analysis) and RO (Regional Optimization). PSM stays unresolved. An expansion is not a merge. 2026-10-28: PSM is Process Safety Management, the decision owner's answer, in organization 2.2.0 (amendment of 2026-10-28).
  7. Who owns a concept's meaning is a separate governance decision, not answered by process RACI.
  8. Organization 2.0.0 is approved for these merges, and a ref-mpc release if one is needed.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Deprecate, never delete. role:rcvo-advisor, role:refinery-technical-service-representative and role:vp-clean-products-crude-trading-ngls-asphalt-petchem keep every 1.0.0 triple and gain owl:deprecated true and dcterms:isReplacedBy their successor. The misspelling becomes a skos:hiddenLabel of RVCO Advisor; the other two source spellings become skos:altLabels of their successors. 60 roles, 57 live.
  2. Assignments keep their IRIs. The three assignments that named a deprecated role now name its successor. Each keeps its IRI, which names the role the source string was registered to, and its source text. 940 assignments, as before; the ledger adds a row for the 3 moved. A SHACL shape rejects an assignment that names a deprecated role. The owl:deprecated shape checks a boolean datatype; test_roles.py checks every value is true. (sh:in ( true ) was tried first; in the projection run of 2026-10-19 the in-graph n10s validation reported each deprecated role's true as not in the list, while pyshacl accepted it. That is what this repo observed, not a documented n10s behaviour.)
  3. Labels. roles.csv keeps the source spelling in label, which the surface rules of EPM-DEC-001-0031 check. An expanded preferred_label becomes the skos:prefLabel, and the source spelling an skos:altLabel. Ten roles take an expanded name: nine from question 6 and Crude RFC from question 5. CP is expanded only in CP Manager, and E&P only in the refinery-planning roles. VP RVCO stays its own role, as the package proposed: it is Accountable where the long VP is Informed.
  4. Evidence. Each answered role carries an editorial note with its evidence and its status: inferred from the process map (CP), or externally corroborated by MPC material. The corroborating sources are an MPC newsroom article for CPVC (2023-04-20) and for GFVC (2021-06-02), both read through a search index because the site blocks automated reads, and MPC job postings on third-party hosts for RVCO, CCI, E&P and BP&A, each read on 2026-10-18. A job posting on a third-party host is weaker evidence than a filing. A 2021 source supports the term, not today's structure.
  5. Definitions. Four roles take a working definition, written by Claude: Refinery Value Chain Optimization Advisor, Refinery Technical Representative and Crude Refinery Feedstock Coordinator carry EPM working definitions, not MPC position descriptions. Business Planning and Analysis carries a definition adapted from an MPC job posting, which its note quotes. The decision owner's answers chose which roles expand; they do not word the definitions.
  6. Not merged on an expansion. E&P Supervisor and Refinery Econ & Planning Supervisor stay apart; CP Manager and the Global Feedstocks Manager stay apart.
  7. Competency question 17 stays waiting, now on this record. The proposed design, not built: a qualified governance assignment that names a concept or scheme, a role, a responsibility (semantic owner, business data steward, technical data custodian or process owner), a scope, dates, evidence and an approval status. 2026-10-23: the decision owner parked Q17 until the ontology is first shared with business consumers; the design above is built then, from the business's assignments (EPM-DEC-001-0086, ontology/business-closeout.md, point 1).
  8. Release. Organization 2.0.0 (issued 2026-10-18) requires core 2.1.0. The branch first required 2.0.0; core 2.1.0 was released while it was open (EPM-DEC-001-0044). 2.1.0 is a minor release: it adds evidence nodes and shapes and keeps every process concept the assignments point at, so the assignments mean the same on it. A new release requires the current release of what it depends on, and organization 2.0.0 was not yet released, so changing its requirement changes no published package. ref-mpc 1.1.0 (issued 2026-10-19) requires organization 2.0.0. It is a minor release: only its requirement changes, and every query on 1.0.0 gives the same answer on 1.1.0, because ref-mpc uses only the control relationship, which organization 2.0.0 keeps unchanged. The 1.0.0 packages of both modules stay on disk as released. build_module.py gives organization 2.0.0 5,987 triples and ref-mpc 1.1.0 140. The projection, rerun on core 2.1.0 with organization 2.0.0, loads 2,811 nodes and 14,865 relationships: three more relationships than on organization 1.0.0, the dcterms:isReplacedBy links. It has no SHACL violation in the graph, an empty round-trip diff and equal hashes for two load orders.

EPM-DEC-001-0046: Market Data and Commercial Risk: definitions, scope and owners#

From EPM-DEC-001-0046-market-data-commercial-risk.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-18: MD1 to MD5; the wording of the register and glossary delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-18
QuestionsMD1 to MD5 in decision-packages/2026-10-14/step7-rulings.md; the "What the decision owner decides" list in market-data-and-commercial-risk-draft.md
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-18

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0006 made Market Data its own domain and EPM-DEC-001-0007 gave Commercial Risk price risk and hedging (P10). EPM-DEC-001-0010 left their full definitions to Step 7. The draft of 2026-10-13 (market-data-and-commercial-risk-draft.md, PR #268) proposed them and asked the decision owner five questions.

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions (their answers to the package's options; their words are under "The decision owner's recorded words"):

  1. MD1, B: accept with changes. The domain split stands, with explicit distinctions between external observations, shared market-data products, risk methodologies and modelled parameters. Their two definitions:
    • Market Data governs externally sourced market observations and shared market-data products, including benchmark identifiers, source provenance, licensing restrictions, timestamps, assessment conventions, and approved curve-construction metadata.
    • Commercial Risk governs commercial exposure, risk measures, limits, risk-model methodologies and parameters, and trading-counterparty credit assessment and exposure.
  2. MD2, A provisionally. The Risk Group is accountable for Market Data, pending confirmation of its mandate. Operational stewardship, licensing controls and independent validation are recorded separately.
  3. MD3, A. The Risk Group owns Commercial Risk (the package's option A: the "Risk Group" team in the role register).
  4. MD4, A. Credit risk is in Commercial Risk, limited to trading counterparties; customer AR credit stays outside it. Trade confirmations stay with trade operations and feed the domain (the package's option A).
  5. MD5, A, for risk-model parameters. Commercial Risk owns volatilities and correlations as risk-model parameters; vendor-supplied volatility observations are governed as Market Data.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The data domain register's two rows carry these definitions and owners; "Owner to be confirmed" is replaced. The register's open item on scope and owners is done; the domains' key entities (the draft's "Key entities" rows) are not decided and stay open.
  2. The glossary rows for both domains carry the decision owner's definitions. Their status stays Draft, because their answer does not approve the glossary wording (the package said the status stays Draft unless their answer says otherwise).
  3. The draft file is marked decided and points here.
  4. The MD2 responsibilities are recorded in words only. Modelling them as assignments waits for the semantic-ownership decision that the decision owner kept separate (role question 7).
  5. The analysis the decision owner pasted with their answers suggested further distinctions, which are not saved as their words and are not decided here: that EPM governs its record of a publisher's methodology rather than the methodology (EPM-DEC-001-0006 still says Market Data owns benchmark methodology, P11); that business requirements come from trading, supply, refinery economics and planning, and finance; that a counterparty keeps one legal-entity identity across trading risk and receivables; and examples of risk-model parameters and vendor observations. Each can be taken up when the domain models are written.

Alternatives considered#

  • Accept the draft as written (MD1 A). Not chosen: The decision owner's answer was B, with the distinctions above.
  • A dedicated market data team, or trading, supply and economics, as Market Data's owner (MD2 B, C). Not chosen: The decision owner chose the Risk Group provisionally.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-18.md: "MD1: B. Accept the domain split with explicit distinctions between external observations, shared market-data products, risk methodologies, and modeled parameters.", "MD2: A provisionally. Assign Risk Group accountability pending mandate confirmation, and record operational stewardship, licensing controls, and independent validation separately.", "MD3: A. MD4: A. MD5: A for risk-model parameters, with vendor-supplied volatility observations governed as Market Data.", and the two definitions: "Market Data governs externally sourced market observations and shared market-data products, including benchmark identifiers, source provenance, licensing restrictions, timestamps, assessment conventions, and approved curve-construction metadata." and "Commercial Risk governs commercial exposure, risk measures, limits, risk-model methodologies and parameters, and trading-counterparty credit assessment and exposure." Items 1 to 5 under "What follows" are Claude's, made under EPM-DEC-001-0028 (saved in delegation-steps-5-12.md: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12."), and the decision owner can reverse them.
