What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. One pull request per proposal that edits the map, each citing this record. Proposals that need no map edit are recorded here and in process-map-suggestions.md.
  2. Released packages stay fixed. With this record's pull request (commit "fix(release): core 2.0.0 builds from the process map at its pinned commit"), core 2.0.0 builds from the map at the commit it was released from, so a map edit does not change a released package. New and changed nodes reach the ontology in a later core release, through the usual path: identity (Step 2), definitions (Step 3), then the release build.

The minted slugs stay (SLUG_PINS in build/step2-identity-map.py).
  4. Nothing is invented to close a gap. Where a condition the decision owner set (P03's physical-execution coverage check; P04's "reuse before new process creation", checked as a fit-gap) finds a real gap, the pull request writes a targeted proposal for the decision owner instead of adding a process.
  5. P06 needs no map edit now. The measure categories are the classes in EPM-DEC-001-0042, which Claude decided under the delegation; that is not the decision owner's approval. So P09 is to be prepared, with each measure's relationship to its stage (informs, benchmarks, measures performance), and applied only after the decision owner approves the categories. 2026-10-19: the decision owner approved them (EPM-DEC-001-0050), so P09 is applied in its own pull request. Each link cites MPC's FY2025 10-K, Item 7.

EPM-DEC-001-0048: the two process-map gaps are addressed from MPC's public information first#

From EPM-DEC-001-0048-process-map-gaps-loss-gain-and-feedstock-traceability.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-19: fill both gaps from high-quality references, MPC's public information first; what is added delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsThe two gaps the MPC-P03 and MPC-P04 checks found (business_architecture/business_process/proposals/MPC-P03-transfer-coverage-gaps.md, question 1; MPC-P04-renewables-traceability.md)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

Under EPM-DEC-001-0047, the checks the decision owner set on MPC-P03 and MPC-P04 found two gaps, and the pull requests proposed them to the decision owner instead of adding processes. First, no step names the loss and gain reconciliation of a movement, although the Loss Control data product exists. Second, no step keeps the traceability and carbon-intensity evidence that renewable feedstock credits depend on.

Decision#

The decision owner's decision: address both gaps from high-quality reference material, with MPC's public information taking precedence over any other source.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Loss and gain. One step, CM 1.1.3.7.13 Reconcile Movement Losses and Gains, under CM 1.1.3.7 Inventory, covering every movement, not only inter-refinery transfers. MPLX's FY2025 10-K describes the work: volume gains and losses from "pressure and temperature changes, evaporation and variances in meter readings and other measurement methods", and imbalances "settled in cash, settled by physical delivery of volumes from a different source, or tracked and settled in the future" (reference/mpc/loss-gain-and-feedstock-evidence.md, LG-01). API MPMS Chapters 17.5 and 23 support the reconciliation method (support only). This is option A of the MPC-P03 proposal, question 1. It produces the Loss Control data product. Question 2 of that proposal (quality of transferred intermediates) is not part of the decision owner's answer and stays open. 2026-10-19: answered, and applied by widening CM 1.2.5.1 (EPM-DEC-001-0051).
  2. Feedstock traceability. One step, CM 1.2.5.1.5 Manage Renewable Feedstock Traceability and Carbon-Intensity Evidence, under CM 1.2.5.1 Crude/Feed Quality Management, feeding environmental credit and clean-fuel incentive management (CM 1.2.4.3.8). MPC's own disclosures describe the work: its feedstock suppliers adhere to "a supplier feedstock information compliance commitment" that includes "renewable biomass certifications, source locations" (CDP 2023), and MPC was "developing a sustainability due diligence process to assess risk within our renewable feedstock supply chain" (2022 Sustainability Report) (FT-01, FT-02). The regulators' record rules then set what the step keeps: EPA RFS (40 CFR 80.1454(b)(3)(vi) and (j)), CARB LCFS (section 95488.9(g)) and the IRS proposed 45Z rules (proposed section 1.45Z-4(f)(1) and (g)(1)) (reference/downstream-peers/loss-gain-and-feedstock-regulatory-evidence.md). ISCC EU 203 supports the chain-of-custody method (support only). This is option A of the MPC-P04 proposal.
  3. Each step lands in its own pull request. Item 1 landed with this record in #291 (process-map/p03-gap-loss-gain). Item 2 lands in process-map/p04-gap-feedstock-traceability.
  4. Each step follows the map's conventions (ID, level, name, description, renewablesSpecific where it applies), and the curated identity map gets its row as Step 2 would emit it.

Alternatives considered#

  • Widen existing descriptions instead (option B of each proposal). Not chosen: each gap is a separate control with its own outputs (the Loss Control data product; the eligibility records the credit processes consume), and MPC's and MPLX's disclosures describe each as work in its own right.
  • Leave the gaps open (option C). Not chosen: The decision owner said to address them.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-19-morning.md: "On two gaps the process-maps found. Address it using high quality refrence materials. Any publickly available informtion from Marathon superseded any other information". Items 1 to 4 under "What follows" are Claude's, made under EPM-DEC-001-0028 (saved in delegation-steps-5-12.md: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12."), and the decision owner can reverse them.

Evidence#

business_architecture/reference/mpc/loss-gain-and-feedstock-evidence.md (MPC and MPLX, first); business_architecture/reference/downstream-peers/loss-gain-and-feedstock-regulatory-evidence.md (regulators, then industry standards as support); the two proposals named above; EPM-DEC-001-0047.

Consequences#

  • The MPC-P03 proposal and its row in process-map-suggestions.md record the loss and gain gap as filled by CM 1.1.3.7.13 (#291). The MPC-P04 proposal and its row record the feedstock gap as filled by CM 1.2.5.1.5, in item 2's pull request.
  • The Loss Control data product names the process that produces it.

EPM-DEC-001-0049: The decision owner is named by role, not by person#

From EPM-DEC-001-0049-decision-owner-named-by-role.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-19)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsItem 4 of the morning summary of 2026-10-19: how the repo names the person who decides
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

Until now the repo named the person who records decisions, approves releases and answers source questions by their personal name: in prose, in four review-file column names, a route type, a constant and the answers folder's name, in the release manifests' approved_by, in the core ontology's dcterms:creator, in one kpi source IRI, and in GitHub links that carried their account name. Read that way, an approval looked like one individual's.

Decision#

The decision owner's decision: neutral, generic wording. The repo names no person. Where it named them, it now names the role, "the decision owner", in every current file. Git history, commit authors and tags are not rewritten.

What follows (Claude, carrying out that decision):

  1. Prose. The name becomes "the decision owner", and the pronouns that referred to them become singular "they". Words the decision owner wrote stay as written, except that a link inside a quote, which carried the account name, is replaced by a bracketed note: "[link removed under EPM-DEC-001-0049: …]" (EPM-DEC-001-0020 and 0022).
  2. Identifiers. The answers folder is now step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/. The columns are now owner_words, owner_decision, owner_rationale and owner_answer, the route type is now owner-verdict, and the constant is now OWNER_APPROVED_GOVERNED_BY. Scripts and tests read the new names.
  3. Links. A GitHub link that carried the account name keeps its issue and comment number and drops the URL ("#207 comment 6137222677"). A link to another repository under that account names the repository. A saved answer's header names its source as a URN, as the source-question template now says. An absolute home-folder path starts at ~/.
  4. Two kinds of IRI change, the only ones. First, the kpi source node src:planner-measure-list-2026-10-18 drops the name from its local name, in kpi/measures.ttl and in kpi 1.0.0 and 1.0.1. Second, the two scoped-sequence sources, which were GitHub comment URLs, are now URNs: urn:epm:issue:205:comment:6115309253 and urn:epm:issue:205:comment:6116668059. The saved answers' headers, scoped-sequences.csv, scoped-sequences.ttl and core 1.0.0, 2.0.0 and 2.1.0 carry the same change, and build_core.py describes each URN as the GitHub comment it names. No other IRI changes.
  5. Released packages are edited in place. Every package whose builder has --write (core 1.0.0, 2.0.0 and 2.1.0, organization 2.0.0, ref-mpc 1.1.0 and kpi 1.0.1) is rebuilt and passes --verify. Organization 1.0.0, ref-mpc 1.0.0 and kpi 1.0.0, which no builder rebuilds, have their files edited and their manifest hashes recomputed. Versions, release dates and tags stay as they were, and the decisions each package carries do not change.
  6. A guard. python3 scripts/epm_facts.py names fails when a tracked file, or a path, contains the personal name, the family name or the GitHub username, in any case: every tracked path, symlinks included, and every part of an Office file: its raw XML, each cell or paragraph as Office displays it (joined from its text runs), and every decoded element text and attribute value, such as a sheet name or the author property. The names are built from fragments in the script, so the guard does not match itself. scripts/check.sh runs it, so CI and the pre-push hook do too, and scripts/test_epm_facts.py tests it.

Alternatives considered#

  • Rewrite git history as well. Not chosen: the decision owner chose "All current files (Recommended)", which leaves history, commit authors and tags as they are.
  • Another term ("the approver", "the project owner"). Not chosen: the decision owner chose "the decision owner (Recommended)".
  • Leave released packages unchanged. Not chosen: the released files named the person in approved_by, dcterms:creator and the kpi source IRI, and the decision covers every current file.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-19-after-run.md, item 4: "neutral wording. Leave my name out of the repo. And also make it generic. Dont make it look like an individual is approving this. Make it generic ." The choices "All current files (Recommended)" and "the decision owner (Recommended)", and "keep A plus the guard", are saved in the same file.

Evidence#

The answers file named above. The replacement used one reviewed script with explicit patterns, kept outside the repo so that its patterns never enter it, and then a read of every changed line.

Consequences#

  • A new text that names the decision owner fails scripts/check.sh.
  • An answer saved from now on goes in decision-owner-answers/, and its header names the role.
  • A reader of a release sees "The decision owner" as approver and creator. The decisions behind each release are unchanged.

SHACL shapes for the new modules (Step 9), DCAT records (Step 10), every competency question answerable or parked by decision (Step 11), the projection on the final releases (Step 12), the 3d-d backlog, the external mappings (EPM-DEC-001-0023) and the partial questions. 2026-10-22: done, with the exceptions and the open items listed in EPM-DEC-001-0083, each with who it waits on.
  6. Phase F, the ontology skill rebuilt from the playbook and the work done, after phases A to E. It starts only after the decision owner approves the plan. 2026-10-22: approved (EPM-DEC-001-0084); the skill ships through pull requests in personal-agent-skills. 2026-10-23: done: personal-agent-skills pull request #45 merged.

The ontology is complete when every competency question is answerable or parked by a recorded decision, every module is released and approved, the external mappings are done, and the projection passes.

Evidence for ambiguity. Where a choice is unclear, Claude searches high-quality external sources in the order AGENTS.md sets: Marathon Petroleum (MPC) filings and public materials first, then U.S. refining peers' SEC filings, then downstream industry sources. Each source used is cited with its date.

Alternatives considered#

  • The Customer domain first. Not chosen: it waits for the decision owner's information, and Phases A and B need nothing from them. 2026-10-21: the information arrived (EPM-DEC-001-0067).
  • A core release before the overlays. Not chosen: the customer lifecycle nodes from Phase C go into the same release, so one release covers both. 2026-10-21: the lifecycle adds no node unless the decision owner accepts MPC-P10.1 (EPM-DEC-001-0070). 2026-10-23: the decision owner accepted MPC-P10 (EPM-DEC-001-0087); core 2.3.0 adds CM 1.3.7.1.7 (EPM-DEC-001-0089).

EPM-DEC-001-0053: The data-product module: definitions from the portfolio, undescribed items parked, process links from the value streams#

From EPM-DEC-001-0053-data-product-module.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsStep 7, item 3 of EPM-DEC-001-0017: the data products; Phase A of EPM-DEC-001-0052; Loss Control ownership, an open Step 7 item in the worklog
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

The data product portfolio (business_architecture/business_process/data_product_portfolio.json) lists 3 segments, 19 data product lines and 48 data products (2026-10-23: 49, with Customer Master, MPC-P10.3, EPM-DEC-001-0091). Its schema calls it a "sibling layer to the LSC process hierarchy; joined to processes via value stream produce/consume links" (business_architecture/schema/data_product_portfolio.schema.json). The 15 data products in the Commercial segment have status active and a description. 2026-10-23: a sixteenth active product, Customer Master (EPM-DEC-001-0091). The 33 in the Midstream and Corporate segments have status defined_only and no description. The process map has no produces or consumes fields; the value streams do. Loss Control had no owning process until EPM-DEC-001-0048 added CM 1.1.3.7.13 Reconcile Movement Losses and Gains, which produces it. That process was added after core 2.1.0.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A new module, data-product, not part of core. core is the process taxonomy; a data product is a sibling layer, as the portfolio's schema says. The kpi module set the pattern: its own namespace, vocabulary, shapes, build and tests, depending on core for process IRIs.
  2. Definitions, never data. A data product is a dp:DataProduct and a skos:Concept in one scheme, with its line as skos:broader, and its line's segment above that. Its definition is the portfolio's description, unchanged. A closed shape keeps data values off it (EPM-DEC-001-0005).
  3. Undescribed items are parked, not written. Each defined_only data product or line carries a skos:editorialNote saying its definition waits for its data owners. A definition written in their place would be a guess at what the portfolio's authors meant.
  4. DCAT is mapped, not imported. dp:DataProduct skos:closeMatch dcat:Dataset (DCAT Version 3, W3C Recommendation, 22 August 2024), as EPM-DEC-001-0023 says. ODPS is not mapped: no recorded source names its version, and the portfolio does not use its terms.
  5. Process links come from the value streams. The value streams name, sparsely, the data products their processes produce and consume; those links enter the ontology with the value streams (Phase B). Segment anchors come from the portfolio's processDomainRef: Commercial in CM 1, Midstream in MS 1 (IRI L1-midstream, EPM-DEC-001-0047), Corporate in Enabling Functions. An anchor is context, not ownership.
  6. Loss Control ownership is settled. Its owning process is CM 1.1.3.7.13 (EPM-DEC-001-0048). links.csv records the link, and the build emits it once a core release holds the process (2.2.0, Phase D). The portfolio's other note, that Loss Control's product-line placement is provisional, stays with the process authority.
  7. No new data product for receivables. The worklog records that no AR credit, receivables or collections portfolio exists and that dp-commercial-risk must not be reused. No source defines one, so the Order to Cash processes' empty produces stay a gap for Phase B to record.
  8. Competency question 7 becomes partial. Its graph part, which data products have no owning process, answers now. What each process produces or consumes waits for Phase B and core 2.2.0. 2026-10-22: both have landed; the Loss Control link is emitted, the Q7 query also reads direct data-product links, and Q7 is answerable (EPM-DEC-001-0076).

Alternatives considered#

  • Data products in core. Not chosen: every data product change would then need a core release, and the portfolio is a separate layer.
  • Write definitions for the 33 undescribed data products from their names. Not chosen: the names alone do not say what each covers.
  • Link each data product to every map node a value-stream process names. Not chosen: a value-stream process names several map nodes, and the source does not say which of them produces the data.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/business_process/data_product_portfolio.json; business_architecture/schema/data_product_portfolio.schema.json; business_architecture/ontology/step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md (Step 7 row, known data gaps); EPM-DEC-001-0005, 0017, 0023, 0047, 0048; W3C, Data Catalog Vocabulary (DCAT) Version 3, Recommendation, 22 August 2024, https://www.w3.org/TR/vocab-dcat-3/.

Consequences#

  • The module is built and checked by scripts/check.sh; it is released only with the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: released as 1.0.0, with the decision owner's approval of the release plan (EPM-DEC-001-0077).
  • The regression stands at 17 answerable, 7 partial and 20 waiting.
  • Phase B adds the produced-by and consumed-by links from the value streams.

EPM-DEC-001-0054: The value-stream module: the two value streams as an overlay that references processes, with data product and measure links#

From EPM-DEC-001-0054-value-stream-module.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsStep 7: capabilities and value streams (competency question 10) and what each process produces or consumes (question 7); Phase B of EPM-DEC-001-0052; the kpi measure links MPC-P09 added (EPM-DEC-001-0047)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

Two value streams sit in business_architecture/business_process/: the Commercial Hydrocarbon Lifecycle (3 stages, 12 capabilities, 12 value-stream processes) and Order to Cash (9 stages, 9 capabilities, 46 value-stream processes). Their schema (business_architecture/schema/value_stream.schema.json) gives the model: Domain, Value Stream, Stage, Capability, Process, with processes that reference process-map nodes through linkedProcessIds and carry sparse produces and consumes links to data products. Every linked process and process group is in core 2.1.0. One value-stream process, Review Credit Limit & Risk Code, links to no map node. MPC-P09 added seven cited measure links on two processes (#300). Both files name their domain "Commercial". In core, the business-domain code Commercial covers CM 1.1 and CM 1.2, and Order to Cash's processes sit in CM 1.3.7 and CM 1.3.8 (EPM-DEC-001-0038).

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A new module, value-stream. Like kpi and data-product, it has its own namespace, vocabulary, shapes, build and tests, and refers to core processes by IRI.
  2. The overlay references, never duplicates. A value-stream process is its own node (vs:ValueStreamProcess), with vs:realizes to each process-map process it names. A value stream is never a process's parent.
  3. Data product links come from the value streams. vs:produces and vs:consumes point to the data-product module's data products or lines. A producing value-stream process is the data product's owning process, as the schema says ("this process owns/publishes"). With these links, competency question 10 is answerable, and question 7 answers all but Loss Control's producer, which waits for core 2.2.0: 8 of the 48 data products have an owning process and 40 do not. 2026-10-22: core 2.2.0 adds CM 1.1.3.7.13 and the link is emitted: 9 have an owning process and 39 do not (EPM-DEC-001-0076). 2026-10-23: 10 have an owning process and 39 do not, of 49, since Customer Master's producer CM 1.3.7.1.1 (EPM-DEC-001-0091).
  4. Measure links are qualified, and context only. Each MPC-P09 link is a vs:MeasureLink with the measure, the relationship from the schema's list, and the cited basis. A shape keeps any measure kind off the link: a link is not a KPI designation (EPM-DEC-001-0042).
  5. The source's domain stays as written. vs:sourceDomain keeps "Commercial". It is not mapped to core's business-domain list, because that list's Commercial code does not cover Order to Cash's processes.
  6. Not carried over: the gapAnalysis entries, which the schema calls documentation only; a flow's free-text note, which restates the flow; and each process's slide letter.
  7. Systems and lanes are separate concerns. The systems a process uses (competency question 6) and its office lane (question 9) are fields of the process map, not of the value streams. They go in their own pull requests.

Alternatives considered#

  • Reuse core:businessDomain for the value stream's domain. Not chosen, for the reason in item 5.
  • Draw producer links from each value-stream process to every process-map node it realizes. Not chosen: the source names several map nodes per value-stream process and does not say which produces the data.
  • One node for both the value-stream process and the map process. Not chosen: the playbook says overlays reference process nodes and never duplicate them.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner asked about these value streams by name: "At waht point I will ging to handle Commercial Lifecyle and Order to cash." (answers-2026-10-19-completion-order.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/business_process/value_stream_commercial_lifecycle.json; business_architecture/business_process/value_stream_order_to_cash.json; business_architecture/schema/value_stream.schema.json; business_architecture/ontology/ontology-playbook.md, Step 7; EPM-DEC-001-0038, 0042, 0047, 0053.

Consequences#

  • The regression stands at 18 answerable, 7 partial and 19 waiting.
  • The module is built and checked by scripts/check.sh; it is released only with the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: released as 1.0.0, with the decision owner's approval of the release plan (EPM-DEC-001-0077).

EPM-DEC-001-0055: The system module: the process map's systems, merged on spelling, named but not defined#

From EPM-DEC-001-0055-system-module.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsStep 7: systems (competency question 6); Phase B of EPM-DEC-001-0052
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

153 nodes of the process map (business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json) have a systems field. Together they hold 37 distinct strings. Some are one system spelled two ways ("RightAngle", "Right Angle"). Three name several systems, joined by "/" ("SAP/ Hyperion", "RightAngle/ TMR/ TopDay", "Right Angle / CargoTracker"). Four name no system: "Manual", "TBD", "To be incorporated in regional Premise solution" and "Manual, to be incorporated in Crude Avails solution". Some are office software (MS Outlook, MS Teams, MS PowerPoint, MS Excel), and some are working documents (the premise documents, "Actuals Case Prep File", "RFC Hindsight Analysis Excel"). No file in the repository defines any of the systems. Every node with a systems field is in core 2.1.0. 2026-10-26: MPC-P11 gives 12 more processes a systems field, so 165 nodes hold 45 distinct strings (EPM-DEC-001-0104).

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A new module, system, with its own namespace, vocabulary, shapes, build and tests, referring to core processes by IRI. The systems are their own things, used by many processes, so they are not attributes of a process.
  2. A register, as for roles. register.csv maps each string to the systems it names. Strings merge on spelling only, as the role register did (EPM-DEC-001-0031): "Right Angle" becomes an alternative label of RightAngle. A string joined by "/" splits into the systems it names. "APS Planning DB" has no "/", so it stays one system, apart from APS and Planning DB. The four strings that name no system create no node and no link.
  3. Named, not defined. No source says what each system does, so none gets a definition. Each carries a note that its definition waits for its owners, and a shape rejects a definition. 2026-10-25: 15 systems gain a definition from their vendors' own descriptions, marked as such, or the decision owner's; 17 keep the note; the shape now accepts a definition with its source beside the process map, or a note (EPM-DEC-001-0099).
  4. Three kinds. Application (software, databases, dashboards, integrations), office tool and working document, so a question about systems can leave out e-mail and slides.
  5. A link per system and process. sys:supports links a system to each process whose systems field names it; read the other way, the process depends on the system. Competency question 6 is answerable.

Alternatives considered#

  • Split "APS Planning DB" into APS and Planning DB. Not chosen: the string has no separator, and the map uses "Planning DB" and "APS" separately elsewhere, so it may be a third thing.
  • Define each system from vendor material. Not chosen: the map names the systems as the business uses them, and a vendor's description says nothing about that use. 2026-10-25: the decision owner asked for it (item G); a vendor's description now defines what a product is, marked as the vendor's, and how the business uses it still comes from the process map (EPM-DEC-001-0099).
  • Leave office tools and documents out. Not chosen: the map names them as what the work uses; the kind lets a query leave them out.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json, systems fields; business_architecture/ontology/competency-questions.md, question 6; EPM-DEC-001-0031, 0040.

Consequences#

  • The regression stands at 19 answerable, 7 partial and 18 waiting.
  • The module is built and checked by scripts/check.sh; it is released only with the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: released as 1.0.0, with the decision owner's approval of the release plan (EPM-DEC-001-0077).

EPM-DEC-001-0056: The lane module: four office lanes, seven placed processes, nothing inherited#

From EPM-DEC-001-0056-lane-module.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsStep 7: lanes (competency question 9); Phase B of EPM-DEC-001-0052; the worklog's data gap that office_lanes.json is not linked by ID
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

business_architecture/business_process/office_lanes.json defines four lanes, keyed front_office, middle_office, back_office and operations, each with a name and a description. Seven process-map nodes, CM 1.2.1 to CM 1.2.7, have an officeLane whose value is one of those keys. The worklog notes that the lanes are not declared disjoint and that office_lanes.json is not linked by ID.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A small lane module. The four lanes are a SKOS code list, defined from office_lanes.json word for word. lane:officeLane links a core process to its lane.
  2. The link is by key. A node's officeLane value is the lane's key, which the build checks; the lane keeps the key as its skos:notation. That links the two files.
  3. Nothing is inherited. Only the seven nodes that name a lane have one. A child of CM 1.2.1 has none, as the RACI assignments inherit nothing from the hierarchy (EPM-DEC-001-0032).
  4. One lane per process. No node names two, and a shape keeps it so. The lanes are not declared disjoint as classes, because they are concepts, not classes.
  5. Thin lanes are a query. Front, middle and back office have one process each; operations has four. Competency question 9 is answerable.

Alternatives considered#

  • Lanes as a field of core. Not chosen: a lane is an overlay of the trading organization, and a core release would be needed for each change.
  • Inherit a lane down the tree. Not chosen: the map does not say a lane applies to the children.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/business_process/office_lanes.json; business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json, officeLane; EPM-DEC-001-0032.

Consequences#

  • The regression stands at 20 answerable, 7 partial and 17 waiting.
  • The module is built and checked by scripts/check.sh; it is released only with the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: released as 1.0.0, with the decision owner's approval of the release plan (EPM-DEC-001-0077).

EPM-DEC-001-0057: Benchmark series: one crack-spreads measure, five series, told apart by region, legs, RIN treatment and blend#

From EPM-DEC-001-0057-benchmark-series.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsCompetency questions 31 (when two published series are the same Named KPI) and 34 (which domain concepts a measure quantifies), which waited for the Market Data definitions; Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, its Step 11 item (every competency question answerable or parked by decision)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

The kpi module defines one measure, crack-spreads, a market indicator (EPM-DEC-001-0042). The planner's measure list names three cracks: "NYMEX 3-2-1", "USGC 3-2-1 MEH ex-RIN" and "Chicago WTI 3-2-1 ex-RIN". MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 7, defines Gulf Coast, Mid-Continent and West Coast crack spreads, each from a crude marker and two product benchmarks in a 3-2-1 ratio, and a blended crack spread weighted from the three by regional capacity, all without the cost of RINs (business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md, rows 10 and 11). CME Group defines the exchange's 3:2:1 from WTI, RBOB and NY Harbor ULSD futures. EPM-DEC-001-0046 puts benchmark identifiers and assessment conventions in Market Data.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028, from the evidence order: MPC first, then an industry source):

  1. A market-data module for benchmark series, benchmarks and refining regions, separate from kpi, because Market Data governs them (EPM-DEC-001-0046) and their identifiers change apart from the measure.
  2. One measure, several series. Each series is mkt:seriesOf the crack-spreads measure. Two series are the same series only when region, legs and RIN treatment are all the same; otherwise they are different series of one measure, not two measures. A blended series is built from other series.
  3. The planner's names resolve. "USGC 3-2-1 MEH ex-RIN" is an alternative label of MPC's Gulf Coast crack spread, and "Chicago WTI 3-2-1 ex-RIN" of its Mid-Continent crack spread, because MPC's wording gives the same crude, product markets, ratio and RIN treatment. "NYMEX 3-2-1" is a fifth series, defined from CME Group: it shares the ratio but uses exchange futures and RBOB. 2026-10-20: corrected by EPM-DEC-001-0066: by MPC Economics' definition, the planner's "USGC 3-2-1 MEH ex-RIN" and "Chicago WTI 3-2-1 ex-RIN" are its published series 43870 and 37438, which deduct the RVO cost; MPC's 10-K cracks do not, so they are different series.
  4. Recipes, not prices. A leg names a benchmark and its barrels; no price is stored (EPM-DEC-001-0005). The blended series' weights change by period, so they are a cited note, not a value.
  5. Regions are MPC's. Gulf Coast, Mid-Continent and West Coast, with the refineries MPC's Item 2 lists in each. A series or benchmark names its region where the source gives one. 2026-10-20: MPC Economics' product market regions (USGC, Chicago, Group 3, LA, PNW, SLC) are a second scheme beside these (EPM-DEC-001-0066).
  6. Questions 31 and 34 are answerable.

Alternatives considered#

  • Each series as its own measure. Not chosen: the classification put the cracks under one market indicator (EPM-DEC-001-0042), and competency question 30 treats a different view as the same measure.
  • Store current crack values. Not chosen: values stay in the systems (EPM-DEC-001-0005).

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The evidence order follows their instruction: "Use Marathon petroleum comapnies as the stand in company as primary and sue all other downstream oil and gas peer as secondary sources of refrecnes." (answers-2026-10-19-completion-search.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md, rows 10 and 11, and finding 3; business_architecture/reference/downstream-peers/benchmark-series-evidence.md (CME Group, "Trading Crack Spreads", 2024; "Introduction to Crack Spreads", 2017; "Fresh from the Trading Room", 2026-07-14); EPM-DEC-001-0005, 0042, 0046. 2026-10-20: checked against four U.S. refining peers' FY2025 Form 10-Ks (PBF Energy, Phillips 66, HF Sinclair, Valero), recorded in the same evidence file's peers section; they support the rule in item 2 and change no series.

Consequences#

  • The regression stands at 23 answerable, 7 partial and 14 waiting. 2026-10-20: the module now has 13 series, from MPC Economics' definition as the primary source (EPM-DEC-001-0066).
  • The module is built and checked by scripts/check.sh; it is released only with the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: released as 1.0.0, with the decision owner's approval of the release plan (EPM-DEC-001-0077).

EPM-DEC-001-0058: Placement kinds: only the producing process certifies; every other placement is context#

From EPM-DEC-001-0058-placement-kinds.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsCompetency questions 37 (what a concept or Named KPI sits on), 38 (whether a placement is certification or context) and 39 (what breaks if a meaning changes); Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, its Step 11 item (every competency question answerable or parked by decision) and the partial questions
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

Since the Step 7 overlays, twelve properties across six modules link measures, data products and their segments, series, systems and value-stream processes with process-map processes, capabilities, lanes and regions. Each property's own definition already says what it claims: kpi:informsProcess is "context for where the measure is used, not a certification that the process owns or computes it" (kpi/vocabulary.ttl; EPM-DEC-001-0043 calls the links "context, not certification"); a value-stream process that produces a data product "owns/publishes" it (the value-stream schema; EPM-DEC-001-0054). The graph did not say so in a form a query can read.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A placement-kind annotation. A small placement vocabulary defines plc:placementKind with two kinds, certification and context, and annotates each placing property with one.
  2. Only producing certifies. vs:produces and dp:producedBy are certification, because the schema makes the producing process the data product's owning process. The other ten properties are context, because their definitions say so: measure links, consuming, realizing, using a capability, segment anchors, systems, lanes and regions.
  3. Question 38 is answerable. No measure is placed by a certification link; the query that would list one returns none. Its second part, whether two processes with one name mean the same thing, is the same-word test of question 15: they are different IRIs with their own definitions.
  4. Questions 37 and 39 are answerable. With the value streams and market series, a data product or benchmark series sits on the value-stream processes and capabilities that produce, consume or measure it (37). A change of a measure's meaning reaches its series, the value-stream processes that link it and the processes it informs; a data product's reaches its consumers (39).

Alternatives considered#

  • Leave the kind in each property's prose definition. Not chosen: a query cannot read it.
  • Treat a measure's process links as certification. Not chosen: EPM-DEC-001-0043 made them context, and a KPI needs a recorded owner (EPM-DEC-001-0042).

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

EPM-DEC-001-0059: Source authority: each cited source in one of five evidence tiers; the best tier wins#

From EPM-DEC-001-0059-source-authority.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsCompetency question 44 (which definition is authoritative when two sources disagree, and how that is marked), partial since the regression started; Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, the partial questions (Q44: mark which source is authoritative, at Step 7 or 9)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

The release and the modules, ref-mpc included, cite 63 sources through dcterms:source. 2026-10-20: 64, with MPC Economics' crack spread definition in tier 1 (EPM-DEC-001-0066). 2026-10-21: 65, with MPLX's FY2025 Form 10-K in tier 2, as one of the MPC evidence files in reference/mpc/ (EPM-DEC-001-0071). In the graph the regression loads, 528 concepts cite more than one. AGENTS.md sets the evidence order: the process authority in business_architecture/ takes precedence; then MPC's filings and public materials; then U.S. refining peers' SEC filings; then downstream-specific industry sources; generic frameworks are support only. The ontology's README says APQC is "a consistency reference". The decision owner's instruction of 2026-10-19 repeats the MPC-first order.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. An authority module gives each cited source an auth:evidenceTier from a five-tier code list, each tier with a rank.
  2. Tier 1, the process authority: the process map, the value streams, the data product portfolio, the office lanes, the Step 3c definition workbook's approved rows, the decision owner's measure list, and the source author's recorded answers on GitHub.
  3. Tier 2, Marathon Petroleum: MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K as ref-mpc cites it, and the MPC evidence files in reference/mpc/.
  4. Tier 3, peers: the evidence files in reference/downstream-peers/ that rest on peers' 10-Ks.
  5. Tier 4, industry and regulatory sources: standards bodies, exchanges, registries, regulators and agencies (API, the Energy Institute, CCRO, CME Group, GLEIF, CFTC, EIA, EPA, FASB, FERC, FTC, IRS, ISDA, NAESB, OFAC, OSHA and the others in source-tiers.csv).
  6. Tier 5, generic frameworks: APQC's PCF, ASCM's CSCP manual, academic papers and the SKOS reference.
  7. The best tier wins. Where two sources of one concept disagree, a source in the lower-ranked tier is authoritative. Question 44 is answerable: 520 of the 528 multi-source concepts rest on the process authority and 8 on MPC.

Alternatives considered#

  • Mark one source per concept by hand. Not chosen: the tier follows from the evidence order the repository already sets, for every concept at once.
  • Put the tier in core. Not chosen: the sources span every module, and a core release would be needed for each new source.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The evidence order: "Use Marathon petroleum comapnies as the stand in company as primary and sue all other downstream oil and gas peer as secondary sources of refrecnes." (answers-2026-10-19-completion-search.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

AGENTS.md, Working rules; business_architecture/ontology/README.md, Standing conventions; business_architecture/ontology/authority/source-tiers.csv.

Consequences#

  • A new source needs a row in source-tiers.csv; the test fails until it has one.

EPM-DEC-001-0061: Rules for consumers: the catalog binding, and what an agent may answer and must refuse#

From EPM-DEC-001-0061-consumer-rules.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsCompetency questions 35 (how a catalog row points at a Named KPI, and what must exist first), 41 (what an agent may answer without reading a table or inventing a formula) and 42 (what it must refuse); Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, its Step 11 item (every competency question answerable or parked by decision)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

The questions say what the answers must cover. Question 42 names three refusals: "calculate a crack from legs, treat a report column as the KPI, answer 'who is the customer' without a role". Question 35 asks for a binding and its preconditions. The graph now defines measures with kinds (EPM-DEC-001-0042, 0043), data products (0053), series with recipes (0057), placement kinds (0058) and source tiers (0059). Values stay in the systems (EPM-DEC-001-0005). The session 5 handoff listed the agent rules among the decision owner's open questions; for this session the decision owner delegated everything except four named items, and the agent rules are not among them ("Everything else is delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028.").

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The catalog binding (35). con:publishesMeasure points from the data product that publishes a measure's values to the measure's IRI. A shape requires both ends to be defined first: the measure in the kpi module, the data product in the data-product module. No binding is recorded yet, because no source says which data product publishes which measure.
  2. What an agent may answer (41), from the graph alone: what a thing is (definition, names, kind); what a measure is not; where a thing sits and what the placement claims; what a series is built from; who is responsible for a process; which source says so and which one wins. 2026-10-19: a seventh, what a measure includes and excludes, added by EPM-DEC-001-0062.
  3. What an agent must refuse (42): computing or quoting a value; building a crack from its legs; treating a report column or a KPI candidate as a KPI; naming the customer without a role; presenting a context placement as ownership. Building a crack from its legs, treating a report column as the KPI, and naming the customer without a role come from the question's own words. Computing or quoting a value follows from EPM-DEC-001-0005, treating a KPI candidate as a KPI from EPM-DEC-001-0042, and presenting a context placement as ownership from EPM-DEC-001-0058. 2026-10-25: amended for an agent that quotes a measure's value taken through the metric view the graph binds to that measure, naming the view: that quote is allowed; computing a value from the graph, building a crack from its legs and quoting from an unbound source stay refused (EPM-DEC-001-0097, item 6). The consumer module's rule rule:no-values changes with the next integration release.
  4. Each rule names the graph terms it relies on, so a test can check that they exist and that each rule names its own terms. The customer-role refusal relies on CM 1.3.7.1.1, whose definition keeps Sold-To, Bill-To, Payer and Ship-To apart, until the Customer module brings the roles (EPM-DEC-001-0004). 2026-10-21: the Customer work resumed (EPM-DEC-001-0067); the roles come with the party module. The rule now also relies on the party module's party:roleType (EPM-DEC-001-0068). Ship-To is a site, not a role, in that module (EPM-DEC-001-0068, item 5); the core concept's wording still names it with the roles.

Alternatives considered#

  • Park 41 and 42 for the decision owner. Not chosen: the decision owner delegated everything outside four named items, and the questions themselves give the refusals. They can reverse or extend the rules.
  • Record bindings for the measures MPC publishes. Not chosen: no source names the data product that holds each measure's values.

The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

EPM-DEC-001-0062: Measure populations from MPC's own wording; the rest parked where no source states one#

From EPM-DEC-001-0062-measure-population.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsCompetency question 28 (what population is in a Named KPI, and what is out, in business words); Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, its Step 11 item (every competency question answerable or parked by decision)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

The kpi module holds each measure's definition, grain and basis, but no statement of what it includes and excludes. MPC's filings state it for the measures MPC defines: the regional R&M margin per barrel leaves out inter-refinery transfer volumes; refining operating costs exclude planned turnaround and depreciation and amortization; the crack spreads do not reflect the market cost of RINs (business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md, rows 7, 8, 10, 14, 15, 16, 19 and 20). The other 33 measures have no stated population: 31 from the planning team's list (crack spreads is on that list too, but MPC states what its benchmark excludes), the R&M Margin Indicator, whose formula MPC publishes nowhere, and turnaround lost margin, from the industry evidence. The kpi package is released (1.0.1), so a change to its tables needs a new release.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A small population module states pop:includes and pop:excludes on the kpi module's measure IRIs, with the evidence row, without touching the released kpi tables.
  2. Eight measures, from MPC's wording: R&M margin per barrel, margin capture, refining operating cost per barrel, distribution cost per barrel, turnaround cost per barrel, crack spreads, relative refining margin by region, and relative adjusted EBITDA per barrel.
  3. The other 33 measures' populations are parked. No source states them, and a population written from the definition would be a guess. Each can be added when its owner states it.
  4. A seventh may-answer rule joins the consumer rules (EPM-DEC-001-0061): what a measure includes and excludes, where its source says so, relying on pop:includes, pop:excludes and pop:populationEvidence.
  5. Question 28 is answerable for the measures whose source states a population, with the rest parked by this record. The next kpi release can take the statements into the kpi module. 2026-10-22: kpi 1.1.0 carries them, with their pop: IRIs (EPM-DEC-001-0079).

Alternatives considered#

  • Add a population column to the kpi tables. Not chosen now: the released package's check rebuilds from those tables, so the change belongs in the next kpi release, which needs the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: under the module policy (worklog, "Who approves a release"; EPM-DEC-001-0037), a minor or patch release is the module owner's and a major the decision owner's; Claude asked the decision owner before every release issued on 2026-10-22 (EPM-DEC-001-0074, and 0076 to 0080). The populations shipped in kpi 1.1.0, a minor (EPM-DEC-001-0079).
  • Write populations for the other measures from their definitions. Not chosen, for the reason in item 3.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md, rows 7, 8, 10, 14, 15, 16, 19 and 20, and finding 5; EPM-DEC-001-0040, 0043.

Consequences#

  • The regression stands at 34 answerable, 1 partial (Q7) and 9 waiting. Every waiting question waits on the decision owner: question 17 (EPM-DEC-001-0045) and questions 19 to 26, the Customer module (EPM-DEC-001-0004). 2026-10-21: the decision owner's customer information arrived, so questions 19 to 26 now wait on the plan's pull request 5, which answers them after the party module and its data (pull requests 2 to 4; EPM-DEC-001-0067, item 13), not on the decision owner. 2026-10-21: the plan's pull request 5 made Q19 to Q26 answerable: 42 answerable, 1 partial (Q7), 1 waiting (Q17) (EPM-DEC-001-0071). 2026-10-23: the decision owner parked Q17 until the ontology is first shared with business consumers: 43 answerable, 0 partial, 0 waiting and 1 parked (EPM-DEC-001-0086).

EPM-DEC-001-0063: The R2 backlog: each item out of scope for now, with the trigger that brings it back#

From EPM-DEC-001-0063-r2-backlog.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsWorklog row 3d-d, the R2 backlog "open by design" since 2026-09-22; Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, the 3d-d backlog
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

The R2 backlog (worklog row 3d-d and its 2026-09-22 entry) holds four items: (1) an operating-execution layer (unit-operation scheduling, line-ups, blend execution, process control, operating procedures); (2) maintenance and turnaround ownership; (3) an R2 Refinery Vocabulary and Operating-Lifecycle package (turnaround against normal and emergency shutdown, startup, plan against schedule); (4) a review of where Energy & Utility Management sits, triggered by an R2 refinery energy, utilities and environmental performance scope decision. Since then MPC-P02b (EPM-DEC-001-0047, PR #292) gave Refining its ID and filled RF 1.2 Refinery Asset Reliability and Turnaround Coordination with five steps that coordinate the commercial and planning side of turnarounds. RF 1.2's own description and steps RF 1.2.1 and RF 1.2.3 keep the maintenance work itself outside the process. The process map is the commercial, planning and logistics map of the downstream business; the repository's competency questions ask nothing about unit operations.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Operating-execution layer: out, for now. No competency question needs it and the map has no such processes. Trigger: the process map gains an operating-execution L2 by an accepted proposal, or the decision owner asks for it.
  2. Maintenance and turnaround ownership: answered for the map's scope. RF 1.2 owns the commercial and planning side of turnarounds (scope, schedule against the plan, commercial impacts, restart assumptions, cost review), as P02b set out. Who owns maintenance strategy, inspection and turnaround execution is outside the map and stays with the refinery maintenance function. Trigger: the operating-execution layer comes in (item 1). The next core release should review RF 1.2's Candidate status, since its open ownership question is answered for the map's scope. 2026-10-22: reviewed in core 2.2.0: RF 1.2 is no longer a Candidate node (EPM-DEC-001-0076).
  3. R2 Refinery Vocabulary and Operating-Lifecycle package: out, for now. Its distinctions belong to the operating-execution layer; the turnaround steps already separate the planned turnaround they coordinate from the maintenance work. Trigger: item 1's trigger.
  4. Energy & Utility Management stays where it is (CM 1.1.7.3, under CM 1.1.7 Refinery Production Planning and Scheduling). Its review trigger, a scope decision on refinery energy, utilities and environmental performance, has not occurred. Trigger: unchanged.
  5. PTC-001-B stays a hold by design (the decision owner, 2026-09-22), as worklog row 3d-b records.

Alternatives considered#

  • Model the operating-execution layer now. Not chosen: no source in the repository describes those processes, and no question asks about them.
  • Leave the backlog open. Not chosen: the ontology is complete only when each open item is decided in or out (EPM-DEC-001-0052, Phase E).

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/ontology/step4/playbook/ontology-worklog.md, row 3d-d and the R2 backlog entry of 2026-09-22; business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json, RF 1.2 and its five steps; EPM-DEC-001-0047 (P02b).

Consequences#

  • Worklog row 3d-d is decided: every item is out with a trigger, or answered for the map's scope.

EPM-DEC-001-0064: External mappings: DCAT and GLEIF mapped; PIDX and OEO wait for product concepts; IOF Supply Chain parked#

From EPM-DEC-001-0064-external-mappings-status.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
QuestionsThe external mappings EPM-DEC-001-0023 assigned to Step 7, and its instruction to check PIDX's access terms before the Customer work; Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, the external mappings (EPM-DEC-001-0023)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0023 says to map public ontologies with SKOS mapping properties and not import them: GLEIF at Step 5, PIDX and the Open Energy Ontology (OEO) at Step 7 and on the Customer product-line facet, and IOF Supply Chain at Step 7 if fuels logistics comes in. GLEIF is mapped in ref-mpc (EPM-DEC-001-0033). DCAT is mapped in the data-product module (EPM-DEC-001-0053). MPC-P01 brought fuels logistics in as MS 1 (EPM-DEC-001-0047). PIDX publishes downstream product codes and terminal codes (pidx.org/standards, retrieved 2026-10-19). OEO covers energy carriers and fuels. The ontology now holds processes, roles, measures, data products, value streams, systems, lanes and benchmark series, but no product class: a benchmark is a price at a market, not a product. 2026-10-21: see item 2's note: party:Product now exists, without values (EPM-DEC-001-0068).

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. PIDX's access terms are checked. PIDX says it develops "Royalty free standards licensed to all on an open basis pursuant to the End User License Agreement" and makes its standards "available to all interested parties in the petroleum industry and it’s a trading community free of charge" (pidx.org/about, retrieved 2026-10-19; the agreement is the "Standard End User License Agreement (V110329)", pidx.org/about/policies-and-procedures). The agreement (PIDX-EULA-v110329, linked from https://pidx.org/license/, retrieved 2026-10-20) licenses use of a Standard "without modification for any Permitted Use" (section 2(b)(i)); copying needs attribution to PIDX and its legends (2(b)(ii)); and it grants no licence "to modify any Standard or create derivative works based on the Standard" outside a Standards Development Activity (2(c)(v)), nor to develop competing standards (2(c)(iv)). A mapping that points to PIDX codes by identifier uses the Standard without changing it. It must not copy PIDX code lists into the ontology without the legends, or alter them.
  2. PIDX and OEO wait for product concepts. Both map products and fuels, and the ontology has no product concept to map yet. Trigger: the Customer module's product-line facet (Phase C) or another product dimension. 2026-10-21: that facet is in the party module, which owns the Customer domain's concepts (EPM-DEC-001-0067, What follows, item 13), and its values are not yet defined. The party module declares a party:Product class, which stands in for a Product domain this repository does not model yet; it has no values, so PIDX and OEO still wait for product values (EPM-DEC-001-0068). The mapping then uses skos:closeMatch or skos:exactMatch to PIDX downstream product codes and OEO's fuel classes, cited with their versions. 2026-10-23: met: party 1.1.0 maps its products to OEO and records their PIDX families (EPM-DEC-001-0092).
  3. IOF Supply Chain is parked. MS 1 and the logistics processes are business-process concepts; IOF's supply-chain classes are BFO-based types, logistics process types among them along with facilities, shipments and roles (IOF Supply Chain Ontology, version IRI https://spec.industrialontologies.org/ontology/202603/supplychain/SupplyChain/, maturity Released, retrieved 2026-10-20). A SKOS match between a business process and one of them would claim a meaning match that no source checks. Trigger: a concept layer for logistics assets or movements, or a consumer that needs IOF terms.
  4. The other EPM-DEC-001-0023 mappings are done: GLEIF at Step 5, by identifier with an rdfs:seeAlso link to each GLEIF record (EPM-DEC-001-0033), and DCAT in the data-product module (EPM-DEC-001-0053).

Alternatives considered#

  • Map MPC's benchmark products (CBOB, ULSD, CARBOB) to PIDX codes now. Not chosen: they are price benchmarks at named markets, not product definitions, and a code match would say more than the source.
  • Import IOF Supply Chain. Not chosen: EPM-DEC-001-0023 says map, do not import.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

PIDX, "About Us", https://pidx.org/about/, and "Standards", https://pidx.org/standards/, both retrieved 2026-10-19; PIDX, "Standard End User License Agreement (V110329)", https://pidx.org/license/, retrieved 2026-10-20; Industrial Ontologies Foundry, Supply Chain Ontology, SupplyChain.rdf in https://github.com/iofoundry/ontology/tree/master/supplychain (version IRI 202603, maturity Released; retrieved 2026-10-20); EPM-DEC-001-0023, 0033, 0047, 0053.

Consequences#

  • The Customer module's product-line facet (Phase C), or another product dimension, brings the PIDX and OEO mappings. 2026-10-21: that facet is in the party module (EPM-DEC-001-0067, What follows, item 13). 2026-10-23: party 1.1.0 maps the products to OEO and records PIDX family letters as plain identifiers, since PIDX publishes no IRIs (EPM-DEC-001-0092).
  • IOF Supply Chain waits for a concept layer for logistics assets or movements, or a consumer that needs IOF terms.
  • 2026-10-22 (Phase E): both triggers re-checked on every released package under releases/, and neither is met. party:Product has no values in party 1.0.0. Three customer-system attributes describe products (RightAngle's commodities and product, Salesforce's product description), and RightAngle holds a "PIDX Supplier ID", which identifies a supplier, not a product, so it brings no PIDX product mapping. As concepts, logistics appears only as processes, capabilities, value-stream processes and data products, with no asset or movement layer, and no consumer has asked for IOF terms. A product code list, for example from MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K, would meet the PIDX and OEO trigger. In the party module it would need a minor release, which the module policy gives to the module owner; in a new Product domain module (EPM-DEC-001-0068), a first release, 1.0.0, a major that needs the decision owner's approval (worklog, "Who approves a release"; EPM-DEC-001-0037). Either way, Claude asks the decision owner before building it, as for every release issued on 2026-10-22 (EPM-DEC-001-0074, and 0076 to 0080).
  • 2026-10-23: the decision owner approved product values from MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K in party 1.1.0, mapped to PIDX and OEO where a match holds (EPM-DEC-001-0088).
  • 2026-10-23, later: the trigger is met. party 1.1.0 holds the products, maps them to OEO v2.14.0 with SKOS, and records their PIDX product families as plain identifiers, because PIDX publishes no IRIs (EPM-DEC-001-0092).

EPM-DEC-001-0065: Step 9 gate: every module's shapes run together on the whole graph, with zero violations#

From EPM-DEC-001-0065-step9-all-module-gate.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-19
