SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-19

Context#

Each module's tests validate its own data with its own shapes and the few graphs it points to. No test ran every shape file on the whole graph, so a shape that reaches too far, or a value one module's shape forbids in another module, went unseen. The first combined run found two such defects: the lane module's shape targeted every subject of skos:inScheme, so it demanded a notation and a definition of every concept in the graph (143 violations); and the consumer rules gave dcterms:source a plain-text value, which core's shapes forbid (22 violations).

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. One gate for the whole graph. validation/test_all_module_shapes.py loads the graph the regression loads and runs the shape files of core 2.1.0 and of the organization, ref-mpc, kpi, data-product, value-stream, system, lane, market-data and consumer modules, plus the controlled-value shapes. It must find zero violations. scripts/check.sh runs it.
  2. A negative test adds a cross-module break (a value-stream process producing an undefined data product) and must fail.
  3. Both defects are fixed at their source: the lane shapes now target a lane:OfficeLane class (EPM-DEC-001-0056's module), and the consumer rules cite their questions with dcterms:bibliographicCitation (EPM-DEC-001-0061's module).

Alternatives considered#

  • Keep per-module validation only. Not chosen: it is how both defects passed.

The decision owner's recorded words#

None for these choices. The delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/ontology/validation/test_all_module_shapes.py; EPM-DEC-001-0035, 0044, 0056, 0061.

Consequences#

  • Step 9 has zero violations on the whole graph. The new modules' shapes join their release packages when each is released.

EPM-DEC-001-0066: The crack spread's primary source is MPC Economics' definition; the market-data module takes its published series#

From EPM-DEC-001-0066-crack-spread-primary-source.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-20, for the source order and scope; the modelling delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-20
QuestionsCompetency questions 31 and 34; the sources of the crack-spreads measure's series; corrects EPM-DEC-001-0057 item 3; Phase E of EPM-DEC-001-0052, its Step 11 item (every competency question answerable or parked by decision)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-20

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0057 built the market-data module from MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 7, and from CME Group. The U.S. refining peers' FY2025 Form 10-Ks were read afterwards, on 2026-10-20, and recorded in reference/downstream-peers/benchmark-series-evidence.md. The same day the decision owner supplied MPC Economics' "3-2-1 Crack Spread KPI Definition", now saved without its governance section and implementation paths in business_architecture/reference/internal/crack-spread-kpi-definition.md.

The definition names six primary published regional 3-2-1 series (USGC, Chicago, Group 3 (Tulsa), LA, PNW and SLC), each with its MPR Price Master ID; the NYMEX 3-2-1; and two published results of an MPC-weighted 3-2-1. It defines two treatments of the Renewable Volume Obligation (RVO): Ex-RVO, whose formula deducts the RVO cost, and Inc-RVO, whose formula does not. It says "'Ex-RVO' and 'ex-RIN' mean the same thing".

Decision#

The decision owner's decision: "Below is actually the source you should consider primary spurce for Crack Spread," and "follow by MPC publickly available information, follow by Peer, follow by additional high quality references. Capture the nuances and completementary information as nedded". Then: "Dont add the governance portion of the text". Asked how much to keep and whether to change the module, they chose "Definition + series IDs (Recommended)" and "Update the market-data module (Recommended)" (answers-2026-10-20-crack-spread-source.md).

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Source order for the crack spread: MPC Economics' definition first (tier 1, process authority, as the decision owner's measure list is); then MPC's public filings; then U.S. refining peers' SEC filings; then industry sources such as CME Group. A later source adds what an earlier one leaves out; where they differ, the earlier one wins (EPM-DEC-001-0059).
  2. The published series come in. The six primary regional ex-RIN series (Price Master IDs 43870, 37438, 37439, 36855, 37443 and 37471), the NYMEX 3-2-1 with its ID 39998, and the two MPC-weighted blends (37464 and 42023) are series of the crack-spreads measure. Each carries its Price Master ID as mkt:priceMasterId. Giving ID 39998 to the CME-sourced NYMEX series reads section 4.3's "NYMEX 3-2-1 industry benchmark" as that exchange series; that reading is this record's.
  3. RIN treatment is told apart, and 0057 item 3 is corrected. By the definition's formulas, an ex-RIN (Ex-RVO) series deducts the RVO cost; MPC's 10-K cracks "do not reflect the market cost of RINs", as the Inc-RVO formula does not. So the planner's "USGC 3-2-1 MEH ex-RIN" and "Chicago WTI 3-2-1 ex-RIN" are MPC Economics' series 43870 and 37438, not MPC's 10-K Gulf Coast and Mid-Continent cracks: same crude and product markets, opposite RIN treatment, so two series by 0057's item 2. The 10-K cracks stay as MPC's public series. mkt:excludesRINCost is false for an ex-RIN series, and its scope note says the labels run opposite to the property's name.
  4. Recipes as the definition gives them. For USGC, Chicago, Group 3 and LA, the legs are the input prices of section 4.2: three barrels of crude, two of gasoline, one of ULSD, and the RVO cost (ID 13056, a new benchmark kind, compliance cost) on each of the three product barrels. PNW and SLC are marked mkt:legsUnstated, because the definition does not list their inputs. Two pairings are this record's reading, not the definition's words: the input table's Gulf Coast row belongs to the USGC series, and the crude inputs 14133, 747 and 4055 are named MEH, WTI and ANS after the series that use them. Each ex-RIN regional series carries the Ex-RVO formula and the averaging rule (daily published values, monthly mean) as a scope note. Prices stay out (EPM-DEC-001-0005).
  5. Market regions are a separate scheme. USGC, Chicago, Group 3 (Tulsa), LA, PNW and SLC are price markets, kept apart from MPC's three refining regions.
  6. The MPC-weighted blends weight USGC, Chicago and LA 0.40, 0.40 and 0.20 before 5 May 2024 and 0.42, 0.40 and 0.18 after, as a scope note. MPC's 10-Q gives the same 40/42/18 split for 2025 and 2026. The definition does not say how 37464 and 42023 differ, and the record does not guess.
  7. The NYMEX diesel leg is CME's NY Harbor ULSD futures contract (ticker HO); the definition calls it NYMEX Heating Oil, kept as a note on the benchmark.
  8. Left out for now: the NYMEX leg cracks (44934, 44935), the 6-3-2-1 cracks, the regional variants (NYH, El Paso, San Francisco, USWC), and any published Inc-RVO series, which the definition names without IDs or recipes. Each can be added from the same source.

Alternatives considered#

  • Keep MPC's 10-K as the primary source. Not chosen: the decision owner set the order.
  • Model the 10-K cracks and the Economics series as one series each region. Not chosen: their RIN treatment differs, and 0057's own rule makes them two series.
  • Leave PNW and SLC out until their inputs are known. Not chosen: the definition names them as two of the six primary series.

The decision owner's recorded words#

In the Claude Code session on 2026-10-20 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-20-crack-spread-source.md): "Below is actually the source you should consider primary spurce for Crack Spread,"; "follow by MPC publickly available information, follow by Peer, follow by additional high quality references. Capture the nuances and completementary information as nedded"; "Dont add the governance portion of the text"; and their choices "Definition + series IDs (Recommended)" and "Update the market-data module (Recommended)". The modelling choices above are Claude's, under the delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

business_architecture/reference/internal/crack-spread-kpi-definition.md (MPC Economics, sections 2 to 4 and the rules of section 5); business_architecture/reference/mpc/mpc-performance-measures-evidence.md, rows 10 and 11; MPC Form 10-Q for the quarter ended 2026-03-31, https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1510295/000151029526000042/mpc-20260331.htm, retrieved 2026-10-20 ("Blended 3-2-1 Mid-Continent/USGC/West Coast crack spread is 40/42/18 percent in 2026 and 2025"; row 10 of the MPC evidence cites the same weights from the Q2 2026 Form 10-Q, MPC-S03); business_architecture/reference/downstream-peers/benchmark-series-evidence.md (CME Group; the peers' FY2025 Form 10-Ks); EPM-DEC-001-0005, 0057, 0059.

Consequences#

  • The market-data module has 13 series, 24 benchmarks and 9 regions (3 refining, 6 market). It is built and checked by scripts/check.sh; it is released only with the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: released as 1.0.0, with the decision owner's approval of the release plan (EPM-DEC-001-0077).
  • The source tiers hold 64 sources, 10 in tier 1. 2026-10-21: 65, with MPLX's FY2025 Form 10-K in tier 2 (EPM-DEC-001-0071).
  • The regression stands at 34 answerable, 1 partial and 9 waiting; questions 31 and 34 stay answerable. 2026-10-21: the party module made Q19 to Q26 answerable: 42 answerable, 1 partial (Q7), 1 waiting (Q17) (EPM-DEC-001-0071). 2026-10-23: the decision owner parked Q17 until the ontology is first shared with business consumers: 43 answerable, 0 partial, 0 waiting and 1 parked (EPM-DEC-001-0086).

EPM-DEC-001-0067: The Customer domain resumes on the decision owner's information: its sources, its scope and the plan#

From EPM-DEC-001-0067-customer-domain-sources-scope.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-20 to 2026-10-21, for the sources, the scope, the five accepted recommendations, the two new roles, the visibility and the plan; the readings marked as Claude's delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-21
QuestionsCompetency questions 19 to 26; EPM-DEC-001-0004, whose wait for the decision owner's details this record ends; Phase C of EPM-DEC-001-0052; MPC-P06's channel dimension (EPM-DEC-001-0047, What follows, item 5)
SourceClaude Code session, 2026-10-20 to 2026-10-21

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0004 held the Customer domain until the decision owner shared their customer information. EPM-DEC-001-0052 set its scope as Phase C: a conceptual data model of Customer, the customer lifecycle from onboarding as proposed process-map nodes, and the channel dimension of MPC-P06. 2026-10-21: the lifecycle's stages map to existing processes; only MPC-P10.1 would add a node, if the decision owner accepts it (EPM-DEC-001-0070). 2026-10-23: the decision owner accepted MPC-P10 (EPM-DEC-001-0087); core 2.3.0 adds CM 1.3.7.1.7 (EPM-DEC-001-0089).

In this session the decision owner sent seven inputs, one at a time:

  1. a diagram of how customer data is ingested, from the first request through credit and compliance approval to the systems that receive the customer;
  2. a diagram of the applications the customer passes through, in three columns: create, enrich and consume;
  3. a later, dated version of the second diagram's core path;
  4. a diagram of the customer lifecycle in six stages;
  5. a table of six core systems, each with the attributes that define a customer there (Create) and the attributes that enrich it afterwards (Enrich);
  6. a conceptual data model of Customer: entities in four zones, their relationships, ten customer classes, and a pane showing where the customer shows up in systems;
  7. an external research report on the Customer domain in downstream oil and gas.

None of these seven inputs is in the repository, by the decision owner's rule below. Input 7 is a report prepared on 2026-10-13. It is not the earlier customer report tracked at the repository root since 2026-08-21 (Enterprise_Customer_Domain_Downstream_Oil_Gas_Report.pdf, with Customer Domain in Downstream Oil & Gas — Modeling Guide.html, commit e96b7d0). Those two files predate this session, are not among the seven inputs, and the party module does not cite them.

Claude asked questions about each input as it arrived. The decision owner's answers are saved in answers-2026-10-21-customer.md, 19 blocks. Its header comment, in Claude's words, records the question each block answers, including the six recommendations block 14 answers.

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions (their words, with the block of answers-2026-10-21-customer.md each is in; the question each answers is in that file's header comment):

  1. "Customer information" (block 1).
  2. "decide once you've seen my data. I will share them one at a time to process" (block 2).
  3. "all of the files and images should stay out of the repo, by the way." (block 4).
  4. "yes, derived definitions can go in the repo. We will use repo information, MPC publickly published information for this. We can use Peer data to guide and resolve ambuiguity but peer data should not go into the repo at this point" (block 5), then "Actually Peer comparison is fine to use as guidance. This rule is specifically for Customer domainhere is the next one" (block 6).
  5. "Now here are the core system and attributes that defines a customer in each system. And also attributes used to enrich customer in each system. These two categories of attrobutes must be captured" (block 7).
  6. Block 8, in full:
    1. correct them
    2. dedup them
    3. use external MPC search and Peer to resolve these ones.
  7. "Now make sure you have all of this captured to represent both the life cycle and data flow of Customer ." and "I will share the conceptual data model for customer next. Which we want to capture separately." (block 9).
  8. Block 11, in full:
    1. they are in scope
    2. scoped to product connects to product from agreement, customer account connects to site throuhg "supplied from". I did not see "authorizes brand at site
    3. correct. L&S is probably logistic and storage. can you confrim that and SDP through an external search when you are ready.
  9. "let us just call it SDP for now?" (block 13).
  10. Block 14, in full:
    1. nothing on the conceptual is out of scope I am okay with the rest. and it is EIS bring me the plan
  11. "yes, keep it private, add both roles, go ahead" (block 15).
  12. 2026-10-21, later: "feel free to turn the repo until goal is achieved." (block 18), then "done, repo is public. carry on" (block 19).

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

These items are Claude's wording and Claude's reading of the decision owner's answers. Items 1 to 5 restate recommendations 2 to 6 of block 14's question (the answers file's header comment), which the decision owner accepted with "I am okay with the rest"; they rejected recommendation 1 with "nothing on the conceptual is out of scope".

  1. Direct dealers sit under a Fuel Supply agreement. MPC's filings describe "long-term supply contracts with direct dealers who operate locations mainly under the ARCO® brand" (reference/mpc/customer-classification-evidence.md, C2). MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K calls the brand its own ("Our Marathon and ARCO trademarks are material to the conduct of our refining and marketing operations", Item 1, Trademarks, Patents and Licenses), and a full-text search of MPC's 10-K filings on EDGAR on 2026-10-21 found no "brand license". Their brand reaches the site through a Site Brand Assignment.
  2. Four classes from the repository's MPC customer classification are to be added, in the party module's pull request (reference/mpc/customer-classification-evidence.md: C4 and C6 from MPC's Form 10-K, C5 from MPC's Unbranded Marketing page, and C8, which the decision owner added on 2026-09-29 for the crude oil revenue MPC reports): independent retailer (C4), unbranded customer (C5), spot market buyer (C6) and crude oil customer (C8). With the ten classes Claude read in the decision owner's model, that makes 14. 2026-10-21: added (EPM-DEC-001-0068, item 7).
  3. The exchange or trading counterparty stays a customer class. The decision owner's model says it becomes a customer only when a revenue-recognized relationship exists.
  4. "Authorizes brand at site" points to Site Brand Assignment, not to Site. The model defines a site brand assignment as the link of a site to a brand under an agreement. The decision owner wrote that they did not see this relationship (block 11). Claude then showed where the image draws it. The decision owner has not answered on it since, so this item rests on their acceptance of recommendation 5 in block 14, and stays open to them.
  5. MPLX's filings count as MPC's public information. MPC consolidates MPLX.
  6. Claude's reading of the decision owner's answers:
    • Block 2 answers whether to accept two candidates that four peers' filings suggested: a delivery-mode facet and a brand-licensing relationship.
    • Block 8's three answers are, in their numbering: correct the misspelled labels (3), remove repeated attributes (1), and search for the meaning of six internal codes (2).
    • Block 11's third answer confirms Claude's reading of COCO, CODO and DODO (company-owned company-operated, company-owned dealer-operated, dealer-owned dealer-operated).
    • "and it is EIS" (block 14) gives the spelling of a landscape system's name.
  7. Sources for the Customer domain, by decision 4 and item 5: derived definitions go into the repository; their sources are repository information and MPC's public information (decision 4), MPLX's filings included (item 5); peer data and the external report guide choices and do not go into the repository (the report, as one of the seven inputs, by decision 3). Elsewhere in the repository, peer comparison stays allowed as guidance and as evidence, as before.
  8. L&S is Logistics and Storage. MPLX's FY2023 Form 10-K, filed 2024-02-28 (accession 0001552000-24-000007), names "Logistics and Storage (“L&S”)" as one of its two segments. MPLX's FY2024 Form 10-K (accession 0001552000-25-000012, Item 1, Our Operating Segments) says: "The primary changes were to rename the Logistics and Storage segment to the Crude Oil and Products Logistics segment and to rename the Gathering and Processing segment to the Natural Gas and NGL Services segment. In addition, we reclassified certain equity method investments serving natural gas and NGL customers from Crude Oil and Products Logistics to Natural Gas and NGL Services." So L&S is close to, not the same as, today's Crude Oil and Products Logistics: some natural gas and NGL investments moved out (reference/mpc/mplx-fy2025-10k-fact-sheet.md has the current names).
  9. SDP stays a label without an expansion. No MPC or MPLX filing contains it, by EDGAR full-text search on 2026-10-21.
  10. The two peer candidates are covered by the decision owner's own model, so neither is added separately. In the model, a customer account carries a delivery tier, and a brand is owned by or licensed to a party through a Brand License agreement.
  11. Two roles are to be added, in the party module's pull request, by "add both roles": Position Holder and Loading-Authorized Party. The Loading-Authorized Party answers competency question 22. The Position Holder is the party whose product a terminal lifting draws on, which the model's terminal-rack class names. The model's own role list, as Claude read it, has twelve roles. 2026-10-21: added (EPM-DEC-001-0068, item 4).
  12. The repository stays private for the whole Customer work, by "keep it private". This narrows the standing rule of EPM-DEC-001-0050's amendment of 2026-10-19 (public while work needs it) for the Customer work. EPM-DEC-001-0040 item 3 lets internal system names into the repository, and its amendment notes that they are readable while the repository is public; keeping it private kept the Customer domain's internal system and field names unreadable from outside. While it was private, GitHub did not enforce ruleset 25440735, so each Customer pull request was to merge through merge-on-verdict.sh, which waits for a MERGE verdict on the current head and for checks to pass. 2026-10-21, later: by decision 12, the decision owner allowed the repository to be public until the goal is achieved, and made it public. The occasion, in Claude's account: GitHub Actions had stopped starting the checks job on the private repository ("The job was not started because recent account payments have failed or your spending limit needs to be increased", run 39561985243). While the repository is public, ruleset 25440735 is enforced and the internal names are readable. It goes back to private at the end of the session or the goal, as EPM-DEC-001-0050's amendment says.
  13. The plan, in five pull requests:
    1. this record;
    2. the party module, the conceptual model. It is named party because the 2026-09-18 module policy makes party the sole concept-owning module for the Customer domain, with no customer module (ontology-worklog.md, step 1 and the Modeling group; ontology-playbook.md, Modules);
    3. the systems, the data flow and the attributes;
    4. the lifecycle, with a process-map proposal for the stages the map lacks;
    5. competency questions 19 to 26.
    • MPC-P06's channel dimension is applied in pull request 2. 2026-10-21: done there, as the values of the dimension (EPM-DEC-001-0068, item 8).
    • The process map changes only after the decision owner approves the proposal.
    • party 1.0.0 is released only with their approval. 2026-10-22: released with it (EPM-DEC-001-0074).
  14. Still open from EPM-DEC-001-0004: the lubricants and marine product-line evidence. None of the inputs covers it, so it stays open. The party module's pull request is planned to settle or park it. 2026-10-21: it parks it (EPM-DEC-001-0068, item 14).

Alternatives considered#

  • Commit the decision owner's diagrams and table as reference files. Not chosen: they asked for the files to stay out.
  • Put peers' filings into the repository for the Customer domain, as customer-classification-evidence.md did. Not chosen: the decision owner wrote that peer data should not go into the repo, for the Customer domain.
  • Keep Agreement, Site, Site Brand Assignment and Party Transaction Association out of scope, as Claude first recommended. Not chosen: the decision owner put every entity in scope.
  • Frame direct dealers with a Brand License agreement, as the conceptual model's class note does. Not chosen: MPC's filings describe supply contracts with direct dealers, call the ARCO trademark MPC's own, and do not mention a brand licence (item 1).
  • Make the repository public while the pull requests merge, as EPM-DEC-001-0050's amendment allows, so the ruleset is enforced. Not chosen at first: the decision owner kept it private. 2026-10-21, later: chosen after all, by decision 12.

EPM-DEC-001-0068: The party module: the decision owner's conceptual model as classes, properties and code lists#

From EPM-DEC-001-0068-customer-module.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-21
QuestionsCompetency questions 19 to 26 (answered in pull request 5 of EPM-DEC-001-0067's plan); MPC-P06's channel dimension (EPM-DEC-001-0047, What follows, item 5); EPM-DEC-001-0004's lubricants and marine evidence
SourceClaude Code night run, 2026-10-21, pull request 2 of EPM-DEC-001-0067's plan

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0067 records the decision owner's customer information, their rules for it and a five-PR plan. Their conceptual data model of Customer has four zones:

  • identity: party, external identifier, address, contact point;
  • customer anatomy: party role, party relationship, customer account, customer class, line of business, brand, agreement, site brand assignment;
  • operational context: party transaction association, product, site;
  • customer classes: ten classes in four groups.

Every entity is in scope ("nothing on the conceptual is out of scope", EPM-DEC-001-0067). The model is not in the repository. This record turns it into a module and records the choices the model leaves open.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A new party module, namespace https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/modules/party/, prefix party:, in business_architecture/ontology/party/. It is named party because the 2026-09-18 module policy makes party the sole concept-owning module for the Customer domain, with no customer module (ontology-worklog.md, step 1 and the Modeling group; ontology-playbook.md, Modules). It holds definitions only: no real customer, account or site (EPM-DEC-001-0005). It depends on no other module. Its five example organizations, in examples.ttl, are invented test fixtures and are not loaded with the module.
  2. Fifteen entity classes, one per entity, plus Person and Organization: 17 classes in all. Each definition is Claude's wording of the decision owner's entity. Party has two subclasses, Person and Organization, which are disjoint. They are the only subclasses of party. Product stands in for a Product domain that this repository does not model yet: the class has a definition but no properties or values. 2026-10-23: party:Product has values since party 1.1.0: the products MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K names (EPM-DEC-001-0092).
  3. Party roles are dated nodes, not subclasses (ontology-playbook.md, Step 5). A party:PartyRole is held by exactly one party (party:plays), has one role type from the party-role list, and is held from a start date (party:validFrom) to an optional end date. So one party holds any number of roles at once, and "customer" is never a class of party. A shape rejects any other subclass of party.
  4. The role list has fourteen types:
    • the model's twelve: Sold-To, Payer, Bill-To, Consignee, Jobber, Dealer, Exchange Counterparty, Throughput Holder, Carrier, Vendor, Surety and Supplier;
    • the two the decision owner added: Position Holder and Loading-Authorized Party (EPM-DEC-001-0067, decision 11; What follows, item 11).
  5. Ship-To is a site, not a role. The decision owner's inputs list Ship-Tos together with stations, and their role list has no Ship-To. So a ship-to is a party:Site, and the party that receives product there holds the Consignee role. This is competency question 21's answer. Systems that keep a ship-to as its own customer number (the decision owner's inputs make Dealer Master the system of record for ship-tos) hold, in this model, one site plus a party in the Consignee role; one sold-to can have many ship-to sites, and the sold-to party can also be the consignee.
  6. The customer class is a dated assignment. The decision owner's model gives each class assignment to an account its own effective dates. So party:CustomerClass is the assignment node: one account, one value from the customer-class list, and a start date. A line of business carries no dates in the model, so party:managedUnder links the account to it directly.
  7. Fourteen customer classes in four groups:
    • the model's ten;
    • the four EPM-DEC-001-0067 adds from the repository's MPC customer classification: independent retailer (C4) and unbranded customer (C5) under Wholesale and unbranded, and spot market buyer (C6) and crude oil customer (C8) under Trading counterparties.
    • The crude oil customer's group follows MPC's own words. MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K reports crude oil revenue from external customers in Refining & Marketing (Note 20) without naming the buyers, and says: "We mitigate credit risk with master netting agreements with companies engaged in the crude oil or refinery feedstock trading and supply business or the petroleum refining industry" (Note 2). Its FY2017 Form 10-K ties those agreements to "sales of crude oil or refinery feedstocks". MPC's filings tie part of these sales to trading and supply companies and refiners, and do not say who all the buyers are (customer-classification-evidence.md, C8). Placing the class under Trading counterparties is this record's reading of that wording.
    • The spot market buyer joins Trading counterparties too, not Wholesale and unbranded. MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K, Item 1, says: "We compete in four distinct markets for the sale of refined products—wholesale, including exports, spot, branded and retail distribution", and a note to its refined product sales volumes splits them into volumes "marketed directly to end-users" and "trading/supply volumes such as bulk sales to large unbranded resellers and other downstream companies". Spot is a market of its own. This record reads spot sales as part of the trading and supply volumes; the filing does not say so, and who the spot buyers are is not stated, so the group is this record's reading.
    • Independent retailer and unbranded customer sit in Wholesale and unbranded. MPC lists "independent retailers" beside "wholesale customers" (Item 1) and apart from brand jobbers and direct dealers; its unbranded marketing page names "independent marketers, commercial end-users, unbranded distributors and high-volume retailers" (customer-classification-evidence.md, C5). Placing independent retailers there is this record's reading.
    • The groups are top concepts; a class assignment must name a class, not a group.
  8. The channel dimension of MPC-P06 gets its values. Each class that matches one of MPC's classes C1 to C8 carries its code as party:mpcClassification, and each code is used exactly once:
    CodeCustomer class
    C1Branded jobber
    C2Direct dealer
    C3Wholesale marketing customer
    C4Independent retailer
    C5Unbranded customer
    C6Spot market buyer
    C7Export customer
    C8Crude oil customer

    The other six classes have no MPC code. This builds the values of option A of P06, which the decision owner chose (EPM-DEC-001-0047): "Channel as a dimension, built from C1 to C8" (the option's wording in decision-packages/2026-10-14/step7-rulings.md, P06), with no process-map change. An account's channel is its customer class. P06 also suggested that the channel become a dimension of Order Management or of KPIs. That is not done here. Trigger: a measure or process that needs a channel breakdown.

    • The codes are a property on the module's classes, not their own SKOS scheme as data-domain-register.md once proposed. One class list, carrying the codes, avoids two schemes for the same customers, and the module's definitions are in Claude's words, not MPC's (EPM-DEC-001-0067).
  9. Direct dealers sit under Fuel Supply. The class definition names a long-term supply agreement and a site brand assignment, not a Brand License (EPM-DEC-001-0067, What follows, item 1).
  10. Code lists, as SKOS schemes built from code-lists.csv:
    • party types (2), party roles (14), relationship types (5), agreement types (7);
    • lines of business (10, with SDP as a label only, EPM-DEC-001-0067);
    • customer classes (14 and 4 groups), site types (6), operating models (3: COCO, CODO, DODO);
    • identifier types (4), address usages (4), contact types (3), transaction kinds (7).
    • Each value has a definition in Claude's words. Agreement types overlap, so an agreement can have more than one.
  11. Relationships follow the decision owner's labels and their two rulings (EPM-DEC-001-0067):
    • Agreement is scoped to Product, and Customer Account is supplied from Site.
    • "Authorizes brand at site" runs from Agreement to Site Brand Assignment.
    • A brand reaches a party through an agreement (party:coversBrand). A party owns or operates a site through a role held at the site (party:atSite), which carries the role's dates.
  12. Account facets without values. Brand status, contract type, product line, segment, geography and delivery tier are secondary facets of the account in the model. The module declares one property for them, party:accountFacet, and defines no values: the inputs name the facets but not their values. Statuses (party, account) are likewise declared without a list.
  13. Mappings, not imports (EPM-DEC-001-0023). Party, Person and Organization carry skos:closeMatch to FOAF and W3C ORG. Nothing is imported. The PIDX and OEO mappings still wait for product concepts (EPM-DEC-001-0064): the product line is a facet with no values yet. 2026-10-23: party 1.1.0 supplies the products, maps them to OEO with SKOS, and records their PIDX families as plain identifiers (EPM-DEC-001-0092).
  14. The lubricants and marine product-line evidence is parked. EPM-DEC-001-0004 held this evidence check with the Customer work. None of the decision owner's inputs names lubricants or marine. The product line is a facet with no values in this module. Trigger: the product line's values are defined, here or in a Product domain.
  15. SHACL (shapes.ttl), run with every other module's shapes on the whole graph (EPM-DEC-001-0065). The shapes check:
    • each party is a person or an organization, with one legal name and a matching party type;
    • each role is held by one party, with one role type from its list, a start date, and no end before its start;
    • each relationship links two roles;
    • each account belongs to a party;
    • each class assignment names a class, not a group, and has a start date;
    • each site brand assignment names a brand and a site, and is made under an agreement;
    • each coded value comes from its own list;
    • no class other than person and organization is a subclass of party, directly or through any chain of subclass links.

Alternatives considered#

  • Customer as a subclass of party, or one subclass per role. Not chosen: a party holds several roles at once and over time (playbook Step 5). Competency questions 19 to 24 ask exactly for this.
  • Ship-To as a role. Not chosen: the decision owner's role list has no Ship-To, and their inputs place it with stations and sites.
  • Customer class as a direct property of the account. Not chosen: the model gives class assignments effective dates.
  • Extend the organization module. Not chosen: that module models the company's own roles and RACI, which are positions people hold. A customer's party role is a different thing, and keeping them apart avoids two meanings of "role" in one module.
  • Load the example organizations with the module. Not chosen: they would be data in the ontology (EPM-DEC-001-0005). They stay test fixtures.

The decision owner's recorded words#

This record is Claude's, under the delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (delegation-steps-5-12.md). It builds on the decision owner's words recorded in EPM-DEC-001-0067 (answers-2026-10-21-customer.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

  • EPM-DEC-001-0067, and the decision owner's words in answers-2026-10-21-customer.md.
  • business_architecture/reference/mpc/customer-classification-evidence.md (C1 to C8).
  • MPC Form 10-K for FY2025 (Item 1, competition; the refined product sales volumes; Note 2; Note 20), https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1510295/000151029526000009/mpc-20251231.htm, and for FY2017 (Summary of Principal Accounting Policies, accounts receivable), https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/1510295/000151029518000024/mpc-20171231x10k.htm, both read 2026-10-21. A search of peers' filings and of SAP and PIDX practice guided items 5 and 7 (EPM-DEC-001-0067 allows peer data as guidance in the Customer domain); none is cited.
  • ontology-playbook.md, Step 5.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0004, 0005, 0023, 0047, 0064, 0065.

Consequences#

  • The party module is built and checked by scripts/check.sh: the code lists must equal a fresh build, and test_party.py checks the lists, the vocabulary, the five organizations both ways and the shapes. It is loaded with the regression graph, and its shapes join the Step 9 gate.
  • The module is not released. A release needs the decision owner's approval. 2026-10-22: released as 1.0.0 with their approval (EPM-DEC-001-0074).
  • The regression stands at 34 answerable, 1 partial and 9 waiting. Questions 19 to 26 move in pull request 5. 2026-10-21: the party module made Q19 to Q26 answerable: 42 answerable, 1 partial (Q7), 1 waiting (Q17) (EPM-DEC-001-0071). 2026-10-23: the decision owner parked Q17 until the ontology is first shared with business consumers: 43 answerable, 0 partial, 0 waiting and 1 parked (EPM-DEC-001-0086).

EPM-DEC-001-0069: The customer systems, data flow and attributes#

From EPM-DEC-001-0069-customer-systems-attributes.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-21
QuestionsPull request 3 of EPM-DEC-001-0067's plan; it extends competency question 6 ("Which systems ... support process P, and which processes depend on system S?") to the customer's systems through the three systems the system module also holds (2026-10-21: four, with the Terminal Margin Ranking System as TMR, EPM-DEC-001-0072)
SourceClaude Code night run, 2026-10-21

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0067 lists the decision owner's seven customer inputs. Inputs 1 to 3 are diagrams of the systems the customer passes through and the flows between them. Input 5 is a table of six core systems, each with the attributes that define the customer there (Create) and the attributes that enrich it (Enrich). Input 6 adds a pane on where the customer shows up in systems. That pane covers six core systems with the model entities each implements, the origin and sync patterns, and other systems in the landscape. The decision owner asked for "both the life cycle and data flow of Customer" and for both categories of attributes to be captured (EPM-DEC-001-0067). None of the inputs is in the repository. This record turns their systems, flows and attributes into the party module (EPM-DEC-001-0068).

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Customer systems are the module's own class, party:CustomerSystem, not new members of the system module. The system module is built only from the process map's systems fields. Its shapes require each system to sit in that module's scheme with the process map as its source (EPM-DEC-001-0055). Claude's plan for this pull request, shown to the decision owner but not in the repository, put them in the system module's register. This record changes that, for that reason. Three systems are in both: RightAngle, KittyHawk and Profisee. Their customer-system node carries skos:exactMatch to the system module's node, so the customer data flow joins the processes those systems support. 2026-10-21: a fourth, the Terminal Margin Ranking System, is the system module's TMR (EPM-DEC-001-0072). Question 15's same-word test reads that link as one thing in two modules (q15b). The system module's sap is the process map's generic "SAP". When this record was decided, the system module's SAP was not matched to SAP MDG or SAP S/4HANA, because the map did not say which it meant. 2026-10-25: the decision owner identified it as SAP S/4HANA, and the two are now matched; SAP MDG stays unmatched (EPM-DEC-001-0099).
  2. 42 systems, in five tiers:
    • core (6): SAP MDG, SAP S/4HANA, Salesforce, RightAngle, Polaris, Dealer Master;
    • integration (1): MAGIC;
    • enrichment (5): Tax Image Silo, Sirion, Terminal Upload, XREF and Information Steward;
    • downstream (22): the systems that use the customer, including the nine systems below MAGIC that feed Profisee;
    • landscape: the eight other sources in input 6's pane (Terminal Audit System, FlowCal, EIS, Asphalt Marketing System, Brand Credit Card System, Terminal Margin Ranking System, Corp_Vetting and Lenel Onguard).
    • Each system has one or two sentences on what it does with the customer, in Claude's words from the inputs.
    • Three names in the inputs are not systems and are left out: MPLX, a company, where input 1 shows the customer activated for Producers; Marketing Users, a user group; and Bloomberg and news, sources of risk information.
    • FuelFacs carries CBS as an alternative label, because input 2 labels FuelFacs "CBS". Whether input 6's "CBS" is the same system is open. 2026-10-21: the decision owner assumes, for this model, that it is FuelFacs (EPM-DEC-001-0072).
  3. What the six core systems implement is input 6's mapping, entity for entity:
    SystemEntities
    SAP MDGParty, External Identifier
    SAP S/4HANAParty, Party Role, Customer Account
    SalesforceParty, Contact Point, Address
    RightAngleParty, Party Role, Contact Point, Agreement
    PolarisCustomer Account, Party Transaction Association, Site
    Dealer MasterParty Role, Site, Site Brand Assignment, Agreement
  1. 35 data flows, each a party:DataFlow with what it carries and the inputs that show it, plus a direct party:feeds link.
    • Where the inputs disagree, this record takes input 3, the only dated diagram, over input 2. Input 6 confirms the core path: the SAP customer number is generated in SAP MDG and backfilled to Salesforce through MAGIC.
    • Input 2's edges that were hard to read and that input 3 does not confirm are left out: S/4HANA to Bridger Insight, RightAngle to TFR, Kittyhawk to RCMS, and FuelFacs to TASDM.
    • Input 1 draws ten boxes below MAGIC that feed Profisee. Nine are systems and feed Profisee here. The tenth, Marketing Users, is a user group and is left out (item 2).
  2. 24 customer sourcings say how a system relates to a kind of customer:
    • origin (input 6): prospects start in Salesforce; jobbers in Dealer Master; direct dealers in SAP MDG.
    • setup (input 1): the lines of business the customer is created for in RightAngle, Salesforce and Polaris.
    • source (input 6's landscape list).
    • Input 6 lists CBS as a source of terminal customers. That sourcing is placed on FuelFacs as a reading, and its note says so, because whether input 6's CBS is FuelFacs is open (item 2). 2026-10-21: the decision owner assumes, for this model, that it is FuelFacs, and the note says so (EPM-DEC-001-0072).
    • "Customer" in input 6's Corp_Vetting line is not a role in the list, so only vendor and surety are taken.
  3. 275 system attributes, 43 Create and 232 Enrich, from input 5 after the corrections and deduplication the decision owner asked for ("correct them", "dedup them"; EPM-DEC-001-0067).
    • Six labels are corrected: Sales Manager, Address_Line_1, Joint_Tariff_Flag, Broker Reconcile Method, SAP Net Out Profit Cntr, and Inactive_Indicator_Flag, which merges two spellings.
    • Nine repeats are removed.
    • Each attribute names its system, its category and the entity of the conceptual model it describes. The mapping is Claude's reading of each field name:
      • fields naming the company's own staff on the account (account manager, territory manager, collection specialist) describe the customer account. This mapping is Claude's reading; a search of vendor customer-master practice guided it, and none of that practice is cited (EPM-DEC-001-0067);
      • authorization groups describe the line of business, because MDG keeps them by line of business (input 6);
      • fields about a system's own record (who created or changed it, and when) describe a party:SystemRecord, which is not an entity of the model.
  4. Nine shared attributes name one thing several systems carry under different names:
    • the SAP customer number (four systems);
    • the business associate ID (three);
    • the tax ID (five);
    • the legal name, the territory manager, the class of trade, credit, status, and the business partner's role and group.
    • The business associate ID in SAP MDG is the identification number of type ZBAID, which RightAngle assigns (inputs 2, 3 and 5).
  5. The six internal codes the decision owner asked about ("use external MPC search and Peer to resolve these ones."; EPM-DEC-001-0067):
    • ZBAID is resolved from the inputs: the SAP MDG identification number that holds RightAngle's business associate ID (item 7).
    • TID Nbr is read as a terminal identifier, next to RightAngle's Site Nbr, so it describes a site. That is a reading; no MPC source states it. 2026-10-22: the reading stands until the business confirms it, when the ontology is shared for consumption (EPM-DEC-001-0072, amendment of 2026-10-22). 2026-10-28: the reading stands and the point is closed; the decision owner kept it as it is (EPM-DEC-001-0072, amendment of 2026-10-28).
    • HOLDORDIND, Taboot, MPA_Flag and CR_Incentive_Flag stay named fields without a definition. Neither MPC's filings nor the inputs define them (EDGAR full-text search of MPC's and MPLX's filings and the inputs, 2026-10-21), and the peer and vendor sources that may explain some of them are not cited in the Customer domain (EPM-DEC-001-0067). 2026-10-21: the decision owner keeps them in and will define them later (EPM-DEC-001-0072). 2026-10-22: they stay undefined until the ontology is shared for consumption, when the business confirms what each means (EPM-DEC-001-0072, amendment of 2026-10-22). 2026-10-28: they stay undefined and the point is closed, at the decision owner's choice (EPM-DEC-001-0072, amendment of 2026-10-28).
    • TABS Seller ID is read as the party's seller identifier in a terminal lifting-control service: the system that authorizes or denies a lifting at the rack against the seller's allocations and the customer's credit, and passes on the bill of lading. It is mapped to an external identifier of the party. Neither MPC's filings nor the inputs name it (EDGAR full-text search, 2026-10-21). The repository already names DTN TABS as one example of a Terminal Automation System, the system that owns the gate, bay, meter and bill of lading in the Order to Cash landscape (O2C/index.html, reference R14; its vendor names are illustrative), and the O2C demo's ontology gives the Loading-Authorized Party the label "TABS customer" (demos/o2c-unbilled-databricks-free-cv-map/o2c-unbilled-databricks-free/ontology/o2c-meaning.ttl). The reading comes from industry and vendor material, which guides it and is not cited (EPM-DEC-001-0067). The decision owner asked for that search and took this recommendation, keeping the vendor material out ("I will take your recommendation", answers-2026-10-21-tabs.md). 2026-10-22: the decision owner confirmed the reading: "I confirm" (EPM-DEC-001-0072, amendment of 2026-10-22). Lifting authorization is what the Loading-Authorized Party role names (item 4 of EPM-DEC-001-0068).
    • TDS Seller ID, which the decision owner did not ask about, stays unexplained. It is mapped to an external identifier of the party, as its name says. 2026-10-21: the decision owner: "the ID of the seller" (EPM-DEC-001-0072).
  6. Internal names are allowed by EPM-DEC-001-0040, item 3. The repository is public until the goal is achieved (EPM-DEC-001-0067, decision 12), and private again at the end of the session or the goal (EPM-DEC-001-0067, What follows, item 12).

Alternatives considered#

  • Add the customer systems to the system module's register. Not chosen: that module is built from the process map, and its shapes say so.
  • Take every edge of input 2, including those hard to read. Not chosen: an uncertain edge would be stated as fact.
  • Leave unmapped the attributes whose entity is unclear. Not chosen: every attribute gets an entity, so the model's coverage can be counted. Each mapping is a reading the decision owner can correct in attributes.csv.

The decision owner's recorded words#

This record is Claude's, under the delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (delegation-steps-5-12.md). It builds on the decision owner's words recorded in EPM-DEC-001-0067 (answers-2026-10-21-customer.md), including "correct them" and "dedup them". On TABS Seller ID (item 8), the decision owner said "sounds good. I will take your recommendation" (answers-2026-10-21-tabs.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

  • EPM-DEC-001-0067 and answers-2026-10-21-customer.md; EPM-DEC-001-0068.
  • business_architecture/ontology/system/systems.csv and shapes.ttl (EPM-DEC-001-0055).
  • EPM-DEC-001-0040.

Consequences#

  • party/systems.csv, data-flows.csv, customer-sources.csv and attributes.csv are the sources. build_party.py builds systems.ttl from them, and scripts/check.sh fails unless the file equals a fresh build. test_party_systems.py pins the figures and the core mappings by hand.
  • The regression loads systems.ttl with the module.
  • Open for the decision owner (2026-10-21: EPM-DEC-001-0072 answers CBS, TMR, TDS Seller ID and keeps the four codes for later): whether input 6's CBS is FuelFacs; whether the Terminal Margin Ranking System is the process map's TMR; and the meaning of HOLDORDIND, Taboot, MPA_Flag, CR_Incentive_Flag and TDS Seller ID, whether TABS Seller ID is read rightly, and whether TID Nbr is a terminal identifier. 2026-10-22: TABS Seller ID's reading confirmed; the four codes and TID Nbr wait for the business, when the ontology is shared for consumption (EPM-DEC-001-0072, amendment of 2026-10-22). 2026-10-28: both close as they stand (EPM-DEC-001-0072, amendment of 2026-10-28).
  • 2026-10-27: the decision owner's customer inputs of that day correct two flows and the Dealer Master sourcing this record set, and take input 2's two uncertain edges, which input 8 shows (EPM-DEC-001-0108).

EPM-DEC-001-0070: The customer lifecycle, linked to processes and systems, and proposal MPC-P10#

From EPM-DEC-001-0070-customer-lifecycle.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028) for the lifecycle and its links; MPC-P10 is proposed and waits for the decision owner; 2026-10-23: accepted (EPM-DEC-001-0087)
Date2026-10-21
QuestionsPull request 4 of EPM-DEC-001-0067's plan; the customer lifecycle from onboarding (EPM-DEC-001-0052, Phase C)
SourceClaude Code night run, 2026-10-21

Context#

The decision owner's inputs give two views of a customer's life (EPM-DEC-001-0067; the inputs are not in the repository):

  • Six business stages: prospect, onboarding, payment management, risk management, relationship management and offboarding.
  • Five system stages, from input 6's pane on where the customer shows up in systems: prospect (Salesforce), master (SAP MDG), record (SAP S/4HANA), execute (the execution systems) and settle (invoices and postings back to SAP S/4HANA).

They asked for the lifecycle to be captured (EPM-DEC-001-0067, "both the life cycle and data flow of Customer"). EPM-DEC-001-0052 says the lifecycle's process nodes are proposed, not applied, under the process-map rule (AGENTS.md). 2026-10-23: no longer: MPC-P10 is accepted (EPM-DEC-001-0087) and applied in core 2.3.0, value-stream 1.1.0, data-product 1.1.0 and party 1.1.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0089 to 0092).

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Both lifecycles are SKOS schemes in the party module: party:lifecycle-stage (six stages) and party:system-stage (five). Each stage has a definition in Claude's words from the inputs.
  2. Each business stage links to the core processes that do its work (party:stageProcess). The links run to existing core 2.1.0 concepts only, so nothing in the process map changes. 2026-10-23: since party 1.1.0 the build reads core 2.3.0, and onboarding also links to CM 1.3.7.1.7, which MPC-P10.1 added to the map (EPM-DEC-001-0087, 0092). The mapping rests on each process's core definition and scope note; the names are core 2.1.0's labels:
    • Prospect: Manage Sales Pipeline (CM 1.3.6.2.1), Set Up Prospect (CM 1.3.6.6.1, today card programs only), and Establish Credit Limit & Risk Code (CM 1.3.7.4.2), which assesses the credit application.
    • Onboarding: Perform KYC Due Diligence (CM 1.3.7.4.6), CM 1.3.7.4.2, Maintain Customer and Commercial Account Master Data (CM 1.3.7.1.1) and Establish Commercial Terms & Contracts (CM 1.3.7.2.1). 2026-10-23: and Coordinate Customer Onboarding (CM 1.3.7.1.7), since party 1.1.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0092).
    • Payment management: Record Customer Receipts and Payment Notifications (CM 1.3.8.2.1), Apply Receipts (CM 1.3.8.2.3) and Manage Receivables Collections (CM 1.3.8.3.2).
    • Risk management: Monitor Credit Against Limit (CM 1.3.7.4.3) and Analyze Portfolio Credit Risk (CM 1.3.7.4.4).
    • Relationship management: Manage Key Accounts (CM 1.3.6.2.2), Conduct Customer Service Reviews (CM 1.3.7.5.5), Manage Customer Portal (CM 1.3.7.2.6) and CM 1.3.7.4.2. Core 2.1.0's scope note for CM 1.3.7.4.2 lists "periodic and event-triggered reviews" in its scope, which covers the annual credit limit review.
    • Offboarding: Perform Credit-Driven Customer Closure and Reinstatement (CM 1.3.7.4.5) and CM 1.3.7.1.1, which retires the records.
  3. Each system stage links to the customer systems that hold the record (party:stageSystem):
    • prospect: Salesforce; master: SAP MDG; record and settle: SAP S/4HANA;
    • execute: RightAngle, Polaris, Dealer Master and Terminal download. Input 6 also names card systems here, but none in the customer system list executes card transactions (the Brand Credit Card System is a source of dealers), so none is linked.
  4. The two lifecycles correspond (party:correspondsTo) where the inputs show it: prospect to prospect; onboarding to master and record; payment management to settle. Risk management, relationship management and offboarding have no system stage in input 6, so they have no correspondence.
  5. Three gaps become proposal MPC-P10, in business_architecture/business_process/proposals/MPC-P10-customer-lifecycle.md:
    • P10.1: onboarding coordination for every customer. Core 2.1.0's scope note for CM 1.3.7.1.1 names "prospect onboarding coordination", but the map's only onboarding coordinator for commercial customers is under Loyalty and Cards (trading counterparties have CM 1.2.1.2.1 Create New Counterparty). The recommendation is a new L5 process under the L4 CM 1.3.7.1.
    • P10.2: the Order to Cash process "Review Credit Limit & Risk Code" has no linked process. The value stream's own rationale names CM 1.3.7.4.3 Monitor Credit Against Limit as the closest; the proposal recommends CM 1.3.7.4.2, which revises limits and risk codes, and gives the decision owner both.
    • P10.3: a customer master data product, produced by CM 1.3.7.1.1, on the Marketing & Sales line.
    • 2026-10-23: the decision owner accepted all three (EPM-DEC-001-0087). party 1.1.0 links the onboarding stage to the new CM 1.3.7.1.7 (EPM-DEC-001-0092).
    • Each waits for the decision owner. Until then the lifecycle rests on the existing processes. 2026-10-23: no longer: the decision owner accepted all three (EPM-DEC-001-0087), and they are applied in core 2.3.0, value-stream 1.1.0, data-product 1.1.0 and party 1.1.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0089 to 0092).

Alternatives considered#

  • Add the lifecycle stages as process-map nodes. Not chosen: the stages are a view across existing processes, and the process map changes only through an approved proposal.
  • Link each system stage to a process. Not chosen: input 6 describes system stages by system, not by process. The business stages carry the process links.

The decision owner's recorded words#

This record is Claude's, under the delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (delegation-steps-5-12.md). It builds on the decision owner's words recorded in EPM-DEC-001-0067 (answers-2026-10-21-customer.md). The decision owner can reverse any item, and decides MPC-P10.

Evidence#

  • Core 2.1.0 definitions of the processes named above (business_architecture/ontology/releases/core/2.1.0/core.ttl).
  • business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json, value_stream_order_to_cash.json and data_product_portfolio.json.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0052, 0053, 0067, 0068, 0069.

Consequences#

  • party/lifecycle.csv is the source of the links. build_party.py builds lifecycle.ttl from it, and fails unless each process is in core 2.1.0 (2026-10-23: core 2.3.0 since party 1.1.0, EPM-DEC-001-0092; 2026-10-26: core 2.4.0 since party 1.4.0, EPM-DEC-001-0104), each system is a customer system, each stage has a link, and each stage has exactly the links items 2 to 4 decide. The module's shapes check the decided links at graph level for each of the eleven stages that holds at least one lifecycle link, so there a real process or system on the wrong stage, a process that does not exist, or a correspondence these items do not grant fails. A stage with no link, a link under another predicate and a stage these items do not name are rejected by the build only. scripts/check.sh checks the file is current, and test_party_lifecycle.py pins the links by hand.
  • The regression loads lifecycle.ttl with the module.
  • MPC-P10 waits for the decision owner. 2026-10-23: accepted (EPM-DEC-001-0087).
  • 2026-10-27: the prospect system stage's definition is corrected, and four system-stage links join (EPM-DEC-001-0108).

EPM-DEC-001-0071: Competency questions 19 to 26 answered by the party module#

From EPM-DEC-001-0071-customer-competency-questions.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028; the real organisations of item 3 by the decision owner, 2026-10-21)
Date2026-10-21
QuestionsCompetency questions 19 to 26 (section VII, "Customer / Party — first domain module"); pull request 5 of EPM-DEC-001-0067's plan
SourceClaude Code night run, 2026-10-21

Context#

Questions 19 to 26 waited for the Customer module (EPM-DEC-001-0004, then EPM-DEC-001-0067). The party module now holds the decision owner's conceptual model (EPM-DEC-001-0068), the systems and attributes (EPM-DEC-001-0069) and the lifecycle (EPM-DEC-001-0070). Each question gets a SPARQL query in regression/queries/, run by scripts/check.sh against the release and the module graphs (EPM-DEC-001-0019).

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Each question's answer, and the query that tests it:
    QAnswer in the graphQueries
    19A party is the identity of a person or an organization. A customer account is the container the party transacts through. They are two classes, and an account is not a kind of party.q19-party-vs-account.rq
    20Any role in the party-role list. A party plays any number of roles at once, as MPC and MPLX do.q20-roles-one-party.rq, q20b-real-parties-both-ways.rq
    21Roles are dated holds, not subclasses of party. A ship-to is a site, and the party that receives product there holds the Consignee role. No Ship-To role exists.q21a-roles-are-dated.rq, q21b-ship-to-is-a-site.rq, q21c-no-ship-to-role.rq (must be none)
    22A Loading-Authorized Party is a role held for a period. The permission that grants it is a separate record, the authorization.q22-loading-authorized-party.rq
    23Credit sits on the customer account. A credit roll-up relationship counts one role's exposure under another's.q23-where-credit-sits.rq
    24Yes. A party plays several role nodes, each with its kind from the one party-role list (Sold-To, Supplier and Exchange Counterparty among them), its dates and the agreement it works under, so the same organisation can hold one role in one deal and another in the next. MPC holds roles of two kinds. There is no second "Customer" class.q24a-one-party-many-roles.rq, q24b-no-customer-class.rq (must be none)
    25A customer-grained measure binds to the customer account by default. A measure about one role binds to that role, and a measure about a location binds to the site. None binds to the bare word customer.q25-measure-binding.rq
    26A party transaction association names the role a party played in the transaction, and the agent must refuse "who is the customer" without a role (the consumer rule, which relies on party:roleType).q26-one-role-per-order.rq
  1. Question 25's binding rule is a scope note on party:CustomerAccount. Core 2.1.0's wording rules out the collapsed customer and names the account among the levels:
    • Establish Credit Limit & Risk Code (CM 1.3.7.4.2) says limits and risk codes "attach at the level the governed customer-domain model defines" and are "never assigned to a collapsed 'customer'".
    • Monitor Credit Against Limit (CM 1.3.7.4.3) says exposure "aggregates at the Party/Customer Account/credit-group level".
    • Analyze Customer Profitability (CM 1.3.7.2.5) analyzes "customers, accounts, channels, and segments".
    • Choosing the account as the default grain is this record's, under the delegation: credit terms and the customer class sit on the account (EPM-DEC-001-0068), and exposure aggregates from it. A named role or a site is the grain when the measure is about one of them.
  2. Section VII's done-when, with real organisations. It asks for five real multi-role organisations described both ways. Customer records stay out of the ontology (EPM-DEC-001-0005), and the decision owner's customer data is not in the repository (EPM-DEC-001-0067). The decision owner chose the real ones: "use MPC and MPLX as the real organisations" (answers-2026-10-21-real-parties.md).
    • party/reference-parties.ttl (2026-10-22: moved to ref-mpc/reference-parties.ttl, its roles and agreement on ref-mpc's namespace; EPM-DEC-001-0075) describes them on ref-mpc's IRIs, as public reference facts (EPM-DEC-001-0005): MPC holds the Throughput Holder and Payer roles, and MPLX the Carrier and Vendor roles, under MPLX's fee-based agreements with minimum volume commitments with MPC. Each role holds from 2012-10-31, the date of the first agreements the filing's exhibit index lists (Exhibits 10.1 and 10.2). Each role cites MPLX's FY2025 Form 10-K (reference/mpc/mplx-fy2025-10k-fact-sheet.md, section 3). No transaction, price or volume is recorded.
    • Query q20b-real-parties-both-ways.rq reads them party to roles and role to party, and test_party.py pins both ways by hand (2026-10-22: now ref-mpc/test_ref_mpc.py, EPM-DEC-001-0075). The five invented organisations of examples.ttl stay as test fixtures.
    • The done-when names five organisations; the decision owner chose these two. That is their decision, recorded here.
  3. Two negative tests in regression/test_run.py show the "must be none" queries fire: adding a Ship-To role fails question 21, and adding a Customer subclass of party fails question 24.

The decision owner's recorded words#

On section VII's done-when, in the Claude Code session of 2026-10-21 (saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-21-real-parties.md): "use MPC and MPLX as the real organisations".

The rest of this record is Claude's, under the delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse any item.

Evidence#

  • Core 2.1.0's wording on CM 1.3.7.4.2 and CM 1.3.7.4.3 (their scope notes) and CM 1.3.7.2.5 (its definition) (business_architecture/ontology/releases/core/2.1.0/core.ttl).
  • business_architecture/ontology/competency-questions.md, section VII.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0005, 0019, 0061, 0067, 0068, 0069, 0070.

Consequences#

  • MPLX's FY2025 Form 10-K, which the reference parties cite, is a described source (src:mplx-s01) in evidence tier 2, as one of the MPC evidence files in reference/mpc/ (EPM-DEC-001-0059).
  • The regression stands at 42 answerable, 1 partial (Q7) and 1 waiting (Q17, EPM-DEC-001-0045). 2026-10-23: the decision owner parked Q17 until the ontology is first shared with business consumers: 43 answerable, 0 partial, 0 waiting and 1 parked (EPM-DEC-001-0086).

Decision#

EPM-DEC-001-0073: The playbook takes the method lessons of the Customer domain#

From EPM-DEC-001-0073-playbook-customer-lessons.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-21, for adding the lessons; their wording is Claude's, and the decision owner can change any of it)
Date2026-10-21
QuestionsThe method lessons of the Customer domain's pull requests (#327, #329 to #333); the playbook's maintenance rule (EPM-DEC-001-0024)
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-21 (session 7); answers-2026-10-21-release-playbook.md

Context#

The Customer domain went through six pull requests, #327 and #329 to #333, with records EPM-DEC-001-0067 to 0072. Their reviews held them for defects of a few recurring kinds, and the playbook did not yet name a rule that would have prevented each. The decision owner asked for the session's approaches to go into the playbook and reminded Claude of its role. The playbook is the company-neutral method; project decisions go in decision records and the worklog, and the playbook changes only when the method changes (EPM-DEC-001-0024).

Decision#

The decision owner's request: "update ontology playbook with new guidline or approach from what we did in this session.. Remember the role of the playbook".

What follows (Claude): each lesson goes in the one section a reader would look in, written without project specifics:

  1. Step 5, item 2: a role held for a period is a node of its own, with a start date, the party and the kind of role.
  2. Step 9, items 8 to 11: a negative test asserts the violation it means; when a decision fixes a set, the build and the shapes guard the set itself; each guard's reach is stated exactly; a rule over a hierarchy follows the whole chain.
  3. Step 11, items 9 and 10: every identity link reaches every identity query, and its test pins both IRIs of each pair; the whole check suite runs before a review.
  4. §2, Source of truth and scope: what is derived from material that cannot be published goes in only with the owner's permission, and is marked as fact or reading; a source outside the evidence order guides a reading without supporting it; an uncertain identity is not settled in the data, and an owner's "assume" is recorded as an assumption.
  5. §2, Modules: two modules that hold the same thing are linked, not merged, with a hand-held match checked by the build where the IRIs differ.
  6. §5.6, Counts: a sweep searches for the claim, not for the words the change used.
  7. §7: one paragraph on how the Customer domain ran, naming the review findings that produced these rules.

Alternatives considered#

  • Put the lessons in the worklog only. Not chosen: the worklog records the project; the next build reads the playbook for the method.
  • Add a separate lessons section. Not chosen: each rule belongs in the step a reader is following when they need it.

The decision owner's recorded words#

From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-21-release-playbook.md: "Please log everything you have done. And update ontology playbook with new guidline or approach from what we did in this session.. Remember the role of the playbook".

Evidence#

  • EPM-DEC-001-0024 (the playbook changes only when the method changes).
  • The review findings on #329 (the subclass guard), #330 (Q15 and skos:exactMatch), #331 (the lifecycle's guards and their stated reach), #332 (the "assume" wording; the missed Questions row) and #333 (the missing evidence tier; a count split across lines), each recorded in its pull request.

Consequences#

  • ontology-playbook.md carries the rules above. The worklog records this change. 2026-10-23: the §7 paragraph (item 7) moved word for word to the worklog's evidence map (§8), with the rest of the playbook's worked example; the rules stay in the playbook (worklog §4, "The playbook's evidence moves here").

EPM-DEC-001-0075: The reference parties MPC and MPLX move into the ref-mpc module#

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0071 described MPC and MPLX in the party module's terms, so that competency questions section VII's done-when has real organisations. The file sat in party/. When party 1.0.0 was released, EPM-DEC-001-0074 kept it out of the package, because the playbook's module policy puts reference instances of real companies in their own module, and left the move open for the decision owner. Decision#

The decision owner's answer: "Yes they should split into their own refrence module"

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The file moves to ref-mpc/reference-parties.ttl. ref-mpc already holds MPC and MPLX as legal entities (EPM-DEC-001-0033), and the parties already use its IRIs for the two companies.
  2. The roles and the agreement take ref-mpc's namespace. The four party roles and the services agreement move from https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/modules/party/reference/ to https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/modules/ref-mpc/party/, so every node the file describes is in the module that owns it, apart from the shared source node for MPLX's 10-K.   4. The tests move with the file. ref-mpc/test_ref_mpc.py reads the parties both ways, checks the dates and the source, checks every described node is in ref-mpc's namespace, checks the file left party/, and validates the parties against the party module's shapes. The regression loads the file from its new place.
  5. No party release is needed: the party 1.0.0 package does not hold the file, and no file in it changes.
  6. The move will ship in ref-mpc 1.2.0, a minor release: it adds instances and changes no released IRI (references/versioning.md in the ontology skill). The release follows in its own pull request, and requires party 1.0.0 beside organization 2.0.0. The release plan the decision owner approved on 2026-10-22 does not list ref-mpc 1.2.0, so its release record cites the decision owner's own approval of it. 2026-10-22: they approved it; EPM-DEC-001-0080 records the release.

Alternatives considered#

  • Keep the old IRIs under the party namespace. Not chosen: ref-mpc would then describe nodes in another module's namespace, and the old IRIs were never released.
  • A new reference module for parties only. Not chosen: ref-mpc is the reference module for MPC and MPLX, and already holds both companies.

From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-22-morning.md: "Yes they should split into their own refrence module". On how to record it: "Aligned with your proposal".

Evidence#

EPM-DEC-001-0076: Release core 2.2.0#

From EPM-DEC-001-0076-core-2-2-0-release.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-22, for the release; the package's contents delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-22
QuestionsThe core release with the process-map changes merged since 2.0.0 (release plan, section 1; EPM-DEC-001-0052, Phase D), with the two checks EPM-DEC-001-0060 and 0063 left for it
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-22; answers-2026-10-22-morning.md

Context#

Released packages stay fixed, and builds read the process map at a pinned commit (EPM-DEC-001-0047, What follows, item 2). core 2.0.0 and 2.1.0 were built from the map at commit b75fd99. Ten merged pull requests changed the map since then (#288 to #296 and #301): the MPC proposals P01, P02b, P03, P04, P05, P07 and P08 (EPM-DEC-001-0047), and the two gaps of EPM-DEC-001-0048 and the widening of EPM-DEC-001-0051. EPM-DEC-001-0060 asked the next core release to check the concepts that reference APQC element 19948, and EPM-DEC-001-0063, item 2, asked it to review RF 1.2's Candidate status. Claude's release plan named core 2.2.0 as its first release, and the decision owner approved the plan.

Decision#

The decision owner's approval: "release plan approved"

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A minor release, 2.2.0, issued 2026-10-22, built by release/build_core_2_2.py from the released 2.1.0 graph and the map at commit 8259354 (the merge of #301, the last change to the map on main). It adds concepts and notes and removes no IRI (references/versioning.md in the ontology skill). The proposal MPC-P10 is not applied to the map, so nothing of it is here. 2026-10-23: the decision owner accepted MPC-P10 (EPM-DEC-001-0087); core 2.3.0 adds CM 1.3.7.1.7 (EPM-DEC-001-0089).
  2. 23 new concepts: CM 1.1.3.7.13 and CM 1.2.5.1.5 (EPM-DEC-001-0048), CM 1.2.4.3.8 and its two children (MPC-P05), RF 1.2.1 to RF 1.2.5 (MPC-P02b) and 13 Midstream service concepts, MS 1.1 to MS 1.4.2 (MPC-P01). The four stubs RF 1, RF 1.1, RF 1.2 and MS 1 keep their IRIs under Step 2's slug pins and gain their IDs as skos:notation. That makes the 27 map IDs the release plan counted.
  3. New concepts are shaped as 2.1.0's map-sourced concepts: preferred label, notation, definition, parent, scheme and level. The definition is the first sentence of the map's description, which states the activity and its outcome; the rest of the description is the scope note, so citations and boundaries stay with the concept. A provenance note names the map commit and the record that added it. They carry no conceptKind, domain or planned flows, because the map gives them none, as with the 175 map-sourced concepts in 2.1.0.
  4. Changed descriptions become scope notes on eight concepts. Approved definitions do not change. Where 2.1.0's definition is the map's earlier description (CM 1.1.7, CM 1.2.5), the note is the text the map added; elsewhere it is the map's new description.
  5. APQC element 19948 (EPM-DEC-001-0060). Element 19948 is "Analyze internal operations" (APQC 1.1.3.2), a strategy element. Each of the 16 concepts carries it as its top-scoring step 3b candidate (build/output/step3b-definition-review.csv, column apqc_id), as every concept with an APQC mapping decision carries its candidate. Fourteen are marked "No candidate": confirmed, because the reference names the candidate compared and the decision says it does not match. Two are marked "Review link" and are corrected to "Rejected", each with a change note: CM 1.2.6.3 Supply Operations, whose own note calls the candidate "an unrelated internal-operations match", and the scheme root Downstream Operations, which is not an analysis of internal operations and whose definition review linked no element.
  6. RF 1.2 is no longer a Candidate node (EPM-DEC-001-0063, item 2). P02b populated it with five steps, and its ownership question is answered for the map's scope. A change note says so; its 2026-09-22 provenance stays as history. RF 1.1's Candidate note is unchanged: no record has reviewed it.
  7. The package holds core.ttl, core.jsonld, the shapes and canonical-fact mapping of 2.1.0 unchanged, a DCAT record, counts, the changelog and a manifest with each file's SHA-256: 19,810 triples and 706 process concepts. The shapes conform.
  8. Repointing follows in its own pull request: the regression, projection, validation and module builds still read 2.1.0 until then, and the data-product module's Loss Control link to CM 1.1.3.7.13 is emitted there.

Alternatives considered#

  • Take each new concept's whole description as its definition, as 2.1.0 did for the original map descriptions. Not chosen: several descriptions carry boundaries and citations, which belong in a scope note.
  • Drop the 19948 references from the "No candidate" concepts. Not chosen: every concept with an APQC decision references the candidate it was compared with; dropping 14 would make them the exception.
  • Rebuild the whole Step 2 to Step 3 chain from the current map. Not chosen: 2.1.0 is released and fixed, and the minor release adds to it.

Consequences#

EPM-DEC-001-0077: Release the Step 7 modules data-product, value-stream, system, lane and market-data as 1.0.0#

From EPM-DEC-001-0077-step7-modules-1-0-0-release.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-22, for the releases; the packages' contents delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-22
QuestionsThe first releases of five Step 7 modules, each a major that needs the decision owner's approval (module policy, 2026-09-18; release plan, section 2)
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-22; answers-2026-10-22-morning.md

Context#

The data-product (EPM-DEC-001-0053), value-stream (0054), system (0055), lane (0056) and market-data (0057, 0066) modules were built and merged during the Step 7 work, each unreleased. A module's first release is a major, and a major needs the decision owner's approval. Claude's release plan listed all five as 1.0.0 releases, after core 2.2.0, and the decision owner approved the plan. core 2.2.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0076) holds every process these modules link, including CM 1.1.3.7.13, Loss Control's producer, and the modules' builds now read it.

Decision#

The decision owner's approval: "release plan approved"

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Five releases, each 1.0.0, issued 2026-10-22, built by release/build_module.py as the other modules' packages are: the module's Turtle and JSON-LD, its shapes, a DCAT record, its changelog and a manifest with each file's SHA-256.
  2. Each package holds its module's vocabulary and instance file, nothing else: data-product 718 triples, value-stream 992, system 589, lane 59 and market-data 668.
  3. Dependencies follow the IRIs each module uses. data-product, system and lane require core 2.2.0. value-stream requires core 2.2.0, data-product 1.0.0 and kpi 1.0.1, because its processes produce and consume data products and carry measure links. market-data requires kpi 1.0.1, whose crack-spreads measure its series belong to; it names no process. Each package's shapes conform with the released packages it depends on loaded. Where two paths reach different releases of one module (value-stream reaches core 2.2.0 directly and 2.1.0 through kpi 1.0.1), the check loads only the highest. Every dependency is pinned to the release named here, and what a dependency itself requires is read from that release's own manifest, so a later kpi or data-product release changes neither these packages nor what their checks load.
  4. The 33 parked data products are in the data-product package as parked items: each is a defined node with its place in the portfolio, its status and a note that its definition waits for its data owners (EPM-DEC-001-0053, item 3). They have no definition, and the release adds none. A later minor release defines them when the portfolio describes them.
  5. Tags data-product-v1.0.0, value-stream-v1.0.0, system-v1.0.0, lane-v1.0.0 and market-data-v1.0.0 follow the merge.

Alternatives considered#

  • One package for the five modules. Not chosen: each module has its own namespace, decision record and owners, and versions independently (the playbook's module policy).
  • Wait for kpi 1.1.0 before releasing value-stream and market-data. Not chosen: they use only measure IRIs that kpi 1.0.1 already releases; a later minor release can move them to 1.1.0.
