EPM-DEC-001-0078: Release integration 1.0.0: placement, authority and consumer; the APQC list stays a reference file#

From EPM-DEC-001-0078-integration-1-0-0-release.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-22, for the release; the package's contents and the APQC list's place delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-22
QuestionsThe first release of the small Phase E vocabularies, a major that needs the decision owner's approval (module policy, 2026-09-18; release plan, section 2), and whether the APQC element list goes with it
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-22; answers-2026-10-22-morning.md

Context#

Four small folders came out of the Phase E work on the competency questions: placement (EPM-DEC-001-0058), authority (0059), consumer (0061) and population (0062). Each is a few dozen triples that ties the other modules together. Claude's release plan recommended releasing them together as one integration 1.0.0 package rather than as four, and left open whether apqc, the full APQC PCF 7.2.2 element list (EPM-DEC-001-0060), goes with them or stays a reference file. The same plan, in section 3, folds the eight measure populations into kpi 1.1.0. The decision owner approved the plan.

Decision#

The decision owner's approval: "release plan approved"

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. integration 1.0.0, issued 2026-10-22, built by release/build_module.py as the other modules' packages are: Turtle and JSON-LD, the consumer shapes, a DCAT record, a changelog and a manifest with each file's SHA-256. 280 triples.
  2. It holds three vocabularies, each in its own namespace: placement/vocabulary.ttl, authority/vocabulary.ttl with authority/source-tiers.ttl, and consumer/vocabulary.ttl. No IRI changes. The package IRI, https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/modules/integration, names the release only; no term is minted under it.
  3. The population statements ship in kpi 1.1.0, not here. The release plan puts them in both places. Section 3 is the specific one: it folds them into kpi, and EPM-DEC-001-0062, item 5, says the next kpi release can take them. They hang on the kpi module's measure IRIs, so they belong with the measures, and shipping them twice would put the same triples in two packages.
  4. The APQC element list stays a reference file, outside any package.
    • It is APQC's content, not this ontology's definitions: a tier 5 consistency reference, support only (EPM-DEC-001-0059; AGENTS.md's evidence order).
    • The APQC/IBM licence lets the list be copied and published only if its notice and attribution "travel with all copies" (business_architecture/reference/README.md). The file carries the notice as a Turtle comment in its header. A package is a serialised graph, which drops comments, and the graph holds no rights statement, so a package would ship APQC's content without its notice.
    • Consumers need what they already have. core carries the 251 elements its processes reference, with the PCF's rights statement. Question 11 needs the full list, and the regression loads the file from the repository.
    • A later release can package it with a rights statement in the graph, if a consumer needs it outside the repository.
  5. Dependencies follow the terms the vocabularies name. The package requires core 2.2.0 and organization 2.0.0, kpi 1.0.1, data-product, value-stream, system, lane and market-data 1.0.0, and party 1.0.0. placement annotates their properties, consumer's rules rely on their terms (the rule that no customer exists without a role relies on party:roleType), and authority tiers the sources they cite. The consumer shapes conform with those packages loaded. The seventh may-answer rule relies on the population terms, which ship in kpi 1.1.0, after this package. A later minor release can require kpi 1.1.0.
  6. Tag integration-v1.0.0 follows the merge.

Alternatives considered#

  • Four packages, one per folder. Not chosen: each is a few dozen triples, with one decision record each, and they change together when a module is added. The release plan recommended one package.
  • Put population in this package too, as section 2 of the plan lists it. Not chosen, for item 3's reason.
  • Package the APQC list here. Not chosen, for item 4's reasons.

The decision owner's recorded words#

From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-22-morning.md: "release plan approved".

Evidence#

  • business_architecture/ontology/release/build_module.py and test_build_module.py.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0058 to 0062; business_architecture/reference/README.md (APQC attribution); the playbook's module policy (§2, policy 8).

Consequences#

  • releases/integration/1.0.0/ is written; scripts/check.sh fails unless it equals a fresh build.
  • The placement, authority, consumer and apqc READMEs and the ontology README say where each folder ships.

EPM-DEC-001-0079: Release kpi 1.1.0: the measure populations join the package, and the measure shape is closed#

From EPM-DEC-001-0079-kpi-1-1-0-release.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-22, for the release; the package's contents and the version class delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-22
QuestionsRelease plan, section 3: fold the eight measure populations (EPM-DEC-001-0062) into the kpi module, and close the measure shape so a value cannot hang off a measure definition
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-22; answers-2026-10-22-morning.md

Context#

kpi 1.0.1 holds 41 measure definitions. What eight MPC-defined measures include and exclude sits in a separate population folder, which EPM-DEC-001-0062 kept outside the released kpi tables and left for the next kpi release. The released kpi shapes also leave the measure open: a data-product, series or region carries a closed "definitions, never values" shape (EPM-DEC-001-0005), but a measure does not, so a target or an actual stated on a measure passes. Pi found this on the market-data branch. Claude's release plan proposed kpi 1.1.0 for both, and the decision owner approved the plan.

Decision#

The decision owner's approval: "release plan approved"

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. kpi 1.1.0, issued 2026-10-22, built by release/build_module.py: 1,056 triples, requiring core 2.2.0. The kpi build reads core 2.2.0, and the 41 measures and their 47 process links are unchanged on it.
  2. The populations join the package as their own files. The package holds kpi/vocabulary.ttl and kpi/measures.ttl, and population/vocabulary.ttl and population/population.ttl: eight pop:includes and five pop:excludes statements on the measure IRIs, each with its evidence row. They keep their pop: IRIs. The integration 1.0.0 package's seventh may-answer rule (EPM-DEC-001-0078) and the regression's question 28 query name those IRIs, so renaming them into the kpi namespace would break both. They are in this package only: integration 1.0.0 leaves them out.
  3. The measure shape is closed. shp:NoValuesOnMeasures lists every property a measure carries: its definition, classification, links, source and population. Any other property fails, so no value, target or actual can be stated on a measure. The three population properties get stated cardinalities, at most one each. Every measure in every module conforms. A repository-wide search found no other property on a measure.
  4. A minor release. No IRI changes and nothing is removed (references/versioning.md in the ontology skill: minor is "new concepts, modules, aliases, references"). The closed shape changes no definition's meaning, so every query and answer stays correct. It encodes EPM-DEC-001-0005, which already ruled out values, and the released content meets it. core 2.1.0 added constraint shapes as a minor release in the same way, and no consumer is active (EPM-DEC-001-0001).
  5. The released dependents stay on kpi 1.0.1. value-stream, market-data and integration 1.0.0 were released against it and pin it, and a check reads each released dependency's own dependencies from its manifest (EPM-DEC-001-0077's builder), so their packages and what their checks load are unchanged by this release.
  6. Tag kpi-v1.1.0 follows the merge.

Alternatives considered#

  • Move the populations into the kpi namespace and tables. Not chosen, for item 2's reason.
  • Ship the populations in integration 1.0.0, as section 2 of the plan lists them. Not chosen: section 3 folds them into kpi, and EPM-DEC-001-0062 item 5 says the next kpi release takes them; one package each (EPM-DEC-001-0078, item 3).
  • A major release for the closed shape. Not chosen, for item 4's reasons.

The decision owner's recorded words#

From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-22-morning.md: "release plan approved".

Evidence#

  • business_architecture/ontology/release/build_module.py, test_build_module.py; kpi/shapes.ttl, kpi/test_measures.py.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0005, 0042, 0043, 0062, 0076, 0078.

Consequences#

  • releases/kpi/1.1.0/ is written; releases/kpi/1.0.0/ and 1.0.1/ are untouched. scripts/check.sh fails unless 1.1.0 equals a fresh build.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0062, the kpi and population READMEs and the ontology README carry dated notes.

EPM-DEC-001-0082: Step 10: one catalog of every release; the Step 10 record brought up to date#

From EPM-DEC-001-0082-step10-release-catalog.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-22
QuestionsPhase E, Step 10: a DCAT record for each new release, and how a consumer finds every release in one place
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-22 (Phase E); answers-2026-10-22-phase-e.md

Context#

Each release package carries its own catalog.ttl: one dcat:Dataset for that release, its distributions, and its link to the catalog <https://w3id.org/lsc/ontology/catalog> (EPM-DEC-001-0029, 0037). All 19 packages under releases/ share that catalog IRI, but no file lists them together, so a consumer had to open each package to find the releases. The worklog's Step 10 row listed releases up to party 1.0.0 and left out core 2.2.0, the five Step 7 modules, integration 1.0.0, kpi 1.1.0 and ref-mpc 1.2.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0076 to 0080).

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. One catalog file. releases/catalog.ttl is the union of the 19 packages' catalog.ttl files, plus the catalog's title and description. It says nothing about a release that the release's own record does not. release/build_catalog.py builds it from the packages on disk. 2026-10-23: 20 packages, with core 2.3.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0089).
  2. Checked in scripts/check.sh. build_catalog.py --verify fails unless the written catalog equals a fresh build, so a new release that is not in the catalog fails the check. release/test_build_catalog.py checks one dataset per package folder; that every distribution a dataset links to is described, and names a file in its package; and that the catalog adds only its own title and description.
  3. No new package and no release. The catalog is a file beside the packages, not a module, so it needs no version or approval. The packages are unchanged.
  4. The Step 10 row lists every release. "Catalog resolves" stays parked: the namespace is not hosted (EPM-DEC-001-0039).

Alternatives considered#

  • Leave the per-package records only. Not chosen: a consumer would have to know every package to find them.
  • Rewrite each package's catalog.ttl to list every release. Not chosen: that changes released packages, which only a new release may do.

EPM-DEC-001-0085: The open-ontologies trial: not adopted; it found no defect the checks miss#

From EPM-DEC-001-0085-open-ontologies-trial.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028; the trial and its adoption rule approved by the decision owner, 2026-10-21)
Date2026-10-22
QuestionsQueue item 9: does open-ontologies' semantic plan or OWL coherence check find anything in the core releases that scripts/check.sh and the competency regression miss?
SourceClaude Code sessions of 2026-10-21 (session 7) and 2026-10-22 (Phase E); answers-2026-10-21-open-ontologies-trial.md, answers-2026-10-22-phase-f.md

Context#

On 2026-10-21 Claude proposed a local trial of open-ontologies (github.com/fabio-rovai/open-ontologies, MIT licence, Rust) once the next core release existed: its plan of core 2.1.0 against 2.2.0, and its OWL coherence check, outside the repository and CI, with the result recorded here and the tool adopted only if its report finds something check.sh and the regression miss. The decision owner added it to the queue. On 2026-10-22, after Phase E, the decision owner gave the go-ahead. The trial ran that night; its report is business_architecture/ontology/trials/open-ontologies-2026-10-22.md.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The tool is not adopted into scripts/check.sh or CI. The trial does not meet the adoption rule, read as a finding of a defect: its only new output is three lint notes, none a defect (item 2).
  2. What it found (commit 93295305, built from source, each run under a second):
    • plan reports 217 added and 7 removed triples, which an independent rdflib diff confirms; release/test_build_core_2_2.py asserts the same removed set (the version header and two APQC decisions) and the 23 new concepts. It counts no skos:Concept as an individual, so it reports no added individuals for a release that adds 23 concepts, and it calls every release "not an extension" because each release rewrites the version header.
    • reason owl-dl finds core 2.2.0, and the union of the 11 newest packages, consistent and every class satisfiable. A deliberate clash fails as it should. The graphs hold one disjointness axiom (Person and Organization in party), which the party shapes' sh:xone already enforces.
    • Three lint notes: core:CanonicalFact and core:ScopedSequence have no rdfs:comment, and core:evidenceBasis has no rdfs:domain. Both classes carry a skos:definition, which is how core defines its terms, and Rule 1 of the projection needs a range, which evidenceBasis has. None is a defect.
  3. When to run it again: if a release adds OWL axioms that SHACL does not test, such as disjointness between classes, class domains and ranges, or property chains. The trial's script is described in the report.

Alternatives considered#

  • Adopt the coherence check for its speed. Not chosen: it would have nothing to find today, and the adoption rule asks for a finding.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-21-open-ontologies-trial.md: "yes, add the Phase D trial to the queue". Saved in answers-2026-10-22-phase-f.md: "you can do the openontology trial as well." The trial plan and its adoption rule are Claude's words, which the decision owner approved; the verdict above is Claude's, under the delegation: "yes, extend the delegation to steps 5 to 12." (business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/delegation-steps-5-12.md). The decision owner can reverse it.

Evidence#

  • business_architecture/ontology/trials/open-ontologies-2026-10-22.md.
  • business_architecture/ontology/release/test_build_core_2_2.py; releases/party/1.0.0/shapes.ttl; EPM-DEC-001-0076.

Consequences#

  • Queue item 9 is done. Nothing changes in the repository's checks.

Decision#

The decision owner's rulings:

  1. P10.1, option A. A new L5 process, CM 1.3.7.1.7 Coordinate Customer Onboarding, under the L4 CM 1.3.7.1 Manage Commercial Master Data Stewardship. It covers capturing the setup request, starting the credit, KYC and contract checks through their owning processes, requesting the governed master record, and confirming the customer is set up in the systems that use it. CM 1.3.6.6.1 Set Up Prospect stays as the card-program case.
  2. P10.2, option A. The Order to Cash process "Review Credit Limit & Risk Code" (p-otc-review-credit-limit-risk-code) links to CM 1.3.7.4.2 Establish Credit Limit & Risk Code.
  3. P10.3. A "Customer Master" data product on the Marketing & Sales line (dpl-marketing-sales), produced by CM 1.3.7.1.1 Maintain Customer and Commercial Account Master Data.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. P10.1 lands in core 2.3.0, a minor release: it adds a concept and changes no released IRI. The process gets its ID, slug and definition through Steps 2 and 3, as every accepted process does. The party module's onboarding stage then also links to it, in party 1.1.0.
  2. P10.2 lands in value-stream 1.1.0. The value-stream file's gap analysis entry for the process is updated to say it is linked. 2026-10-23: the entry is removed instead, since the process is no longer a gap, and its rationale says why it realizes CM 1.3.7.4.2 (EPM-DEC-001-0090, item 2).
  3. P10.3 lands in data-product 1.1.0, which the data-product module builds from data_product_portfolio.json (EPM-DEC-001-0053).
  4. The proposal's status becomes Accepted, with this record.

Alternatives considered#

  • P10.1 B, widen Set Up Prospect and move it under CM 1.3.7. Not chosen: moving a node changes its place in the taxonomy, and its card-program steps would no longer fit.
  • P10.2 B, link to CM 1.3.7.4.3 Monitor Credit Against Limit. Not chosen: monitoring watches exposure against a limit and does not set or revise it.
  • Change nothing. Not chosen for any of the three.

Decision#

The decision owner's approval: "Approve all six (Recommended)"

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A minor release, 1.1.0, issued 2026-10-23. It adds a link and two ranges and removes no IRI.
  2. The credit review realizes CM 1.3.7.4.2. In value_stream_order_to_cash.json the process's linkedProcessIds names it, its rationale says why (the process's scope includes periodic and event-triggered reviews; CM 1.3.7.4.3 monitors exposure and does not revise a limit), and the gap-analysis entry that called it missing is removed. The module now has 73 links to core processes and no value-stream process without one.
  3. Both properties take rdfs:range skos:Concept. The projection's note proposed dp:DataProduct for vs:produces, but the property's definition and shape allow a data product line too, and an OWL range of dp:DataProduct would let a reasoner type a line as a data product. Data products and lines are both SKOS concepts in the data-product module, so skos:Concept is the one concrete range that holds for every allowed value. The shapes keep the narrower check: each value is a data product or a data product line.
  4. It requires the current releases: core 2.3.0, data-product 1.0.0 and kpi 1.1.0. 1.0.0 stays on disk as released, requiring core 2.2.0 and kpi 1.0.1.
  5. The projection loads value-stream 1.1.0, and the two gaps leave its list: 14 remain, in kpi, party and integration. The rerun gave 33,754 triples, 3,928 nodes and 19,698 relationships, no SHACL violation in the graph (588 in-graph constraints, the mutant still caught), an empty round-trip diff and equal hashes for the sorted and shuffled loads.

Alternatives considered#

  • rdfs:range dp:DataProduct for vs:produces, as the projection's note said. Not chosen: it is narrower than what the property allows (above).

EPM-DEC-001-0092: Release party 1.1.0: products from MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K, mapped to OEO, with PIDX families; onboarding linked to CM 1.3.7.1.7; eight projection fixes#

From EPM-DEC-001-0092-party-1-1-0-release.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-23, for the release and for PIDX family codes; the package's contents delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-23
QuestionsThe fourth close-out release (EPM-DEC-001-0088): the product values that the PIDX and OEO mappings wait for (EPM-DEC-001-0064); MPC-P10.1's link from the onboarding stage (EPM-DEC-001-0087); party's eight projection gaps (EPM-DEC-001-0036)
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-23; answers-2026-10-23-closeout.md

Context#

party:Product had no values, so PIDX and OEO waited (EPM-DEC-001-0064). The decision owner chose product values from MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K in party, mapped to PIDX and OEO (EPM-DEC-001-0088). Research against the primary sources on 2026-10-23 found:

  • OEO. The current release is v2.14.0, released 2026-10-08 (version IRI https://openenergyplatform.org/ontology/oeo/releases/2.14.0/oeo.owl). It has classes for motor gasoline, gas diesel oil, jet fuel, kerosene, heating oil, liquefied petroleum gas, hydrocarbon, petroleum coke, mineral oil product, diesel fuel and liquid biofuel, and none for distillates, NGLs, fuel oil, asphalt or renewable diesel (OEO issue 1958, still open, asks for fuel oil classes).
  • PIDX. The downstream product codes are free to download (document 04-101-15-45-2010, file PIDX_ProductCodes_10_20_2026.xlsx, from pidx.org/standards). PIDX publishes no IRIs, and each code names one traded grade that a company requested. Codes are grouped in one-letter families, but the Families sheet is dated 2015-09-29, and PIDX's notice of 2024-07-31 changed family S. The licence (EULA V110329) allows using a Standard unmodified and copying it with attribution and PIDX's notices (section 2(b)(ii)), and grants no licence to modify it or create derivative works outside a Standards Development Activity (section 2(c)(v)).

Claude put PIDX to the decision owner again, because no SKOS mapping to PIDX is possible.

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions: the release, "Approve all six (Recommended)" (EPM-DEC-001-0088); and for PIDX, "Family codes, flagged (Recommended)": each product records its PIDX family letter as a plain identifier, with the workbook as its source, PIDX's notice and a note that the family list dates from 2015; no IRIs; the business confirms the letters with PIDX.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A minor release, 1.1.0, issued 2026-10-23. It adds values, a property, links, ranges and shapes, and removes no IRI.
  2. Products. The scheme party:product holds 27 values, each also a party:Product. The top concepts are the 10-K's six refinery product groups (gasoline, distillates, propane, NGLs and petrochemicals, heavy fuel oil, asphalt) and renewable diesel, from the Renewable Diesel segment. Under them sit the 20 products the 10-K names: reformulated and blend-grade gasoline; ULSD, jet fuel, kerosene, and No. 1 and No. 2 fuel oils; propylene, xylene, butane, benzene, toluene, cumene, and fuel-grade and anode-grade coke, which the 10-K names in its NGLs and petrochemicals paragraph; and five asphalt products. Each cites the 10-K. The definitions are Claude's words, and an editorial note says so.
  3. OEO mappings, in party/product-mappings.csv, each with its reason: 3 skos:closeMatch (gasoline to motor gasoline, jet fuel to jet fuel, No. 2 fuel oil to heating oil) and 16 skos:broadMatch (the two gasoline grades, ULSD, kerosene, propane, butane, the five petrochemicals, both cokes, heavy fuel oil, and renewable diesel to both diesel fuel and liquid biofuel). There is no skos:exactMatch: the 10-K names products and does not define them, so no two definitions can be shown to say the same thing. Nine products have no OEO class to map to.
  4. PIDX families, in party/pidx-families.csv, through a new datatype property party:pidxProductFamily (range xsd:string, at most two per product, one capital letter): ULSD V and S, jet fuel K and J, kerosene H, No. 1 fuel oil E and L, No. 2 fuel oil M, butane N, asphalt Y. Only the letters are recorded, not PIDX's family names or code lists. The property's scope note gives the source, the 2015 caveat and PIDX's notice, as the licence requires.
  5. Onboarding. The onboarding stage links to CM 1.3.7.1.7 Coordinate Customer Onboarding (MPC-P10.1). The build and its decided-links shape name five processes for the stage.
  6. The eight projection fixes: ranges for party:evidenceInput (xsd:string), party:customerRole and party:carries (rdf:langString), and shapes stating the cardinality of party:plays, party:governedBy, party:implements and party:feeds (each optional) and party:mpcClassification (exactly one, C1 to C8, on each class that has one).
  7. party:Product's definition drops "the module gives products no properties or values", which 1.1.0 makes untrue; a scope note says where its values come from.
  8. It requires core 2.3.0, the current release, for CM 1.3.7.1.7. The party build reads 2.3.0. 1.0.0 stays on disk as released.
  9. The projection loads party 1.1.0 with a fixed prefix oeo for the mapping targets. The eight gaps leave its list, so 6 remain, in kpi and integration. The rerun gave 34,054 triples, 3,971 nodes and 19,840 relationships, no SHACL violation in the graph over 602 in-graph constraints (the mutant still caught), an empty round-trip diff and equal hashes for the sorted and shuffled loads.

EPM-DEC-001-0094: Release integration 2.0.0; the projection's list of release gaps is empty#

From EPM-DEC-001-0094-integration-2-0-0-release.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-23, for the release and for making it a major; the package's contents delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-23
QuestionsThe sixth and last close-out release (EPM-DEC-001-0088): integration's five projection gaps (EPM-DEC-001-0036)
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-23; answers-2026-10-23-closeout.md

Context#

After value-stream 1.1.0, party 1.1.0 and kpi 1.1.1, five gaps remained in the projection's list, all in integration 1.0.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0036, 0090, 0092, 0093): no range for plc:placementKind or con:reliesOn, and no shape stating the cardinality of plc:placementKind, auth:rank or auth:evidenceTier. con:reliesOn is defined as "the property or class an answer reads"; 24 of its 25 values are properties, and one, on the rule "Name the customer without a role", is the process concept CM 1.3.7.1.1, which is neither.

Decision#

The decision owner's approval: "Approve all six (Recommended)", with the counts corrected to five integration fixes: "Approve 8 and 5 (Recommended)". The release was approved as a patch, 1.0.1. The independent review held it: narrowing con:reliesOn's published definition from property or class to property is a changed meaning, which the playbook makes a major release (ontology-playbook.md, Appendix B). Claude asked again, and the decision owner chose "Major: integration 2.0.0 (Recommended)".

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. A major release, 2.0.0, issued 2026-10-23. No IRI changes; con:reliesOn's meaning narrows (item 3). No class value was ever used, and nothing consumes the ontology yet.
  2. plc:placementKind takes rdfs:range skos:Concept, the type of the placement-kind values. placement/shapes.ttl names the twelve placing properties EPM-DEC-001-0058 classifies and states exactly one kind for each, so a property that loses its kind still fails; any other property with a kind has at most one.
  3. con:reliesOn takes rdfs:range rdf:Property, and its definition says property, not property or class, with a change note. The one value that was not a property, CM 1.3.7.1.1, moves to rdfs:seeAlso on its rule, so the rule still points to it. dcterms:references was tried first; the consumer shapes read its object as a cited source. The narrowed definition makes the release a major.
  4. authority/shapes.ttl states exactly one auth:rank per tier (an integer from 1), selecting the tiers by their scheme, and exactly one auth:evidenceTier per cited source, selecting the sources as the objects of dcterms:source anywhere in the graph, so a cited source that loses its tier still fails. The release builder now runs SHACL with SHACL-AF enabled (advanced=True), as the other checks do, so the SPARQL target runs there too. All 5 tiers and 65 sources conform, and a test pins each tier's rank: process-authority 1, mpc 2, peers 3, industry 4, framework 5.
  5. The package's shapes.ttl holds the consumer, placement and authority shapes together. release/build_module.py gains an own_shapes count, so a module can package more than one shape file of its own; every other module keeps one.
  6. It requires the current releases: core 2.3.0, organization 2.0.0, kpi 1.1.1, data-product 1.1.0, value-stream 1.1.0, system 1.0.0, lane 1.0.0, market-data 1.0.0 and party 1.1.0. 1.0.0 stays on disk as released.
  7. The projection loads integration 2.0.0, and its list of release gaps is empty: the 16 gaps of EPM-DEC-001-0036's amendment of 2026-10-22 are all closed. The rerun gave 34,058 triples, 3,971 nodes and 19,843 relationships, no SHACL violation in the graph over 602 in-graph constraints (the mutant still caught), an empty round-trip diff and equal hashes for the sorted and shuffled loads.

Alternatives considered#

  • owl:Thing as con:reliesOn's range. Not chosen: it is concrete only in name and says nothing about the values.
  • Keep property or class and a patch, 1.0.1, with con:reliesOn left on the exception list. Not chosen by the decision owner.
  • Split con:reliesOn into a property link and a concept link. Not chosen: it adds a property for one value, and the rule's link to the concept is context, which rdfs:seeAlso states.

The decision owner's recorded words#

From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-23-closeout.md: "Approve all six (Recommended)", "Approve 8 and 5 (Recommended)" and "Major: integration 2.0.0 (Recommended)".

Evidence#

  • placement/vocabulary.ttl, consumer/vocabulary.ttl, authority/vocabulary.ttl, authority/source-tiers.ttl; consumer/shapes.ttl.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0036, 0058, 0059, 0061, 0078, 0088, 0090, 0092, 0093.

Consequences#

  • releases/integration/2.0.0/ is written; scripts/check.sh fails unless it equals a fresh build.
  • projection/pipeline.py's RELEASE_EXCEPTIONS is empty; a new gap fails the build until a release closes it or it is listed with its fix.
  • Tag integration-v2.0.0 after the merge.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0088, which approved this release as 1.0.1, carries a dated note.

EPM-DEC-001-0095: The ontology-to-LPG guideline moves into the ontology-to-lpg skill; EPM's copy points to it#

From EPM-DEC-001-0095-lpg-guideline-to-skill.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-23, for making the guideline a skill, moving it into the skill, testing it and the skill's design; the skill's contents and EPM's pointers delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-23
QuestionsWhere the method for projecting a W3C ontology into a labelled property graph lives (EPM-DEC-001-0021); how the lessons of the close-out releases reach it
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-23; answers-2026-10-23-closeout.md, blocks 12 to 32

Context#

Since 2026-10-05 this repository has kept a pinned copy of the decision owner's guideline for projecting the ontology into a Neo4j labelled property graph (LPG), in business_architecture/ontology/lpg-projection/. The master copy was in the enterprise-people-graph repository, and the copy was last re-pinned to v2.2, commit 8ce9ada, on 2026-10-14 (EPM-DEC-001-0021). Step 12 follows it (EPM-DEC-001-0020, 0036).

Projecting every released package, through the close-out releases, taught lessons v2.2 does not hold: the inputs are released packages, each with its own shapes; every namespace used for predicates or classes needs a fixed prefix; release gaps need a checked list, each with its fix; and a Rule 4a node may count an inverse link as one of its ends (EPM-DEC-001-0036, 0090, 0092 to 0094). While the close-out releases were in review, the decision owner asked whether the projection documents were being updated, and whether the guideline should become a skill.

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions:

  1. The guideline becomes a skill, ontology-to-lpg, in the personal-agent-skills repository.
  2. The guideline moves into the skill. The enterprise-people-graph repository and this repository point to it rather than keep copies.
  3. The skill is tested before it is relied on (block 18).
  4. The skill's design is "Read from engine": its in-graph SHACL gate reads what n10s says it compiled (n10s.validation.shacl.listShapes()), its Python subset of the shapes is only an advisory dry run, and S3PG and PG-Schema are cited as background.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  • How the skill was tested. Claude chose the method: a fresh agent ran the skill on its own library example and on EPM's released packages. Before the skill merged, test runs on 2026-10-23 and 2026-10-24 also used the current release set, a Neo4j Aura Free instance and a local Apache Jena Fuseki, and deleted the test data afterwards, as block 27 allows (the results are in personal-agent-skills #46 and the skill's SOURCES.md). Those runs tested the skill. Loading the ontology for the decision owner to explore, and the Databricks steps, are the separate later task (EPM-DEC-001-0096).
  1. The skill is skills/data/ontology-to-lpg in personal-agent-skills, merged in personal-agent-skills #46 as da75164. It is the guideline's version 2.3, and its references/versions.md lists what 2.3 adds over 2.2. It keeps the five design rules, options 4a and 4b for Rule 4, and add-ons A to G, so the playbook's references to them still hold.
  2. The enterprise-people-graph guideline folder points to the skill (enterprise-people-graph #4).
  3. EPM's lpg-projection/ README points to the skill at da75164. The two v2.2 files stay in the folder as superseded evidence of what Steps 1 and 12 followed until 2026-10-23. They are not deleted and are not copied again. This amends EPM-DEC-001-0021: the repository no longer keeps a copy of the current method.
  4. Current references point to the skill: the ontology README's contents table, the playbook (its file list, Step 1, item 10, and Step 12's procedure), the worklog's Step 12 row and the glossary's LPG projection entry. Dated history, earlier records and the worked example in the playbook's §7 keep their references to lpg-projection/, because they describe what was done then. 2026-10-23: the playbook's file list and its §7 moved to the worklog's evidence map (§8), so those references to lpg-projection/ and to the skill's location are there now; the playbook names the skill only (worklog §4, "The playbook's evidence moves here").
  5. projection/pipeline.py stays on v2.2. The skill says a project pinned to an earlier version keeps its own scripts (SKILL.md, "First, find where the projection stands", item 2). The pipeline, its manifest and its report still name "v2.2 (lpg-projection/, pin 8ce9ada)", which is what the pipeline implements. Moving the pipeline onto the skill's scripts would be a separate change, and no record asks for it.
  6. Neo4j Aura. At the decision owner's request, Claude installed the neo4j-contrib/neo4j-skills plugin, 1.0.1, at user scope. n10s is not available on Aura (the Neosemantics documentation, "Availability & Installation"), so the projection validates on a local Neo4j with n10s, and copying the verified graph to Aura is an add-on. The Aura credentials are in a local credentials file outside the repository and never enter it.
  7. Triple stores. The decision owner asked whether the skill should also load the ontology into any triple store (block 28) and chose "Add it now (Recommended)" (block 29): the skill loads the same validated files into any SPARQL 1.1 store through the W3C Graph Store HTTP Protocol, one named graph per package, and checks per-predicate counts against the files and the Neo4j export.

Alternatives considered#

  • Update the guideline only, in enterprise-people-graph, as v2.3, and copy it again. Not chosen by the decision owner.
  • Fold the guideline into the ontology skill. Not chosen by the decision owner: the projection is its own gated method, and the ontology skill's Step 12 hands over to it.
  • Keep the guideline in enterprise-people-graph, with the skill pointing to it. Not chosen by the decision owner.
  • Keep fixing the skill's own subset of the shapes. Not chosen by the decision owner: n10s decides which shapes it compiles, so the gate reads that answer from the engine.
  • Add the triple-store loading later, or put it in a separate skill. Not chosen by the decision owner (block 29).

The decision owner's recorded words#

From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-23-closeout.md:

  • "By the way are updating RDF to LPG projection documents/playbook... I am not sure what we call it."
  • "Do you think we should make a skill out of this projection guidline/playbook?" The choice: "Make it a skill (Recommended)".
  • "Actually do you think we should move the guideline / playbook to personal skill repo from people graph?" The choice: "Move it into the skill (Recommended)".
  • "add that as part of the goal"
  • "let us make sure we test the skill to ensure it works . The guideline gives what should be done to prepare for the projection and the skill do the projection with the user. correct?"
  • "Are there any high quality external refreces either from Neo4j or somewhere else that can further guide us?" The choice: "Read from engine (Recommended)".
  • On Neo4j's skills and Aura: "Also let us install https://github.com/neo4j-contrib/neo4j-skills And further see where any of the skills from Neo4j may help." and "We will eventual test projection to Aura."

Evidence#

  • EPM-DEC-001-0020, 0021, 0036, 0090, 0092, 0093, 0094.
  • The skill's SKILL.md and references/versions.md, in personal-agent-skills at da75164.
  • Neo4j Labs, Neosemantics (n10s) documentation, "Availability & Installation" (neo4j.com/labs/neosemantics), read 2026-10-23.

Consequences#

  • lpg-projection/README.md names the skill as the current method and carries a dated note that the v2.2 files are superseded.
  • EPM-DEC-001-0021 carries a dated note and is amended by this record.
  • The store tests are a separate task after the ontology closes (EPM-DEC-001-0096; blocks 26 to 32): load the ontology into Apache Jena and into a Databricks Delta triple table, project it to a local Neo4j and to Aura, rebuild both from the Databricks store and check that no data is lost, then use OntoBricks to link Unity Catalog metric views to the processes.

EPM-DEC-001-0096: The ontology is closed#

From EPM-DEC-001-0096-ontology-closed.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028, on the goal the decision owner set on 2026-10-23)
Date2026-10-23
QuestionsIs the ontology done, after every outstanding question is addressed? What stays open after it closes?
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-23; answers-2026-10-23-closeout.md, blocks 8 and 26 to 32

Context#

Phase E closed on 2026-10-22 with Steps 0 to 12 done or standing as recorded, each with its listed exceptions (EPM-DEC-001-0083). That record listed the items still waiting on the decision owner, on a process owner, on the business and on the next releases. On 2026-10-23 the decision owner answered the items that waited on them, and set the session goal: keep going until the ontology is done, after addressing all the outstanding questions.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028): the ontology is closed. Five facts close it.

  1. Every item that waited on the decision owner is answered. Q17 is parked (EPM-DEC-001-0086). MPC-P10 is accepted (EPM-DEC-001-0087). The product values and the six close-out releases are approved (EPM-DEC-001-0088), and each release is recorded (EPM-DEC-001-0089 to 0094), with integration made a major release by the decision owner's choice (EPM-DEC-001-0094). Phase F, the ontology skill, is done: personal-agent-skills pull request #45 merged (EPM-DEC-001-0084). The projection guideline is now the ontology-to-lpg skill (EPM-DEC-001-0095).
  2. Q17 is parked with the business. It waits for the ontology's first sharing with business consumers, and the business close-out package, business_architecture/ontology/business-closeout.md, lists it with every other point only the business can settle (EPM-DEC-001-0086).
  3. The six close-out releases are built and tagged: core-v2.3.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0089), value-stream-v1.1.0 (0090), data-product-v1.1.0 (0091), party-v1.1.0 (0092), kpi-v1.1.1 (0093) and integration-v2.0.0 (0094). releases/catalog.ttl lists every release (EPM-DEC-001-0082).
  4. The projection's list of release gaps is empty. projection/pipeline.py's RELEASE_EXCEPTIONS is empty, and the rerun with integration 2.0.0 passed every check (EPM-DEC-001-0094).
  5. The regression is green. It stands at 43 answerable, 0 partial, 0 waiting and 1 parked (regression/run.py, EPM-DEC-001-0086). A question parked by a recorded decision counts as green (EPM-DEC-001-0019).

What stays open after closure. None of it blocks the closure, and none of it is part of the ontology build:

  • With the business, when the ontology is first shared: every point in business-closeout.md. These are Q17, Market Data's accountable group, the four codes and TID Nbr, the product list and its PIDX families, Forecaster, Crude RFC, PSM, four working role definitions and the 33 measures with no stated population (EPM-DEC-001-0086). Each answer is recorded as an amendment to the record the package names. 2026-10-28: the decision owner worked through the package: points 3 and 4 are closed, point 5 is narrowed to the PIDX family letters, and points 17 and 18 are added (EPM-DEC-001-0086, amendment of 2026-10-28).
  • Parked for other reasons, each with its trigger: the IOF Supply Chain mapping (EPM-DEC-001-0064), IRIs that resolve (EPM-DEC-001-0039), and the 462 held process-map rows, which get a fresh verdict only if their source is corrected (EPM-DEC-001-0025, 0083; python3 scripts/epm_facts.py counts).
  • A separate, later task: loading the ontology into Apache Jena and exploring it there and in Neo4j Aura at the same time, which the decision owner set apart from the ontology goal (block 26). The decision owner later allowed Claude to delete the test data it loads into Aura and Jena after thorough testing; both are loaded again with the projection skill in that later task (block 27). The same later task loads the ontology into a Databricks triple store, a plain Delta triple table, and rebuilds the local Neo4j and Aura graphs from it with a check that no data is lost; OntoBricks follows, to link Unity Catalog data and metric views to the ontology (blocks 30 to 32).

Alternatives considered#

  • Keep the ontology open until the business answers the package. Not chosen: the decision owner parked those points until the ontology is first shared (EPM-DEC-001-0086), so they cannot be answered before it closes.
  • Keep it open until the store tests (Jena, Databricks, Neo4j and Aura). Not chosen: the decision owner made that a separate task after the ontology is complete (block 26).

EPM-DEC-001-0097: The pricing measures map to the decision owner's Databricks metric views and Genie agents; they stay market indicators, the crack-spread view is the crack spread's source, and the pricing data products are classified as foundational or derived#

From EPM-DEC-001-0097-pricing-measures-bound-to-metric-views.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-25, items 1 to 17; delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028: the benchmark view's value column in item 11; the questions each answer replies to are in answers-2026-10-25-item-f.md); open questions listed below
Date2026-10-25
QuestionsWhich ontology measures do the pricing metric views and Genie agents serve? Are those measures KPIs? Which view is the crack spread's source? How are the views' layers, lineage and systems recorded? How does market-data cover every series the views hold? Which pricing tables and views are data products, and of which kind? What may a Genie agent quote?
SourceClaude Code sessions of 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25; answers-2026-10-25-item-f.md

Context#

The decision owner said they would provide "the replica, silver, and gold tables, metric views, and the genie agents for all pricing Operational metrics/KPI", to close out the data-product side of those measures. On 2026-10-25 they shared files describing three Databricks metric views with their lineage and two Genie agents, then answered three rounds of questions on lineage and data products. Following the rule for customer inputs (EPM-DEC-001-0067), the files stay outside the repository: "The only thing we dont add to the repo is the file that I shaerd with from which you extract those information. but the infor should go in the repo." This record holds what Claude derived from them, in Claude's words. Internal system, table and view names are allowed here by EPM-DEC-001-0040, item 3; the 2026-10-19 amendment of EPM-DEC-001-0050 says they stay readable while the repository is public. Every object named here is in idp_sbx, a sandbox catalog (item 7).

The assets:

AssetNameBuilt from
Genie agentCrack Spread Price Analytics (room 01f1a79c27e9120d93e3bc2baa5b11ea)a crack-spread metric view (item 1)
Metric view (bound)idp_sbx.semantic_sem_commercial.mpr_mips_crack_spread_price_fact_metrics_vwthe gold table below, filtered to Record_Active_Indicator = 'Y', no joins
Metric view (same result, another pattern)idp_sbx.semantic_sem_commercial.mpr_mips_crack_spread_price_fact_metricsfromsilver_vwsilver, with KPI_ID, Crack_Spread_Sum and Effective_End_Datetime exposed
Gold tableidp_sbx.gold_commercial_core.mpr_mips_crack_spread_price_fact, built by the job Job_UDM_Master_Pipelinesilver: mpr_price_detail_fact (the values), mpr_price_master_dim (the names), mips_price_assessment_group_dim, mips_price_group_dim and mips_price_sheet_dim (which price masters are crack spreads, and their group); mips_price_batch_dim and mips_price_assessment_fact are read with no column flowing

Metric view (benchmarks)idp_sbx.semantic_sem_commercial.mips_mpr_benchmark_prices_fact_vwsilver directly, no gold: mpr_price_detail_fact joined to mpr_price_master_dim on the price master ID
Silver tablesidp_sbx.silver_udm_core.* (facts) and idp_sbx.silver_mdm_core.* (dimensions)the replica tables
Replica tablessynapse_dev.replica_mips.*, replica_mpr.*, replica_kittyhawk.*the systems MIPS, MPR and KittyHawk

The Executive Summary agent groups its prices into nine groups: Benchmark, Crack Spread, Gasoline Basis, Gasoline Cash, Distillate Basis, Distillate Cash, Crude, Renewables and NGLs. Its own notes name four of them "Market Margin Potential" measures, under exactly the names kpi 1.1.1 uses: Crack Spreads Pricing, Gasoline Market Pricing, Distillate Market Pricing and Crude Market Pricing.

Decision#

The measures and their sources#

  1. The crack spread comes from the crack-spread view, mpr_mips_crack_spread_price_fact_metrics_vw. The decision owner: "Crack view wins. Anyting related to crack spread comes from crack spread specific metric view", and on the two views: "the agent can consume both. But we chose for the agent to consume the one without "silver" in the name. The one with silver is another design pattern that gives the same result." The bound view reads the gold table, which takes each crack's value unchanged from the MPR settle price. The Executive Summary agent's six crack-spread blends, built from MIPS assessment values, are a second presentation of the same series, not a source.
  2. The benchmark view is mips_mpr_benchmark_prices_fact_vw, a metric view of its own: "This is actually another metric view . This one is focused on benchmark prices". It reads silver directly, and its own definition holds the five instruments (572 ICE Brent, 747 NYMEX WTI Cushing, 750 NYMEX Natural Gas, 752 NYMEX Heating Oil, 9471 NYMEX RBOB), a 160-day window and a cap on forward periods. It serves Crude Market Pricing (item 4).
  3. The pricing measures stay market indicators, and the crack spread's KPI ID is kept as an identifier. The decision owner: "let us keep them as market indicator. Not all of these are KPIs so our intepretation here is correct unless you think otherwise." and "It is okay to leave the KPI_ID even though they may not be KPI . We will need the ID to integrate with other data (e.g governance data in Purview or unity catalog)". The classes stand as EPM-DEC-001-0042 set them and EPM-DEC-001-0050 approved them. Crack Spreads Pricing gets dcterms:identifier "KPI-0001" (the agent's identifier), with the gold table's constant KPI_ID value "KPI Crack Spread 3-2-1" beside it and a note that this constant labels all 127 series and all 1,090,423 rows of the gold table alike, so it names the measure, not a series. The files give no KPI ID for the other measures.
  4. The mapping of assets to measures. The decision owner asked for it: "You may need to map to which pricing metric / kpi they map to and let me know." Claude presented the table below on 2026-10-25 as its finding, not as a question, followed by the six numbered questions in the saved answers (block 7). Asked later the same day to approve the mapping as a table (saved answers, blocks 17 and 18), the decision owner: "I agree and align with the mappng." That table put benchmarks with renewables and NGLs under Additional Market Prices; the decision owner asked in the same message whether that row belongs to the benchmark metric view, so Claude asked that row on its own: whether the benchmark view maps to Crude Market Pricing, whose definition names its instruments, and Additional Market Prices covers only renewables and NGLs (block 19). The decision owner: "sounds good. I agree". The table as recorded:
    Asset or agent groupMeasure in kpi 1.1.1Evidence
    Crack-spread view and agent (127 series)Crack Spreads Pricing (crack-spreads)the agent's scope; the Executive Summary notes name it; the KPI ID
    Executive Summary: Crack Spread group (six blends)Crack Spreads Pricing (a presentation, item 1)the same series

    The identifiers agree where both sides hold them. The six primary regional 3-2-1 series the crack-spread agent names (43870 USGC MEH, 37438 Chicago WTI, 37439 Group/Tulsa WTI, 36855 LA ANS, 37443 PNW ANS, 37471 SLC WTI), the blended cracks (37464, 42023) and the NYMEX crack 39998 are crack-spread series with the same Price Master IDs (mkt:priceMasterId) in market-data 1.0.0. NYMEX WTI Cushing (747) is there too, as a benchmark (a leg of the WTI cracks), not a series. The other NYMEX cracks the agent names (44934, 44935) are not in market-data 1.0.0.

  5. market-data covers every series the views hold, by naming the columns that list them. The decision owner: "Our sereies should cover all of what I just shared. They are more comprehensive", then "can we just add the table columns that will give us this to show that the full series can be obtained ieven if we just list a few in market data". So:
    • the complete set of crack-spread series is the distinct Price_Master_ID values in the crack-spread view, each named by Price_Master_Name and grouped by MIPS_Groups_Text (127 series in 44 groups on 2026-10-25);
    • the complete set for the other groups is the distinct MIPSDisplayName values in the Executive Summary view, filtered as the agent's group filters do (by Price_Group_Name or Price_Sheet_Name);
    • the complete set of benchmarks is the benchmark view's five instruments, under Crude Market Pricing;
    • market-data keeps its named series and adds the ones the files name, each with its Price Master ID where one is given, as examples linked to their rows.
  6. A Genie agent may quote a value from its bound source. The decision owner: "Yes . the agent will use the Metric view to get the value", building on "The intention is that the genie agent should be able to add context to the KPI value using the ontology." This amends EPM-DEC-001-0061, items 2 and 3, for this case: an agent may quote a measure's value taken through the metric view the graph binds to that measure, naming the view, and add the ontology's context. Computing a value from the graph, building a crack spread from its legs, and quoting a value from a source the graph does not bind to that measure stay refused.

The lineage#

  1. The names are the sandbox's, marked as sandbox. The decision owner: "yes mark as sandbox for now". Each table and view records that it is in the idp_sbx sandbox catalog.
  2. The layers are recorded, in the decision owner's names, each linked to the layer it is built from by prov:wasDerivedFrom: replica, silver (UDM facts and MDM dimensions), gold, metric view; and the Genie agent, which uses a metric view. Q3 asked whether to record exactly these five layers, in the decision owner's names, linked by derivation (and, as Q3 put it, as data-product distributions, which item 14 replaces); the decision owner: "We record those layesr." The closing summary's row 3 restated it, and the decision owner confirmed the summary: "Yes we are both aligned."
  3. The joins and derivations are recorded as facts. Q4 proposed table lineage as fact and the joins and derivations as inferred; the decision owner: "record both as facts. I can double check later". The closing summary's row 4 restated it, and the decision owner confirmed the summary. For the crack spread, the gold table's joins were checked against the data on 2026-10-25: all 127 price masters match, and the crack value equals the MPR settle price on every gold row checked since 2026-09-01. For the Executive Summary, the join keys and the derivations of Price_Master_ID, ChangeValue and SortColumns come from column lineage. For the benchmark view, the join and filters come from the view's own definition.
  4. Only the idp_sbx silver layer is recorded; the legacy reads are left out. The decision owner: "Leave legacy out only use the one with udm_core." and "We leave out the legacy". The silver tables' and the crack-spread gold table's reads from the legacy synapse_dev.mdm_core, udm_core and commercial_core tables are not recorded.
  5. Lineage is recorded table to table at every hop, and column to column only for the value columns. Q7 proposed exactly this, naming the crack price, assessment_amount, Previous_amount and ChangeValue; the decision owner: "I agree." The closing summary's row 7 restated it, and the decision owner confirmed the summary. The value columns are the crack value (gold Crack_Spread_Price_Amount from silver Settle_Price_Amount), the Executive Summary's assessment_amount, Previous_amount and ChangeValue, and, delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028 because Q7 predates the benchmark view's lineage and does not name it, the benchmark view's Settle_Price_Amount.
  6. The systems each view depends on come from its lineage. The decision owner: "Lineage is the evidence". The benchmark view depends on MPR and KittyHawk (no MIPS, despite its name); the crack-spread view on MPR (the prices), MIPS (which price masters are crack spreads, from the sheets 'Crack Spreads', 'Crack Spreads - MPC' and 'Crack Spreads - 3rd Party', and their group) and KittyHawk; the Executive Summary view on MIPS, MPR and KittyHawk.

The data products#

  1. All pricing sits under Master Pricing. The decision owner: "All pricing sits under "Master Pricing" data portfolio". The new pricing data products are recorded as parts (dcterms:isPartOf) of the existing product:master-pricing (data-product 1.1.0, "Master Pricing", active).
  2. Each data product is classified as foundational or derived, in a new Data Product Kind code list (Q12 proposed the kinds with definitions and the layer mapping, and Q15 the code list with scope notes and the sources in the authority module; the closing summary's row 12 restated the kinds, definitions, criteria, the sources at the lowest tier and the layer mapping below, and the decision owner confirmed the summary). The decision owner asked for "the concepts of Foundationa Data Product and Derived Data Produt", researched from good sources, then: "Let us add these definitions that we found in the ontology as well. We can refrence those sources with link. I like the criteria you listed as well." To Q15: "yes". The definitions, in Claude's words:
    • Foundational data product: a governed data product that publishes the facts or master data of one business domain, built only from the operational systems of record, with transformations limited to cleansing, validation, conforming and identity resolution, and not modelled for a particular consumer.
    • Derived data product: a governed data product built from one or more other data products, adding business logic (aggregation, calculation, measures or a model for a use case) for a known group of consumers.
    • A skos:scopeNote lists the criteria that tell them apart: inputs (operational systems, or other data products); logic (cleansing only, or aggregation and measures); consumers (not fitted to one, or fitted to a group of use cases); owner (the source or domain team, or the consuming team); rate of change (stable, or changes more often and can be rebuilt from its sources).
    • The sources, each registered in the authority module with its link at tier 5 (generic frameworks): Z. Dehghani, "How to Move Beyond a Monolithic Data Lake to a Distributed Data Mesh", martinfowler.com, 2019; the Data Mesh Architecture site (INNOQ); Databricks, "What is the medallion lakehouse architecture?"; Microsoft Purview, "Create and manage data products"; the Open Data Product Standard 4.0 (Linux Foundation, 2025), whose product type includes raw and derived data; and W3C PROV-O (prov:wasDerivedFrom). No MPC, peer or downstream source defines the two kinds, so these are support only, as AGENTS.md's evidence order says of generic frameworks. Q16 proposed this lowest tier; the decision owner: "yes".
    • The layers: silver tables are foundational; gold tables and metric views are derived; replica tables are not data products (they copy a system's own records); Genie agents are not data products but consumers (prov:SoftwareAgent) that use (prov:used) the metric views.
  3. Each classified data product carries a classification test and a reason, and SHACL shapes check the criteria that can be checked, as the kpi module does for measure kinds: a derived data product is derived from at least one other data product; a foundational one is derived from none and has at least one primary source system. Q15 proposed these; the decision owner: "yes".
  4. Only the new pricing data products are classified now. The decision owner, to Q17: "Yes we classify the new pricing products now". The portfolio's other data products stay unclassified until a later decision.

Items 13 to 16 are the design of the next data-product release; nothing is released by this record.

Testing#

  1. Synthetic data for testing. The decision owner: "We have the freedom when using synthetic data in Free databrick edition to shape it as we want . as long as it ihas the same shape as the one shared". The views are in another Databricks workspace, so tests in the Free Edition use synthetic tables and views with the same columns and grain.

Differences between the views and the measures' definitions#

Each is a question for the decision owner, not changed here. 2026-10-25 (evening): Claude now decides each under EPM-DEC-001-0028 (amendment of 2026-10-25, evening) and records the decision here; a change to a released measure ships only in a release approved under that amendment:

  1. Forward periods. All three views carry forward periods (Relative_Period_Number, 0 to 5 in the crack-spread data; Price_Period_Datetime with forward monthly averages in the Executive Summary). The five pricing measures' grains in kpi 1.1.1 are daily only ("daily, by market hub", "daily, by hub", "daily, by marker").
  2. Crack Spreads Pricing: the measure's grain is by market hub; the view's series are by Price Master ID, grouped by region. The view's monthly value is the mean of daily settles, which the measure does not state. The agent's trend is a 20-day moving average; the measure compares against a 30-day trend (the Executive Summary agent also uses 30 days).
  3. Gasoline and Distillate Market Pricing: the measures say "spot prices"; the views hold both cash prices and basis (a differential to the futures contract). The measures' basis is "$/gal as published"; the agent reports cents per gallon, multiplying the stored dollars by 100.
  4. Crude Market Pricing: the measure covers "Crude oil benchmark prices and differentials, with related futures (natural gas, heating oil, RBOB)". The Executive Summary's Crude group holds differentials only (two of them fuel oil against Brent), and the benchmark futures come from the benchmark view, which item 4 maps to this measure. Three crude instruments (30532, 79068 and 79069) shift their pricing period by two months before monthly averages.
  5. Additional Market Prices: the renewables group includes credits and RINs, quoted per credit, and soybean oil in cents per pound; the measure's basis says only "as published".

Open questions#

  1. The differences above, each kept, changed in the measure, or noted as the view's own rule.
  2. For the decision owner's team: the crack-spread agent's instructions name MEASURE(Crack_Spread_Sum) for its day-over-day totals, and the bound view has no Crack_Spread_Sum (only the other view does). Once the agent reads the bound view, either the view gains the measure or the instructions change.
  3. Who is accountable for Market Data. Not decided: "we wont make that decision now" (business-closeout.md, point 2).
  4. Ownership and stewardship of the data products: "it will remain parked" (Q17 of the build, EPM-DEC-001-0086).

What follows#

Each of these needs the decision owner's approval of the release; none is given yet. 2026-10-25 (evening): the decision owner approved the plan of all five ("Approve the plan (Recommended)", EPM-DEC-001-0028, amendment of 2026-10-25, evening). They are released as EPM-DEC-001-0100 records.

  • market-data 1.1.0, requiring the current kpi: the columns that list each group's series (item 5), the named series the files give with their Price Master IDs, and the link from each series to its measure (mkt:seriesOf). Definitions only; the module's shapes reject prices. It also moves market-data off kpi 1.0.1 (EPM-DEC-001-0077).
  • A data-product release: the Data Product Kind code list (item 14), the pricing data products as parts of Master Pricing with their kind, classification test and reason (items 13 to 16), their tables and views as DCAT distributions with prov:wasDerivedFrom from layer to layer (items 8 to 11), the bindings con:publishesMeasure from each data product to its measures (EPM-DEC-001-0061, item 1), and SHACL shapes for the kinds (item 15).
  • system additions: MIPS, MPR and Databricks (with the workspace, catalogs and schemas the files give), beside KittyHawk, which the module already holds; each view's dependence on its systems (item 12).
  • kpi: the KPI ID on Crack Spreads Pricing (item 3); Additional Market Prices narrowed to renewables and NGLs (item 4), which changes its label (now "Additional Market Prices (Benchmark, Crude, Renewables, NGLs)"), definition and classification reason but keeps its slug, as the playbook says of a renamed concept; and any change the open questions bring. This record changes no module. 2026-10-26: the label named here is the one being replaced; kpi 1.2.0's label is "Additional Market Prices (Renewables, NGLs)" (EPM-DEC-001-0100, item 1).
  • 2026-10-25 (evening): the item F kpi, market-data and data-product releases also carry the qSKOS fixes EPM-DEC-001-0098 gives their modules (item 7 there); the system, integration and party fixes ship in item G's releases: kpi its disjoint label and two schemes' top concepts, market-data its four schemes' top concepts, data-product its segments' and dp:PortfolioStatus's top concepts and the four line labels.
  • integration: the amended consumer rule (item 6) with its tests, the data-product sources registered at tier 5 (item 14), and any pins that move.

Known gaps#

  • Completeness of series. Only the named series carry definitions, legs and regions; the rest are known only through the views' columns (item 5).
  • The benchmark view's scaling defect. The view multiplies all five instruments by 100, which is right for NYMEX Heating Oil (752) and RBOB (9471), converting to cents per gallon, but wrong for ICE Brent (572), NYMEX WTI Cushing (747) and NYMEX Natural Gas (750); the Executive Summary agent divides those three back. Recorded as a known gap for the data product's release note.
  • The benchmark view's 160-day window. It shows only the last 160 days, so longer trends cannot be answered from it.
  • The KPI ID's lineage. The crack-spread gold table's KPI_ID is a constant with no silver source; it comes only from the legacy table that item 10 leaves out of the record.
  • Validated, not read. The crack-spread gold table's joins were checked against the data, because the build job's code could not be read; the Executive Summary's joins and derivations come from column lineage only (item 9).
  • Reads with no column flowing. The crack-spread gold build reads mips_price_batch_dim and mips_price_assessment_fact without any of their columns reaching gold; their role is not known.
  • The lineage's reach. It covers the last 180 days of Unity Catalog lineage, and the replica tables are the top of what it records.
  • Freshness. On 2026-10-25 the newest crack-spread price in gold was from 2026-10-08, and the job last wrote to the table on 2026-10-12.

Alternatives considered#

  • Classify the pricing measures as KPIs. Not chosen: the decision owner kept them as market indicators (item 3), and none has a target or an accountable owner, which EPM-DEC-001-0042 requires of a KPI.
  • Bind both crack-spread sources. Not chosen: the decision owner made the crack-spread view the source of anything crack spread (item 1); two bound sources for one measure would leave a consumer to choose.
  • List every series in market-data. Not chosen: the decision owner proposed naming the columns that list them (item 5), so the module does not change each time a series is added.
  • Record the legacy Synapse reads. Not chosen: the decision owner left them out (item 10).
  • Treat replica tables and Genie agents as data products. Not chosen: a replica copies a system's own records, which the sources researched keep apart from data products; an agent consumes data products (item 14).

EPM-DEC-001-0098: Dispositions for the qSKOS findings on the released modules: preferred labels shared across schemes are accepted, seven process labels shared inside core's scheme are accepted until a process-map proposal, and every other finding is fixed in its module's next release#

From EPM-DEC-001-0098-qskos-findings-dispositions.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-25, for accepting preferred labels shared across schemes; the other dispositions delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-25
QuestionsStep 3's rule for checks adopted after release: what happens to each finding the ontology skill's qSKOS checks make on the newest release of each module? Should EPM rename the seven preferred labels it shares across schemes, or should the skill exempt them?
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-25; answers-2026-10-25-qskos.md

Context#

personal-agent-skills #53 added the qSKOS quality issues that an offline build can check to the ontology skill's check_taxonomy.py. The ontology playbook's Step 3 says: "When you adopt a check on modules already released, record what it finds in them and give each finding a disposition by recorded decision (fix it in the next release, or accept it with a reason); a finding in new work blocks the gate." EPM's modules do not run the skill's checker, so nothing failed, but the worklog recorded that this build did not yet meet that part of the gate (ontology-worklog.md, the Step 3 "Done when" row of the evidence for the skill capabilities added on 2026-10-24 and 25).

On 2026-10-25 Claude reran the checker read-only on the newest release of each of the eleven modules (core 2.3.0, data-product 1.1.0, integration 2.0.0, kpi 1.1.1, lane 1.0.0, market-data 1.0.0, organization 2.0.0, party 1.1.0, ref-mpc 1.2.0, system 1.0.0, value-stream 1.1.0), once per module. It ran the checker as of #53 and as of personal-agent-skills #69, merge 056d984, which exempts the shared preferred labels. Counted as before, findings new with #53 only, it found:

Check (qSKOS issue)FindingsWhere
scheme (Omitted Top Concepts)2710 modules; every one is a flat code list, and no member is stated as a top concept
reciprocal (Unidirectionally Related Concepts)109party 106, across 19 schemes; data-product 3, the segments in dp:DataProducts. Each is a skos:topConceptOf with no skos:hasTopConcept the other way
collision (Overlapping Labels)117 preferred labels shared across schemes; 4 alternative labels in core that equal another concept's preferred label
disjoint-labels (Disjoint Labels Violation)1kpi, measure:rm-margin-indicator
uri-scheme0

The worklog recorded 28 scheme findings. Both versions of the checker find 27 on the same releases, so 27 is the count this record uses, and the worklog row gets a dated note.

Decision#

The decision owner's decision: accept the preferred labels EPM shares across schemes. Claude asked whether EPM should rename the seven, or the skill should exempt shared labels, and recommended exempting them "when each scheme is a different view", with same-scheme collisions still failing. The decision owner: "I agree . But can you explain why this is the best route." The skill now carries the exemption (personal-agent-skills #69, merge 056d984).

The dispositions (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028, from the skill's own rules):

  1. Seven preferred labels shared across schemes: accepted, no change. Finance, IT Services, Marketing and Refining are each a business domain (core:BusinessDomain) and a process (the mod:core scheme) in core. Asphalt is a line of business and a product, Cardlock a line of business and a site type, and Prospect a lifecycle stage and a system stage, in party. Each label is the right business term in its scheme, and identity is the IRI. The checker as of #69 no longer reports them.
  2. Four alternative labels in core: fixed in core's next release. The process map gives two pairs of processes the same names, and core 2.3.0 renamed the second pair's preferred labels, keeping the process map's name as an alternative label. Each alternative label now equals the other concept's preferred label:
    ConceptAlternative labelThe same text is the preferred label of
    CM-1-3-2-3-1 Test Consumer Value PropositionTest Customer value propositionCM-1-3-2-2-1
    CM-1-3-2-3-2 Formulate and Evaluate Consumer Value Proposition AlternativesFormulate and Evaluate Strategic Operating Alternatives (CVP)CM-1-3-2-2-2
    CM-1-3-2-3-3 Establish Consumer Value Proposition Principles and ObjectivesEstablish CVP principles and objectivesCM-1-3-2-2-3
    CM-1-3-2-3-4 Develop and Update Consumer Value Proposition StrategyDevelop/Update Strategy (CVP)CM-1-3-2-2-4

    The next core release moves each of these names from skos:altLabel to a history annotation, so the process map's name is still recorded. The skill makes an earlier name an alias "only if it is unique, accurate and useful" and keeps "other former names in a history annotation" (identity-taxonomy.md, Step 3, rule 8): a search on a shared alias finds two processes.

  3. One disjoint-labels finding in kpi: fixed in kpi's next release. measure:rm-margin-indicator has "R&M Margin Indicator" as both its preferred and an alternative label, which SKOS forbids (SKOS Reference, integrity condition S13). The next kpi release drops the alternative label. That release is already planned for item F (EPM-DEC-001-0097, the narrowing of Additional Market Prices).
  4. 109 one-way top concepts: fixed in party's and data-product's next releases. Each scheme states skos:hasTopConcept for every concept that states skos:topConceptOf it. The meaning does not change, since SKOS defines the two as inverses.
  5. 27 schemes with no top concept: fixed in each module's next release. Each of these code lists is flat: no member has a parent in its scheme. The next release states every member as a top concept, both ways:
    • core: core:ApqcMappingDecision, core:BusinessDomain, core:Cadence, core:ConceptKind, core:EvidenceBases, core:GovernanceStatus, core:HoldStatus, core:LifecycleStatus, core:OperatingMode, core:PlanningLevel, core:ScopeUnits;
    • data-product: dp:PortfolioStatus;
    • integration: auth:EvidenceTier, con:RuleKind, plc:PlacementKind;
    • kpi: kpi:MeasureKind, kpi:Measures;
    • lane: lane:OfficeLanes;
    • market-data: mkt:BenchmarkKind, mkt:MarketRegions, mkt:RefiningRegions, mkt:Series;
    • organization: orgm:RaciLevel;
    • party: party:customer-systems;
    • system: sys:SystemKind, sys:Systems;
    • value-stream: vs:MeasureRelationship.

    The skill treats a code list as "a flat scheme of top concepts by design" (quality.md, "The qSKOS issues"). 2026-10-26: lane 1.1.0, organization 2.1.0 and value-stream 1.2.0 carry the fixes for lane:OfficeLanes, orgm:RaciLevel and vs:MeasureRelationship (EPM-DEC-001-0106).

Each release that carries one of these fixes runs the skill's check_taxonomy.py on its own module, and the fixed findings must be gone. None of these fixes changes an IRI or a definition.

Decided after the first draft (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028, amendment of 2026-10-25, evening)#

  1. Four preferred labels shared inside dp:DataProducts: fixed in data-product's next release. The portfolio gives a data-product line and a data product the same short name: HR, IT, S&BD and SC (supply chain). The line takes its short name followed by "product line" (for example "HR product line"), and the product keeps the portfolio's short name. The line's old label goes to a history annotation, not an alternative label, which would collide again. Identity is the IRI, and the skill leaves how a label is made distinct to the modeller (quality.md, the Z39.19 row on qualifiers, which the skill does not adopt). Claude's choice: the line is the grouping and the product is what consumers ask for by name, so the product keeps the portfolio's short name and the line takes the qualifier.
  2. Item F's releases carry their modules' fixes. kpi 1.2.0: items 3 and 5 (the disjoint label, kpi:MeasureKind and kpi:Measures). market-data 1.1.0: item 5 (its four schemes). data-product 1.2.0: items 4 to 6 (the segments' skos:hasTopConcept, dp:PortfolioStatus, the four labels). system and integration carry theirs in the item G releases (system 1.1.0, integration 2.1.0, with party 1.2.0). Core, lane, organization and value-stream carry theirs in their next releases, which no queue item plans yet. 2026-10-26: lane 1.1.0, organization 2.1.0 and value-stream 1.2.0 carry theirs (EPM-DEC-001-0106), and core's land in core 2.4.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0105).
  3. Seven preferred labels shared inside core's process scheme: accepted, with a reason, until a process-map proposal settles them. The same run reports 11 preferred labels shared inside one scheme, found by the per-scheme collision check that predates #53. Seven are in core's process scheme, where the process map gives more than one process the same name: under different parents for six (Analyze Data, Define Analysis Objectives & Scope, Determine Taxability, Document Findings, Obtain latest XPIMS MOP model, Source & Collect Information), and a process and its own child for Actualizations (CM 1.2.3 and CM 1.2.3.1); the other four are item 6. Core takes process names from the process map, which is the authority (AGENTS.md), so renaming them needs a process-map proposal, not a core release. Each process has its own IRI and its place in the hierarchy, so the shared name is ambiguous only in a search by label. The night's report lists them as a candidate proposal. Once a proposal is accepted, the next core release applies it. 2026-10-26: the decision owner accepted MPC-P12, which names each of the 25 processes by its parent, and core 2.4.0 applies it (EPM-DEC-001-0105).

Alternatives considered#

  • Rename the seven shared preferred labels in EPM. Not chosen by the decision owner. Each is the right term in its own scheme, and a qualifier would make a business term read like a technical one.
  • Accept the findings of items 2 to 6 with a reason instead of fixing them. Not chosen: each fix adds or moves a statement without changing any meaning, and each finding is one the skill's checks fail in new work.

The decision owner's recorded words#

From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-25-qskos.md: "I agree . But can you explain why this is the best route."

Evidence#

  • The newest release of each module under business_architecture/ontology/releases/.
  • check_taxonomy.py in the ontology skill as of personal-agent-skills #53 (merge 4dc0d91) and #69; references/quality.md, "The qSKOS issues"; references/identity-taxonomy.md, Step 3, rule 8.
  • business_architecture/business_process/downstream_process_map.json, the names of CM 1.3.2.2 and CM 1.3.2.3's subprocesses.
  • W3C, SKOS Simple Knowledge Organization System Reference, Recommendation, 18 August 2009, https://www.w3.org/TR/skos-reference/: S13 (the preferred, alternative and hidden labels are pairwise disjoint) and the inverse pair skos:hasTopConcept and skos:topConceptOf.

Context#

system 1.0.0 names 32 systems and defines none: each carries a note that its definition waits for its owners (EPM-DEC-001-0055). party 1.1.0 names 42 customer systems, each with a one-line customer role from the decision owner's inputs and no definition (EPM-DEC-001-0069). The same day they identified APS and six other systems and approved four descriptions.
