Decision#

The decision owner's answers, item by item (the questions are in the context comment of answers-2026-10-25-item-g.md):

  1. A vendor's description of its own product is evidence tier 4, industry sources (EPM-DEC-001-0059). It says what the product is in general and nothing about how MPC uses it, so it never outranks MPC's sources or the process map.
  2. The process map's "SAP" is SAP S/4HANA, the same system as party's SAP S/4HANA, linked by skos:exactMatch.
  3. XPIMS and PPIMS are Aspen PIMS planning models: XPIMS multi-plant and multi-period, PPIMS multi-period. Each is defined as an Aspen PIMS model, not as a separate product.
  4. PI is the AVEVA PI System.
  5. Hyperion is Oracle Hyperion Planning. Claude's recommendation called this a guess between Planning, Financial Management and Essbase; the system keeps a note saying so.
  6. TopDay and VALT are MPC-built until the decision owner says otherwise. Neither has a definition yet.
  7. Polaris is a Marathon Pipeline LLC scheduling system, built or bought unknown, from PHMSA's notice of amendment of 2024-09-26. 2026-10-28: the decision owner believes it is bought; the vendor stays unknown (amendment of 2026-10-28).
  8. Drop Tank, Gas Buddy, DTN File Server and Federal Health go to the customer-data owner as one question (business-closeout.md, point 11). None is defined. 2026-10-28: Drop Tank, GasBuddy and the DTN File Server are defined from their vendors' own published words (Drop Tank's from a 2018 press release, its site being gone) in party 2.1.0; Federal Health, and what MPC sends the four, still go to the customer-data owner (amendment of 2026-10-28).
  9. Terminal Upload's direct flow to KittyHawk stays, because the decision owner's input shows it; KittyHawk's note says its customer data comes from RightAngle and Terminal Upload sends it cross-reference IDs.
  10. Claude drafts a description of each internal system from the repository (its process links or its customer role) for one approval round with the decision owner, as was done for DPO.
  11. The customer processes' system links go to a process-map proposal (MPC-P11), after the decision owner checks the weak ones.

The identifications of 2026-10-24 (answers-2026-10-24-systems.md): APS is Aspen Petroleum Scheduler; CargoTracker, MAGIC, Allocation Manager and DPO are MPC-built, and DPO's name is "Distribution Planning Optimizer"; Quorum is Quorum Software, product not named; OneSource is Thomson Reuters ONESOURCE; KittyHawk is an MPC in-house application used by the Commercial organization, fed from RightAngle. The decision owner approved the descriptions of CargoTracker, MAGIC, Allocation Manager and DPO.

EPM-DEC-001-0100: Item F's five releases: kpi 1.2.0, market-data 1.1.0, data-product 1.2.0, system 1.2.0 and integration 2.2.0#

From EPM-DEC-001-0100-item-f-releases.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-25, for the release plan; the build choices delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-25
QuestionsHow do the decisions of EPM-DEC-001-0097 and the qSKOS dispositions of EPM-DEC-001-0098 ship?
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-25; answers-2026-10-25-queue-delegation.md

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0097 records the decision owner's decisions on the pricing measures, their metric views and Genie agents, and lists five releases under "What follows". EPM-DEC-001-0098 gives each qSKOS finding a disposition, and its item 7 routes kpi's, market-data's and data-product's fixes to these releases. On 2026-10-25 Claude put the plan of the five releases to the decision owner, and they chose "Approve the plan (Recommended)".

Decision#

The decision owner's decision: release the five as planned.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028): the build choices, each from the evidence order the amendment of 2026-10-25 (evening) names.

  1. kpi 1.2.0. Crack Spreads Pricing carries dcterms:identifier "KPI-0001" and a skos:note on the gold table's constant (0097, item 3). Additional Market Prices is now "Additional Market Prices (Renewables, NGLs)": label, definition and classification reason change, and a skos:changeNote keeps the planner list's name; the slug stays (0097, item 4). Narrowing a definition while keeping its IRI and kind is a minor change here: no released package used the measure for benchmarks or crude, and the crude and benchmark prices move to Crude Market Pricing, whose definition already named them. R&M Margin Indicator loses its repeated alias, and the build refuses one (0098, item 3).
  2. market-data 1.1.0. A new class, mkt:MarketPrice, names the prices the views report for the other measures, linked by mkt:priceOf, because mkt:seriesOf is the crack series' property and its domain would make each price a crack series. Where a view's price has the same name and market as a benchmark already in the module (LA CARBOB, USGC CBOB, USGC ULSD, and NYMEX WTI 747), that benchmark gains the link instead of a second concept. The benchmark view's other four instruments get labels with their Price Master ID, as MPC Economics' inputs do. mkt:SeriesEnumeration records 0097 item 5's columns, with the view's full name as a string, since market-data requires only kpi. Two NYMEX cracks the files name without legs (44934, 44935) join as series with legs unstated.
  3. data-product 1.2.0. The portfolio file, which is process authority, does not change. The pricing tables and views are built from data-product/pricing/ into pricing.ttl, as dp:PlatformTable, and the data products among them as dp:PlatformDataProduct, not dp:DataProduct, whose shapes require a portfolio line and status. 0097's plan named the tables as DCAT distributions; item 14 replaced that with classified data products, so they are platform tables. Replica tables are recorded one by one where the lineage names them, and as no data products (item 14).
  4. The binding of a view to its measures is dp:servesMeasure, stated in data-product. 0097's plan named con:publishesMeasure, whose domain is the portfolio's dp:DataProduct and which lives in integration, which requires data-product; a binding there would type each view as a portfolio data product. The consumer rule relies on dp:servesMeasure instead.
  5. Catalogs and schemas are parts of each table's full name, not systems. The system module's kinds are the process map's (application, office tool, working document), and a schema is a storage location, not a system. Databricks itself is a system, of kind application.
  6. system 1.2.0. MIPS and Databricks come from other-systems.csv, named by the decision owner's pricing assets, not by the process map, so they support no process. MIPS's alternative label "Market Information and Pricing System" is the Executive Summary agent's expansion. MPR gains a definition from the same files, with a note that they expand MPR both as Master Price Record and as Market Price Report. Databricks' definition comes from its documentation, marked as the vendor's, and quoted whole in reference/vendors/.
  7. integration 2.2.0. rule:value-through-bound-view is a new may-answer rule; rule:no-values keeps refusing a computed value and a value from an unbound source, with a change note (0097, item 6). The decision owner's pricing assets are tier 1, as the decision owner's own material; Databricks' documentation is tier 4; the six kind sources are tier 5 (0097, item 14).
  8. Known gaps go in the changelogs of market-data and data-product, from 0097's list.
  9. The series scheme names what it now holds. Because the 40 new market prices sit in mkt:Series, its label becomes "market series and prices" (it was "benchmark series") and its definition names the market prices; the IRI is unchanged and a change note keeps the old label. A minor change, in market-data 1.1.0.

Known gaps#

  • The 40 new market prices have no definition from the decision owner's files. Each definition is built from the Executive Summary or benchmark agent's filters, which name the price and its group; each price carries an editorial note saying so, and the market-data 1.1.0 changelog lists it as a known gap.
  • The other gaps are 0097's, carried into the changelogs (item 8).

Alternatives considered#

  • Hold the releases until production names exist. Not chosen: the decision owner said to mark the sandbox names (0097, item 7).
  • Put the bindings in integration with con:publishesMeasure. Not chosen, for item 4's reason.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-25-queue-delegation.md: "Approve the plan (Recommended)".

Evidence#

  • EPM-DEC-001-0097 and the decision owner's pricing assets it cites (held outside the repository).
  • EPM-DEC-001-0098, item 7.
  • reference/vendors/README.md, the Databricks row.

Consequences#

  • The packages are in releases/kpi/1.2.0/, releases/market-data/1.1.0/, releases/data-product/1.2.0/, releases/system/1.2.0/ and releases/integration/2.2.0/; released packages do not change.

EPM-DEC-001-0101: The ontology playbook moves into the ontology skill as its guide, with three new sections#

From EPM-DEC-001-0101-ontology-playbook-to-skill.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-25, for the move and the three sections; how each is applied delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028, amendment of 2026-10-25, evening)
Date2026-10-25
QuestionsItem E: where does the ontology playbook live, and what does it add on modules, the integration module and deciding between sources?
SourceClaude Code sessions of 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25; answers-2026-10-25-item-e.md

Context#

The ontology playbook (business_architecture/ontology/ontology-playbook.md) has been a domain-neutral method since 2026-10-23, and epm_facts.py playbook kept it free of EPM records, companies and paths. The ontology skill in personal-agent-skills runs the same method as a gated procedure, with the rules in its references, so the two documents stated many rules twice. On 2026-10-24 the decision owner asked for three additions: the integration-module pattern, how to decide between sources, and why to split an ontology into modules. On 2026-10-25 they asked whether the playbook should move into the skill, Claude recommended it, and the decision owner's queue for the session reads: "Item E: the three playbook sections, and move the ontology playbook into the ontology skill."

Decision#

The decision owner's words are quoted below.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028, amendment of 2026-10-25, evening):

  1. The playbook becomes the ontology skill's narrative guide, skills/data/ontology/references/playbook.md in personal-agent-skills (#71, merged as a2b8d1a on 2026-10-25). Each step names the reference that holds its rules, and the guide points to them instead of restating them; where the two differ, the reference wins.
  2. The three sections are written in the guide, in the skill's terms, each pointing to the rule it explains: "Why split an ontology into modules" (§2, beside the module policy), "Deciding between sources" (§2, after source of truth and scope) and "The integration-module pattern" (end of Step 7), with one worked case in the guide's library-lending domain.
  3. The skill's consumer rule follows EPM-DEC-001-0097, item 6: an agent may quote a measure's value taken through the source the catalog binds to that measure, naming it; computing a value from the graph and quoting from an unbound source stay refused.
  4. The neutrality guard moves with the guide as the skill's scripts/test_playbook_guide.py, with its fixed list of terms and a test that each kind is caught. The checks that read EPM's released packages and tracked paths stay behind, because the skill cannot see them; review catches the rest, as REVIEW_STANDARDS.md rule 5 says. epm_facts.py playbook, its tests and its check.sh line are removed.
  5. EPM deletes its copy. Live documents point to the skill's guide; the worklog's §8 evidence map stays and illustrates the guide section by section. Released packages and historical records that cite ontology-playbook.md keep their citations, because a released package never changes and a record states what was true when it was written.

Alternatives considered#

  • Keep the playbook in EPM and copy it to the skill. Not chosen: two copies of one method drift, and the skill already holds the rules.
  • Keep the guard's package and path checks in the skill. Not chosen: they read one repository's releases and files, which a general skill cannot see.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-25-item-e.md: "can you add this and the decision tier to the plan to be added to the Playbook? Even though the playbook is generic... these are great exmaple of how to think about an Integration module and how to make decisions on sources. Also add a section in the playbook about why it is important to vreak an ontology into modules like we did here."; "4. Should we move to he ontology skills itesef since the Playbook is a guide on how to create an ontology. what do you think?"; "Item E: the three playbook sections, and move the ontology playbook into the ontology skill."

Evidence#

  • personal-agent-skills #71 (merge a2b8d1a), branch ontology/playbook-guide: references/playbook.md, references/integration.md, scripts/test_playbook_guide.py, SKILL.md, README.md.
  • The skill's rules the new sections explain: foundations.md, policies 3 and 8; integration.md, rules 25 to 28.

Consequences#

  • scripts/check.sh no longer runs a playbook check; the skill's scripts/check.sh runs the guide's test.
  • REVIEW_STANDARDS.md rule 5 and the ontology README name the guide's new home.

EPM-DEC-001-0102: The synthetic pricing demo in Databricks Free Edition: real public data first, labelled rows, the shared assets' names#

From EPM-DEC-001-0102-pricing-demo-databricks-free.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-25: a realistic synthetic demo in Free Edition kept in this repository, real data where it is public, Postgres source systems with DuckDB, the replica, star-schema, gold and metric-view layers, the briefings without the mention of ChatGPT, no need to match the O2C demo, and the generic system names; everything else delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028, amendment of 2026-10-25, evening)
Date2026-10-25
QuestionsItem H: how is the demo built, what is real and what is synthesized, and what enters the ontology?
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-25; answers-2026-10-25-item-h.md

Context#

The decision owner asked for "a realistic synthetic data to demonstrate this in Databrick Free edition" once the ontology was complete, with the pricing assets' names and columns (EPM-DEC-001-0097, item 17: "We have the freedom when using synthetic data in Free databrick edition to shape it as we want . as long as it ihas the same shape as the one shared"). Their item H answers (answers-2026-10-25-item-h.md): keep it in this repository as another demo; Postgres source systems, DuckDB as needed, a replica, a star schema, gold and metric views; "the new demo does not have to mathc it"; "Let us use real data where they are available in the public"; "Remove any mention of ChatGPT"; and the generic system names ("they work"). Which public sources to use, how rows are labelled, the scenario's relabelling and the Genie agents are Claude's choices, below. The demo is demos/downstream-pricing-databricks-free/.

Decision#

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028, amendment of 2026-10-25, evening; none of it is the decision owner's words):

  1. Free Edition supports the demo. Its limits page (docs.databricks.com, "Databricks Free Edition limitations", updated 2026-10-18) lists no restriction on metric views or Genie, and restricts outbound internet to trusted domains. The run of 2026-10-25 created the idp_sbx catalog, four metric views (YAML 1.1, with joins and filters) and two Genie agents through the Genie API.
  2. The public sources, and a label on every row. Claude chose EIA daily spot prices and the Weekly Petroleum Status Report, cached with their series IDs and the date pulled, and MPC's 13 refineries and capacities from its FY2025 Form 10-K. A row is real (an EIA value on its own date, or one converted only in unit), spot-anchored (a formula of EIA values only, such as a front month or a basis, or an EIA value carried from an earlier date) or synthesized (anything with a generated part). EIA's NYMEX futures table states "Futures prices after April 5, 2024, are not available" (release of 2026-10-21), so a benchmark's front month is its spot price; EIA publishes no New York Harbor RBOB spot, only conventional regular gasoline there (spot price table, release of 2026-10-21), so the demo's NYMEX RBOB front month uses that series.
  3. Not used: EPA RIN prices and NOAA tracks. EPA publishes RIN prices only as an interactive dashboard, with no file to cite, so RINs and the RVO are synthesized. Hurricane Isaias comes from the scenario, so its effects are synthesized.
  4. EIA wins over the scenario. The briefings' weekly balances for 2026-09-25 match EIA; their WTI and Brent futures settlements for 2026-09-30, October 8, 9 and 16 differ from EIA's spot prices, and the demo uses EIA. The README lists each. The scenario keeps the decision owner's briefings as written, with the line naming where they were produced removed, as asked, and "FACT" relabelled "Scenario", which is Claude's choice, so a reader does not take a scenario figure for a fact.
  5. The shared assets' names, columns, joins and filters, including the benchmark view's defect (all five instruments times 100) and its 160-day window, so the ontology's known gaps can be seen in the demo. Three differences, stated in the README: the price detail's high and low prices are left empty, because the demo generates only the settle; the crack view has no Crack_Spread_Sum (as the bound view lacks it); and the MIPS assessment fact carries its batch. Names keep the shared assets' spelling even where it is a typo: the decision owner's lineage file for the pricing assets (an item F input, kept out of the repository by EPM-DEC-001-0067) lists the MPR price master dimension's facility column as Facitlity_Id, so silver_mdm_core.mpr_price_master_dim carries that name, and a query that names this column runs here unchanged. The three crude series the shared system stores two months early (Price Master IDs 30532, 79068, 79069) are generated with that lag, so the agent's two-month shift applies as it does to the shared view.
  6. One Postgres server with one database per source system, in local Docker and reachable only from this machine (2026-10-28: also on an exe.dev VM, under public names; amendment of 2026-10-28 below); the replication reads SOURCE_PG_DSN, so the databases can move to another host. A paid host costs money, which stays the decision owner's choice. Each replication run is a full snapshot with a manifest, which the upload and the build check.
  7. The Genie agents' instructions are written for the demo from the decision owner's description of the two agents; the decision owner's files stay outside the repository (EPM-DEC-001-0067).
  8. No new measure enters kpi. Under the amendment of 2026-10-25 (evening), a measure from the briefings needs an MPC or U.S. refining peer definition. For the five candidates (inventory coverage, product netback versus target, margin at risk, freight cost per barrel, distillate availability), none is defined in the repository's MPC evidence (business_architecture/reference/mpc/) or in Valero's or Phillips 66's FY2025 Form 10-K (searched 2026-10-25). Netback appears only in industry sources (Platts, Haverly; reference/downstream-peers/kpi-classification-evidence.md). All five stay candidates.

Amendment, 2026-10-28: the source databases move to an exe.dev VM, under public names#

The decision owner had already created a Postgres VM on exe.dev ("I have already created a vm for postgress on exe.dev"), so the hosting choice in decision 6 is made.

The decision owner's decisions:

  1. Move the demo there and keep the local container: "Move demo there, keep local (Recommended)".
  2. Six databases owned by a demo login, with public names where they exist: "Option 1. Also can we use a puckly available name for the systems. Use RightAngle instead of Kittyhawk, chnae mips, mpr, and proacct if this is a system name form this session".
  3. Rename the databases and replica schemas only: "Databases and replicas only (Recommended)". The silver and gold tables keep the shared asset's names, so its queries run unchanged, and the ontology keeps the real system names.
  4. Drop the old replica schemas: "drop tehm".

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  • The databases are rightangle (was ctrm), aspen_pims (was planning), reference_data_hub (was kittyhawk), price_assessment (was mips), price_reference (was mpr) and production_accounting (was prodacct). RightAngle cannot also name the KittyHawk database, because the CTRM database is RightAngle's, so KittyHawk takes a generic name.
  • On the VM, databases.exe.xyz, Postgres listens only on the VM's localhost, so the demo reaches it through an SSH tunnel. A new login role demo owns the six databases (2026-10-28, later: seven, with eia_weekly, PR E1). Its password is generated, kept only in the demo's local .env, and never printed. The VM's other databases are untouched.
  • The copy holds 17 tables with row counts equal to the local container's, 167,804 rows in all. The full pipeline ran from the VM on 2026-10-28 with the same counts in every layer, except the benchmark view, whose 160-day window moves with the date (4,173 rows that day). 2026-10-28, later: after the three-year extension (PR E1) the copy holds 22 tables, and the full pipeline ran again from the VM with 46 objects counting the same as the local container's; the benchmark view held 4,134 rows, counted with Databricks' CURRENT_DATE() already at 2026-10-29 (UTC). demos/downstream-pricing-databricks-free/DATA.md gives every table's count. On this machine the local databases were renamed in place (ALTER DATABASE … RENAME TO), so the fallback works unchanged. A volume created before 2026-10-28 elsewhere still holds the old names, because the image runs its initialization scripts, which create the databases, only on an empty data directory (Docker Official Image postgres, "Initialization scripts", read 2026-10-28); the README's run steps give the one-time rename.
  • The six old replica schemas and their landing-volume folders were removed from the demo's catalog idp_sbx.

Alternatives considered#

  • Synthesize every price. Not chosen: the decision owner asked for real data where it is public.
  • Host the source databases in Databricks Lakebase. Not chosen: the decision owner asked for Postgres source systems replicated with DuckDB, and Free Edition allows one Lakebase project, which the O2C demo already uses.

EPM-DEC-001-0104: MPC-P11 is accepted: the customer processes name the systems they use, with the strong and medium evidence, and the weak links stay out; released as system 1.4.0 and party 1.4.0#

From EPM-DEC-001-0104-mpc-p11-accepted.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-26, for accepting the proposal; how it is applied, and the two minor releases, delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-26
QuestionsProposal MPC-P11 (business_architecture/business_process/proposals/MPC-P11-customer-process-systems.md; EPM-DEC-001-0099, item 11; EPM-DEC-001-0103)
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-26; answers-2026-10-26-night.md

Context#

The system module links a system to a process only where the process map's systems field names it (EPM-DEC-001-0055). No customer-lifecycle process named a system, so the lifecycle reached Salesforce, SAP MDG and SAP S/4HANA only through its system stages (EPM-DEC-001-0070). EPM-DEC-001-0103 wrote proposal MPC-P11 from each customer system's role and data flows in party, and left it for the decision owner, because a change to the process map needs an accepted proposal (AGENTS.md).

Decision#

The decision owner's ruling: accept the proposal's "Proposed (strong and medium evidence)" table, and leave out its "Not proposed (weak evidence)" table.

The table has 18 rows. Two rows name more than one system (CM 1.3.7.1.7 with SAP MDG and MAGIC, and with Salesforce, SAP S/4HANA and RightAngle), so the 16 single-system rows and those two hold 21 links, across 12 processes. The option the decision owner chose said "Add all 19 proposed links"; that count was wrong, and this record counts the links themselves.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The process map's systems field of each of the 12 processes gains its systems, in downstream_process_map.json, under the names the system module's register reads: Salesforce, SAP MDG, MAGIC, Bridger Insight, Information Steward, Profisee, Sirion, Customer Portal, SAP S/4HANA and RightAngle.
  2. The system module adds the seven systems the map did not name before: SAP MDG, Salesforce, MAGIC, Bridger Insight, Information Steward, Sirion and Customer Portal. Each takes the slug, label and definition the party module already gives it (EPM-DEC-001-0099, 0103), so the two modules describe each system once, in the same words. "SAP S/4HANA" is a new register string for the existing system sap (EPM-DEC-001-0099), and Profisee and RightAngle were already in the module.
  3. The weak links stay out: Allocation Manager for CM 1.3.7.4.3, SAP S/4HANA for CM 1.3.7.4.5 and Data Products IDP for CM 1.3.7.4.4. Each can come back through a new proposal when its owner confirms it.
  4. The proposal's status becomes Accepted, with this record.
  5. system 1.4.0, a minor release, carries the seven systems and the 21 links. It adds IRIs and removes none, so a minor is enough. It requires core 2.4.0, the current release, which holds all 12 processes (EPM-DEC-001-0105); the system build reads 2.4.0 since that release. 39 of its 41 systems are defined, and 306 links reach 162 of the 165 processes with a systems field.
  6. party 1.4.0, a minor release, carries seven more skos:exactMatch links, one from each of the seven customer systems to the system system of the same slug, so 12 customer systems match. The party build states a match wherever the system module holds a customer system's slug (party/build_party.py), so the two releases go together. It requires core 2.4.0 and system 1.4.0. EPM-DEC-001-0105 left the party build on core 2.3.0 until party's next release, because its output names the core version; party 1.4.0 is that release, so its build now reads core 2.4.0. The only change in party's output is that name in lifecycle.ttl's header: every lifecycle link's process IRI is in both releases.
  7. party 1.4.0 rewords its one shape message that held an apostrophe, so no sh:message in party's shapes holds one. The rule applies to every module; this record covers party only. EPM-DEC-001-0106 reworded lane's message and records that market-data 1.1.0 still holds one, for its next release. Party's: "A customer system's definition cites its source." becomes "The definition of a customer system cites its source." The meaning does not change. The ontology skill's SHACL rule 19 keeps apostrophes out of sh:message, because the neosemantics (n10s) validator writes the message into Cypher unescaped (personal-agent-skills #72).
  8. Claude approves both releases under the delegation. EPM-DEC-001-0028 lets Claude approve the queue's minor releases, and the decision owner's night brief of 2026-10-26, which says Claude approves the night's minor releases, was approved with "Approve (Recommended)" (answers-2026-10-26-night.md, block 7).

Alternatives considered#

  • Accept the strong rows only. Not chosen by the decision owner.
  • Reject the proposal. Not chosen: the lifecycle would keep reaching its systems only through its system stages.

The decision owner's recorded words#

Saved in business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-26-night.md, block 2: "Accept strong + medium (Recommended)". For the releases, block 7: "Approve (Recommended)", to a brief that says Claude approves the night's minor releases.

EPM-DEC-001-0105: MPC-P12 is accepted: the processes that share a name take their parent's subject; released with core's qSKOS fixes as core 2.4.0#

From EPM-DEC-001-0105-mpc-p12-core-2-4-0.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-26, for accepting MPC-P12; the release and how it applies the proposal delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-26
QuestionsEPM-DEC-001-0098, item 8: should the seven preferred labels shared inside core's process scheme be renamed through a process-map proposal? And which release carries core's deferred qSKOS fixes (EPM-DEC-001-0098, items 2, 5 and 7)?
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-26 (night); answers-2026-10-26-night.md; proposal MPC-P12 (business_architecture/business_process/proposals/MPC-P12-shared-process-names.md)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0098 accepted seven preferred labels shared inside core's process scheme "until a process-map proposal settles them" (item 8). The process map gives 25 processes one of those seven names, and core takes its labels from the map, which is the process authority (AGENTS.md). The same record left core's own qSKOS fixes, items 2 and 5, for "their next releases, which no queue item plans yet" (item 7).

On 2026-10-26 Claude put the proposal to the decision owner, with a preview of every rename, and recommended renaming by parent. The decision owner accepted it, and approved the night's brief, which plans core 2.4.0 as a minor release that Claude approves.

Decision#

The decision owner's decision: accept MPC-P12, renaming each shared process by its parent, as the preview listed.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. The map takes the 25 names in the proposal's table. Only the name field changes. IDs, parents and descriptions stay. The analysis steps name their parent analysis as the map names it, so "Customer And Marketer Analysis" keeps the map's capital A; "Analyze ... Data" names the subject only, as the preview did.
  2. A minor release, core 2.4.0, issued 2026-10-26, built by release/build_core_2_4.py from the released 2.3.0 graph and the map at commit 4906666, the commit that applied MPC-P12. It adds and removes no IRI and moves no concept. Claude approves it under the delegation ("Yes, delegate as described (Recommended)", EPM-DEC-001-0028, amendment of 2026-10-25, evening), as the night's brief planned.
  3. Each renamed concept keeps its old name as core:priorPreferredLabel, with a change note. Core defines that annotation for "generic, ambiguous, overstating or scoped former names", never skos:altLabel; an old name as an alternative label would still be shared, and the ontology skill keeps "other former names in a history annotation" (identity-taxonomy.md, Step 3, rule 8).
  4. Item 2 of EPM-DEC-001-0098 lands here: the four alternative labels of CM 1.3.2.3.1 to CM 1.3.2.3.4 move to core:priorPreferredLabel, with a change note.
  5. Item 5 of EPM-DEC-001-0098 lands here: every member of core's eleven flat code lists is skos:topConceptOf its list, and the list skos:hasTopConcept it. That is 51 members.
  6. The builder stops on any other map change since 2.3.0, except to the systems field, which core does not read. MPC-P11 changes that field in its own pull request, so either can merge first.
  7. The shapes and the canonical-fact mapping are 2.3.0's, unchanged. The package conforms to them: 19,975 triples and 707 process concepts.
  8. Repointing in the same pull request: the regression, the controlled-value and module-shape checks, the projection and the builds of the apqc, data-product, kpi, lane, system and value-stream modules read 2.4.0. Each of those builds checks only that a process IRI or ID is in core, and none of their outputs changed. The party build, whose output names the core version, moves with party's next release. 2026-10-26: party 1.4.0 is that release; its build reads 2.4.0 (EPM-DEC-001-0104). The released module packages keep the core version they were released against.
  9. Competency question Q38 still answers. Its query q38c-same-name-processes.rq listed the names more than one process shares; on 2.4.0 no two processes share a preferred label, so it now lists the names shared as a preferred or a prior preferred label. Each such name still belongs to different IRIs with different parents, which is the answer Q38 asks for.

The ontology skill's check_taxonomy.py (personal-agent-skills, as of merge 125c9e8) on 2.3.0 and on 2.4.0:

Check2.3.02.4.0
collision11 (7 shared preferred labels, 4 alternative labels)0
scheme11 (code lists with no top concept)0
parent51 (the code-list members: each had no parent and was not stated as a top concept)0
every other check1,192: untagged 1,130, notation 56, domain-range 3, definition 3, none of them a finding EPM-DEC-001-0098 disposes ofthe same 1,192

Alternatives considered#

  • Keep the shared names. Not chosen by the decision owner. Each process has its own IRI and parent, but a search by label, or a list without the hierarchy, cannot tell them apart.
  • Put the old names in skos:altLabel. Not chosen: each old name would still be shared, and the collision check would fail on it.
  • A separate core release for the qSKOS fixes. Not chosen: both change only labels and statements, and one minor release carries both.

QuestionsEPM-DEC-001-0098, item 7: "Core, lane, organization and value-stream carry theirs in their next releases, which no queue item plans yet." Which releases carry lane's, organization's and value-stream's fixes, and what else do they carry?
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-26 (night run); answers-2026-10-26-night.md (the decision owner approved the night's brief, which names qSKOS releases for these modules)

Context#

EPM-DEC-001-0098, item 5, found one code-list scheme with no top concept in each of these three modules: lane:OfficeLanes, orgm:RaciLevel and vs:MeasureRelationship. Each is flat: no member has a parent in its scheme. The ontology skill treats such a code list as "a flat scheme of top concepts by design" (quality.md, "The qSKOS issues"). Item 7 left these modules' fixes to releases that no queue item planned yet.

The ontology skill's SHACL rule 19 (personal-agent-skills #72) says a shape's sh:message must not hold a single quote, because n10s 2025.06.1 writes the message into Cypher unescaped and the quote breaks its validator. The rule names no module. The ask to reword party's and lane's messages comes from personal-agent-skills #72's pull request, where party 1.2.0's message broke the load, and from the night's brief.

Decision#

  1. Three minor releases, issued 2026-10-26 and approved by Claude under the delegation: lane 1.1.0, organization 2.1.0 and value-stream 1.2.0. None removes or renames an IRI or changes a definition, so none is a major.
  2. Top concepts both ways. Each member of the three schemes states skos:topConceptOf its scheme, and the scheme states skos:hasTopConcept for it: the 4 office lanes, the 4 RACI levels and the 3 measure relationships, 11 members in all. SKOS defines the two properties as inverses, so the meaning does not change.
  3. Lane's shape message is reworded. "A process's lane is one of the four office lanes." becomes "The lane of a process is one of the four office lanes." The organization and value-stream shapes hold no message with an apostrophe. Party's shapes also hold one such message (party/shapes.ttl: "A customer system's definition cites its source."). It is reworded in party's next release (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028); no party release is part of this change. 2026-10-26: party 1.4.0 rewords it (EPM-DEC-001-0104). Market-data 1.1.0's released shapes also hold one such message (releases/market-data/1.1.0/shapes.ttl, line 96: "A market price says its definition is built from the agents' filters."). It is reworded in market-data's next release (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028).
  4. Each release requires the current releases. Lane 1.1.0 requires core 2.3.0 (1.0.0 required 2.2.0), which its build already reads. Organization 2.1.0 requires core 2.3.0 (2.0.0 required 2.1.0). Value-stream 1.2.0 requires core 2.3.0, data-product 1.2.0 and kpi 1.2.0 (1.1.0 required data-product 1.0.0 and kpi 1.1.0). This follows value-stream 1.1.0, which required "the current releases" (EPM-DEC-001-0090). The released packages that pin the older versions stay as released: ref-mpc 1.2.0 still requires organization 2.0.0, and integration 2.3.0 still requires lane 1.0.0, organization 2.0.0 and value-stream 1.1.0. Their next releases can move the pins.
  5. The projection loads the new releases. projection/pipeline.py projects every module at its newest release (EPM-DEC-001-0036, amendment of 2026-10-22), so it now loads these three.

Core's fixes from EPM-DEC-001-0098 (items 2 and 5) are not in this record. They go in core's next release. 2026-10-26: core 2.4.0 carries them (EPM-DEC-001-0105).

Alternatives considered#

EPM-DEC-001-0108: The customer inputs of 2026-10-27: Dealer Master masters the ship-to stations, jobbers are mastered in SAP MDG and RightAngle, and party 2.0.0, system 1.5.0 and integration 2.4.0 carry them#

From EPM-DEC-001-0108-customer-inputs-2026-10-27.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-27, for the rulings, the 13 modeling questions and the releases; how each is applied delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-27
QuestionsWhat the decision owner's customer inputs of 2026-10-27 change in the party and system modules, how to model what they add, and which releases carry it
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-27; answers-2026-10-27-customer-inputs.md

Context#

On 2026-10-27 the decision owner shared four customer inputs, numbered 8 to 11 after the seven of EPM-DEC-001-0067:

  • Input 8: an image of how a new customer record is created, enriched and consumed across the applications, with 26 data flows.
  • Input 9: an image of the customer lifecycle and the five system stages of the six core systems, with each system's core and enrich fields.
  • Input 10: the notes of a meeting with Dealer Master's system expert on Dealer Master's business context and data flows.
  • Input 11: Dealer Master's architecture flow.

As with the earlier inputs, they are kept out of the repository, and this record states only what the ontology takes from them. Claude transcribed the two images, and the decision owner settled every point the transcriptions left open (block 1 to block 6 of the answers file). Claude then compared the inputs with party 1.4.0 and system 1.4.0: 113 items as the ontology holds them, 45 new, and 9 conflicting rows in 6 places, with 13 modeling questions the facts leave open. The comparison and the plan stay with the inputs.

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions:

  1. Jobbers are mastered in SAP MDG and RightAngle, not in Dealer Master. Input 9 says jobbers originate in Dealer Master; inputs 10 and 11 say Dealer Master does not create them. The decision owner chose the documents: Dealer Master holds a replica of each jobber to link it to its stations, and what Dealer Master originates and sends to SAP MDG is the ship-to station, which SAP MDG numbers in the 20000 series.
  2. The direct dealers that sync from SAP MDG into Dealer Master are the replicated six-digit sold-tos.
  3. SAP MDG and SAP S/4HANA stay two systems.
  4. TMT is its own system, as party holds it, and TMR is another. The decision owner first answered that the two are one, then, once Claude noted that party holds them apart, kept them apart.
  5. The six core systems are as party holds them; MAGIC is not one. Salesforce has 10 core fields. The images' repeated fields count once. Input 8's arrows from RightAngle to the Gravitate customer portal and from SAP MDG to Star are right, and its label for the new customer setup belongs to the arrow from Terminal Upload to Kittyhawk.
  6. All 13 of Claude's recommendations, and the releases they lead to. The decision owner aligned with the plan: party 2.0.0, a major release, so the decision owner's to approve; system 1.5.0 and an integration minor, Claude's under the delegation; no core change.

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Corrections, which change meaning. Dealer Master's role and definition no longer say it is built on Salesforce, which is only its intake, or that jobbers start there: it is the Retail Payments Application, the system of record for the ship-to stations. The flow from Dealer Master to SAP MDG carries ship-to stations, not jobbers; the flow from SAP MDG to Dealer Master carries the replicated sold-tos. The sourcing of direct dealers in SAP MDG is reworded to say the sold-tos are mastered in SAP MDG and RightAngle and replicated to Dealer Master. The prospect system stage's definition is corrected the same way. Each keeps its IRI and gains a dated skos:changeNote (party/change-notes.csv).
  2. One deprecation. The sourcing that says jobbers originate in Dealer Master is false, so it is deprecated, its triples kept, and replaced by a sourcing that says ship-to stations originate there. A sourcing can now name a site type.
  3. The 13 questions, as recommended:
    • (Q1) A ship-to stays a site, as EPM-DEC-001-0068 decided. A new property, party:siteIdentifiedBy, gives a site its external identifier, and a new identifier type holds the SAP ship-to number in the 20000 series. A new property, party:systemOfRecordFor, records Dealer Master as the system of record for sites and for the link from each jobber to its stations.
    • (Q2) Dealer Master's dealer classes 4, 2 and 9 are a code list, each value an exact match of the jobber role, the new sub-jobber role and the station site type. A record stays in the class it was created in.
    • (Q3) A new party role, sub-jobber, and a new relationship type, grouped under.
    • (Q4) The direct-dealer sourcing is reworded, and the direct-dealer customer class (C2) is kept.
    • (Q5) The sold-to role's scope note says it is also called the Capital C customer and is identified by a six-digit SAP customer number.
    • (Q6) The jobber-to-station link uses the existing supplies relationship and party:atSite, with Dealer Master as its system of record (Q1).
    • (Q7) TMR keeps its held descriptions; an editorial note gives the inputs' description, and which is right waits for its owner.
    • (Q8) PRM is a system of its own, not MPR. The channel of trade is the class of trade, with MST and DTW as the values of a new class-of-trade list.
    • (Q9) The third-party street price aggregator and the Enterprise Data Lake are systems; external vendors and Marketing Users are not modeled until they are named.
    • (Q10) The Brand Credit Card System gains the alternative label Brand Credit Card Master database.
    • (Q11) The flow from RightAngle to Terminal download is added, and the flow from SAP MDG is kept.
    • (Q12) Tax Image Silo's role says the customer supplies the certificates; no flow starts at the customer.
    • (Q13) Salesforce gains the core field Financial Statement; the held Financial Statement Request Frequency keeps its IRI and meaning.
  4. What else the inputs add: eight systems in both modules, each defined from the inputs in Claude's words (PRM, the station point-of-sale systems, the street price aggregator, Credit Card Summary, the Enterprise Data Lake, MSMIS, MCS and Terminaling Throughputs), and matched with skos:exactMatch from party to system; 13 data flows, among them Dealer Master's loop with RightAngle, the flows to PRM, and the flows from SAP S/4HANA to Bridger Insight and from RightAngle to TFR, which EPM-DEC-001-0069 left out as uncertain and input 8 shows; six flows that carry more or other than before; four system-stage links (Dealer Master in the prospect and settle stages, Salesforce in the record stage, Polaris in the settle stage); and Bridger Insight's interface name. Inputs 8 to 11 join the inputs a statement can cite.
  5. The releases. party 2.0.0 is major: the corrections and the deprecation change what existing statements mean. system 1.5.0 is minor: it adds systems and notes and changes no definition. integration 2.4.0 is minor: it puts the inputs of 2026-10-27 in tier 1, as 2.3.0 did for the earlier inputs, and requires the current releases.

Alternatives considered#

  • Deprecate Dealer Master's system concept and mint a new one. Not chosen: it is the same system, and only its description was wrong. The skill asks for a major release when a definition's meaning changes; a deprecation is for a term replaced by another.
  • Keep both descriptions of where jobbers start. Not chosen: the decision owner ruled on it (decision 1).
  • Make Channel of Trade a separate attribute. Not chosen: Q8.

Amendment, 2026-10-27 (later): the brands#

Claude asked the decision owner about the three known gaps. From business_architecture/ontology/step4/review-evidence/decision-owner-answers/answers-2026-10-27-customer-inputs.md: "yes those can be brand values. Others I cannot answer."

  • The brands gap is closed. Marathon, ARCO and Tesoro are individuals of party:Brand. They are instances of a real company, which the module policy puts in ref-mpc only (MODULES.md, "Three kinds of content"), so they go in ref-mpc/reference-brands.ttl on ref-mpc's IRIs, not in party. Each cites the customer inputs of 2026-10-27 and MPC's FY2025 Form 10-K, as business_architecture/reference/mpc/customer-classification-evidence.md and mpc-fy2025-10k-fact-sheet.md record its wording: brand jobbers "operate primarily Marathon® branded outlets"; direct dealers "operate locations mainly under the ARCO® brand"; and the brand jobbers' outlets also carry "Shell, Mobil, Tesoro, others". Who owns or licenses each brand is not stated, because the repository holds no source for it.
  • ref-mpc 1.3.0 carries them. It is a minor, because it only adds; Claude approves it on the decision owner's answer, delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028. It requires organization 2.1.0 and party 2.0.0, the current releases.

EPM-DEC-001-0109: Databricks and the ontology: KPI and OPM identifiers, metric views by fact grain, Genie agents under a supervisor, the agents read epm_triples, lineage in its own places, a technical graph in Neo4j, and three years of demo data#

From EPM-DEC-001-0109-databricks-ontology-integration.md

FieldValue
StatusDecided (the decision owner, 2026-10-28, for the answers to the grill's questions and the data extension; the build choices delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028)
Date2026-10-28
QuestionsHow Databricks agents use the ontology (Part 2 of the store-tests handoff): which identifiers measures carry, how metric views and Genie agents are organized, which store the agents read, how they handle lineage, what a technical graph in Neo4j adds, and how much synthetic data the demo needs
SourceClaude Code session of 2026-10-28; answers-2026-10-28-databricks-grill.md

Context#

The ontology is done (EPM-DEC-001-0107), and the pricing demo rebuilds the pricing assets in Databricks Free Edition with synthetic data (EPM-DEC-001-0102). Since 2026-10-28 its source databases run on an exe.dev VM (EPM-DEC-001-0102, amendment of 2026-10-28). Part 2 of the work connects the two: Databricks agents that answer from the ontology. Claude put the design to the decision owner as a grill, in four rounds of questions with a recommendation each.

What Claude found first, from the repository:

  • Only one measure had an identifier: Crack Spreads Pricing carries KPI-0001, the crack-spread Genie agent's identifier (EPM-DEC-001-0097, item 3). The kpi module holds 41 measures in eight kinds, and classes Crack Spreads as a market indicator.
  • The demo has two Genie agents: Crack Spread Price Analytics, bound to the crack-spread metric view, and Executive Summary KPIs Analytics, bound to the Executive Summary and benchmark-prices metric views. A refinery operations metric view already holds utilization, planned utilization, yields, R&M margin per barrel, margin capture and opex per barrel over 1,846 refinery-days. 2026-10-28, later: 9,724 refinery-days since the three-year extension (demos/downstream-pricing-databricks-free/DATA.md). 2026-10-29: four Genie agents since PR E4 (decision 3 below).
  • The projection driver writes the whole ontology to Databricks as a Delta table of triples, epm_triples.

What Claude found from primary sources, on 2026-10-28:

  • Databricks has a Supervisor Agent in Agent Bricks, generally available, which routes a question to Genie agents and other subagents, and the SDK creates it (w.supervisor_agents) (docs.databricks.com, "Use Supervisor Agent to create a coordinated multi-agent system"; the Databricks blog, "Agent Bricks Supervisor Agent is Now GA"). 2026-10-29: the same page, updated 2026-10-14, now marks Supervisor Agent legacy and "not recommended for new systems", and its SDK management Beta; this account can no longer create one (decision 3's note).
  • Databricks Free Edition's limits page (updated 2026-10-18) lists no Supervisor Agent limit, and says "outbound internet access is restricted to a limited set of trusted domains" (docs.databricks.com, "Databricks Free Edition limitations").
  • Composite databases are "Enterprise Edition" and "Not available on Aura" (Neo4j Operations Manual, "Composite databases: Concepts").

Decision#

The decision owner's decisions:

  1. How the work runs: questions now, then one goal run alone: "Q&A now, then one goal (Recommended)". The brief for that run: "Approve, start (Recommended)".
  2. What the integration is for (Q1): a Genie user gets the number and the ontology's definition of the measure, and a data steward sees each metric view tagged with the measures it implements: "Q1. correct".
  3. The source of truth and what the agents read (Q2): "The pattern is the sourse of truth is the ontology serialized in turtle. This is then projected to consuming application. In this case Neo4J, AuraDB, and databricsk and those must be kept in sync with the source, which we plan to save in git. So since the ontology will be in a delta table, it may be best for the genie agent to read the ontology from that table."
  4. Measure identifiers (Q3, Q5, Q6): "Give all measure classify as KPIs an ID like we did for crakc spread but make it incremental. The other measures tagged as Operational metrcis/KPI shoud also have ID that start with "OPM-0001" and make it incremental as KPI." OPM means operational metric: "By the way OPM = Operationam Metrics. This is to distinguisg Operation KPI from the tru KPI i the KPI Store." The decision owner accepted round 2's recommendations on which kinds take which prefix and on keeping four digits: "Yes to all with some coments below".
  5. Metric views and Genie agents (Q7, Q8, Q9, Q11, Q12): metric views are grouped logically, a new metric view holds the pricing measures, and Genie agents each cover measures that belong together under a supervisor: "I do want you to also create a Metric view for all of the pricing operational metrics/KPI" and "I like the concept of the supervisor agent. Is this a concept in Databricks? Can you check? If it is let us implement even if we have two agents today."
  6. Lineage and a technical graph (Q2, Q10, Q13 to Q15): the agent handles lineage held in two places, Neo4j and Databricks, and a small technical graph in Neo4j, outside the ontology, shows what Neo4j adds: "Can we add a small techical graph in Neo4j not captured as part of the ontology to demo how genie or Databrick can leverage the graph in neo4j to add additional context." A live connection from Databricks to AuraDB is tested: "I do want to test the connection to auraDB. This will give me an idea on how this will work with Neo4j enterprise as well." No composite database: "Q16: I do not have Enteprise Neo4j on this machine. So we will have to go with what we have."
  7. Where the code lives (Q4): in the demo: "Q4. agreed".
  8. The data extension: "extedn it and use the current EIA key", and a data description document: "also create data description document for this synthetic dataset, including the grains and how they were geretaed with links to scripts/notebook used to generate them".
  9. A data-flow diagram: "By the way when we complete the grilling we should created an updated data flow in https://app.eraser.io/folder/w07vEqNDXhSrfxz2GdiK as a new file".

What follows (Claude, under EPM-DEC-001-0028):

  1. Identifiers, in kpi 1.3.0. KPI-0001 stays Crack Spreads Pricing, because the pricing assets use it, although the module classes it as a market indicator. The two measures classed as KPIs take KPI-0002 and KPI-0003. Every operational metric, market indicator, plan-variance diagnostic, decision-support indicator and alert takes an OPM identifier, OPM-0001 onward, in the module's order: 30 measures. In round 2 Claude told the decision owner 31, counting Crack Spreads among the market indicators; the rule, not the number, is what was decided, and Crack Spreads keeps KPI-0001. Trackers, reports and non-measures take none. Four digits keep the identifiers sorting as text and give one pattern per prefix. A measure promoted to KPI later takes a new KPI identifier; its OPM identifier is kept and never reused.
  2. Metric views are grouped by fact grain: one view per subject area that shares a fact and its dimensions, never one per measure. A new view, pricing_market_margin_metrics_vw, holds the six market-price measures (Gasoline, Distillate and Crude Market Pricing, Price Comparison, Additional Market Prices and the R&M Margin Indicator). R&M Margin per Barrel and Margin Capture stay in the refinery operations view, at refinery-day grain. Each measure carries its identifier in its name and comment, and each view a tag listing the identifiers it implements. Where the price catalog has no renewables or NGL series, the demo adds synthetic ones, labelled so. 2026-10-29: landed in PR E3, with these build choices, delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028: each measure that implements a kpi measure carries its identifier in its comment, its display name and its synonyms, not in its name, because the Genie agents and the shared pricing assets query the measures by name (EPM-DEC-001-0097); each view's comment and a Unity Catalog tag, epm_measure_identifiers, list its identifiers. The R&M Margin Indicator is not computed, because kpi gives its basis as not published; the pricing view's comment says so. The price catalog already held renewables and NGL series, so none was added. Unit Utilization (OPM-0001) has its own view, refinery_unit_operations_metrics_vw, at unit-day grain.
  3. Genie agents: one per subject area. Crack Spread Price Analytics and Executive Summary KPIs Analytics stay, and a Pricing and Market Margin agent joins them, with a Refinery Operations agent over refinery_operations_metrics_vw, the refinery-day subject area the use cases below need. A Supervisor Agent routes between the four, and each subagent's description names the identifiers it answers for, built from epm_triples. If Free Edition refuses the Supervisor Agent, the fallback is Databricks' documented multi-agent app pattern, and the record of the run says which ran. 2026-10-29: landed in PR E4, with these build choices, delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028. The Refinery Operations agent also binds refinery_unit_operations_metrics_vw, the unit-day view E3 added for Unit Utilization. Free Edition refused the Supervisor Agent: create_supervisor_agent returned "PermissionDenied: Supervisor Agent is now a legacy feature. New instances can no longer be created for this account. Please use Genie Agents instead." Databricks' page marks it legacy too ("Use Supervisor Agent to create a coordinated multi-agent system (legacy)", updated 2026-10-14). The fallback ran: the documented pattern ("Build a multi-agent system on Databricks Apps", updated 2026-10-14), in which an orchestrator model treats each subagent as a tool chosen by its description. The demo runs it from the laptop (python -m pricing_demo.supervisor ask), not deployed as a Databricks App; its model is databricks-meta-llama-3-3-70b-instruct, which the function-calling page lists ("Function calling on Databricks", updated 2026-10-26), because this workspace does not serve the template's databricks-claude-sonnet-4-5; and it calls each Genie agent through the Genie Conversation API ("Use the Genie Agents API", updated 2026-10-23), not the Genie MCP server the template uses. Each subagent's description is built at run time from its definitions view (identifiers and labels from epm_triples) and its metric views' epm_measure_identifiers tags, which mark a defined measure no view computes (OPM-0026). The multi-agent page is itself marked legacy and points new work to the Agent Bricks CLI.
  4. What the agents read: the Turtle in git is the source of truth; epm_triples, AuraDB and the local Neo4j are projections kept in sync with it. Inside Databricks the agents read the ontology from epm_triples, through a definitions view per agent. 2026-10-29: the definitions views landed in PR E3, in their own schema, ontology_definitions (delegated under EPM-DEC-001-0028): measure_definitions_vw, every measure with an identifier, and one view per subject area (crack spread, Executive Summary, pricing and market margin, refinery operations). No agent reads them yet. 2026-10-29, later: since PR E4 each of the four Genie agents binds its subject area's definitions view, and its instructions tell it to read each measure's definition, grain, basis and not-to-be-confused-with labels there before answering.
  5. Lineage: each place answers for its own scope. The ontology holds designed lineage (the data-product module's pricing tables and their lineage by layer); Unity Catalog records observed lineage in its system tables; Neo4j's technical graph holds what runs outside Databricks. A test compares designed and observed lineage after each demo run and names any edge found in only one. If Free Edition lacks the lineage system tables, the test is skipped with that reason.
  6. The technical graph: about 100 nodes describing the demo pipeline, which the business ontology does not model: the six source databases on the exe.dev VM (2026-10-28, later: seven, with eia_weekly), the replication and build steps, every table and view at each layer with its row count and last refresh, and the data-quality checks with their last result. Each node links to the ontology by IRI where one exists (system, data product, KPI or OPM identifier). It lives in the local Neo4j and in AuraDB under a Tech label, in the same database as the ontology, so one Cypher query walks from a gold table to the measure it implements. The projection's counts and hashes count only the ontology's labels.
  7. Databricks and Neo4j: a sync step run from the laptop copies the technical graph into two Delta tables, tech_graph_nodes and tech_graph_edges, which the agents read; Neo4j stays the source. A live connection from Databricks to AuraDB is tried and reported, with the AuraDB login in a Databricks secret scope written from the local login file without printing it. Free Edition's restriction on outbound access may block it; the record of the run quotes the error if so.
  8. The data extension: three years of refinery operations with process units (about 14,000 refinery-days and about 140,000 unit-days), monthly plan targets for the same span, and three years of EIA price history fetched with the current EIA key. rightangle and reference_data_hub stay as they are. A data description document gives every table, its grain, its row count, how it is generated and a link to the script that generates it. 2026-10-28, later: built over 748 trading days (2023-10-21 to 2026-10-20), because each refinery-day joins that day's crack: 9,724 refinery-days and 76,296 unit-days, fewer than the estimate above, and 481 monthly plan targets (13 refineries over the 37 calendar months the window touches). Freight landed in rightangle and the event table in reference_data_hub, so those two databases did change; EIA's weekly data by PADD landed in a seventh database, eia_weekly. demos/downstream-pricing-databricks-free/DATA.md is the data description document.

Alternatives considered#

  • IDs for all 41 measures as KPIs. Claude's round 1 recommendation; not chosen. The decision owner separated KPIs from operational metrics.
  • Renumbering Crack Spreads as an OPM. Not chosen: it would break the pricing assets' KPI-0001.
  • No padding. Not chosen: identifiers would sort wrongly as text (KPI-10 before KPI-2).
  • One metric view or one Genie agent per measure. Not chosen: it repeats joins and dimensions and gives Genie more places to choose between.
  • A composite database for the ontology and the technical graph. Not chosen: composite databases need Neo4j Enterprise Edition, which this machine does not have, and Aura does not offer them.
  • AuraDB as the agents' source inside Databricks. Not chosen: Genie queries only Unity Catalog tables, and Free Edition restricts outbound access.

Consequences#

  • kpi 1.3.0 carries the identifiers (decision 1 above), with its shapes, tests and changelog; it still requires core 2.3.0, and the modules that require kpi 1.2.0 keep that pin.
  • The demo README's "Use cases" section (PR #399) lists the 18 questions the build answers, the data each needs beyond what the demo held then (EIA's weekly data by PADD, synthetic freight, an event table, synthetic plan versions and a decision log; 2026-10-28, later: of these, EIA's weekly data by PADD, the freight and the event table have landed with the three-year history, described in demos/downstream-pricing-databricks-free/DATA.md, and the plan versions and the decision log were still to come; 2026-10-28, later still: the plan versions, the decision log and its evidence items landed in PR E2, with the full decision dossiers in a database the pipeline never replicates, decision_truth) and the minimal part of the decision owner's enterprise-context-engineering repository the demo takes. Each question becomes an acceptance test of this build.
  • Later pull requests build, against the reloaded stores: the store reload; the data extension and the data description document; the pricing metric view and the identifiers on every metric view; the definitions views from epm_triples; the Pricing and Market Margin and Refinery Operations Genie agents and the Supervisor Agent; the lineage test; the technical graph, its Delta copy and the AuraDB connection test; and the Eraser data-flow diagram. Each records what ran. 2026-10-28, later: the data extension and DATA.md landed in PR E1; the rest of this list is still to come. 2026-10-29: the pricing metric view, the identifiers on every metric view and the definitions views landed in PR E3. The same PR records the store reload, which ran on 2026-10-28 with kpi 1.3.0, in the stores runbook, LOCAL-STORES, the projection README and the guide. 2026-10-29, later: the Pricing and Market Margin and Refinery Operations Genie agents landed in PR E4, with the orchestrator that stands in for the Supervisor Agent (decision 3).
